       &&(u\le s),\notag\\
 u_{p,r}(s,u)
 &=\exp\left\{-\delta^{-1}\int_s^u a_p(v)\,\dd v\right\}
       &&(s\le u).                                  \label{eq:phase-action-oriented-scalar-kernels}
\end{align}
Since \(m=(R^3+\varepsilon)/R^2\),
\begin{equation}
 |\partial_R\log m|
 =\frac{|R^3-2\varepsilon|}{R(R^3+\varepsilon)}
 \le\frac2R,\qquad
 |\partial_s\log m|\le C\frac\delta R.             \label{eq:phase-action-log-weight}
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\delta/R}{R/\delta}
 =\frac{\varepsilon}{R^2}
 \le\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2}
 =O(S_\delta^{-2}).                                \label{eq:phase-action-weight-absorption}
\end{equation}
Thus, after decreasing \(\delta_0\),
\begin{equation}
 u_{p,\sigma}(s,u)\frac{m(u)}{m(s)}
 \le C\exp\left\{-\frac c\delta
      \left|\int_s^uR(v)\,\dd v\right|\right\}      \label{eq:phase-action-conjugated-scalar-kernel}
\end{equation}
in the appropriate orientation.  Consequently, with
\(\mathcal O_a(s)=[0,s]\) and \(\mathcal O_r(s)=[s,S_r]\),
\begin{equation}
 \sup_s\frac1{\delta m(s)}
   \int_{\mathcal O_\sigma(s)}
    u_{p,\sigma}(s,u)R(u)m(u)\,\dd u\le C,          \label{eq:phase-action-scalar-convolution}
\end{equation}
and the same conjugated estimate controls the scalar endpoint or terminal
lift.

Set
\begin{equation}
 \kappa_\delta
 =\sup_{\rho_\delta\le R\le r_{\rm out}}Rm_\delta(R)
 \le r_{\rm out}^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}.          \label{eq:phase-action-current-size}
\end{equation}
In \(\mathfrak X_{\sigma,m}\), the two current edges therefore satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal K_{Zp}\|\le C\kappa_\delta,\qquad
 \|\mathcal K_{pZ}\|\le C.                         \label{eq:phase-action-current-loop}
\end{equation}
Indeed, \(C_{Zp}p=O(\varepsilon Rm)\|p\|_m\), whereas
\[
 \frac{\|C_{pZ}Z\|}{\varepsilon Rm}
 \le C\frac{R}{m}\frac{\|Z\|}{\varepsilon}
 \le C\frac{\|Z\|}{\varepsilon}.
\]
Choose \(r_{\rm out}\) first and then \(\delta_0\) so that
\(C\kappa_\delta<1/4\).  The attracting current system, with two forward
diagonal lines, and the repelling current system, with forward \(Z\) and
future \(p\), then have uniformly bounded current-loop resolvents.

For a strict current-delay insertion, the \(Z\)-source contributes at most
\(C\varepsilon\) in the normalized \(Z\)-row, while the scalar source
contributes
\[
 \frac{\|B_k^pZ(s-\theta_k)\|}{\varepsilon R(s)m(s)}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon}{m(s)}
       \|(Z,p)\|_{\mathfrak X_{\sigma,m}}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}
       \|(Z,p)\|_{\mathfrak X_{\sigma,m}}.
\]
Delay translation changes \(R\) and \(m\) only by uniformly comparable
factors.  After the current-loop resolvent, one generation has norm
\begin{equation}
 \beta_{\sigma,m}
 \le C\left\{\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right\}
 \le C\frac\delta{S_\delta}=o(1).                  \label{eq:phase-action-generation-number}
\end{equation}
This closes the attracting delay Neumann series and, on the repelling
branch, the component-generation series of
\cref{lem:repelling-component-generations} in the new weight.

It remains to close the compatible repelling collar.  Put
\[
 m_0=m_\delta(\rho_\delta),\qquad
 M=\|(Z,p)\|_{\mathfrak X_{r,m}}.
\]
Since \(P(0)=0\),
\[
 |p(0)|\le\varepsilon m_0M,\qquad
 \|\gamma\|_{N,-\rho_\delta,\delta}^{\sharp}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon m_0}{\rho_\delta}M.
\]
Let
\(E_\delta=e^{2\kappa_{-\rho_\delta}\theta_m}
\le\delta^{-2\vartheta}\).
Old-history reads occur only in the first delay window, where
\(R\asymp\rho_\delta\) and \(m\asymp m_0\).  Their normalized transverse
and scalar source responses are, respectively,
\[
 CE_\delta\frac{\varepsilon m_0}{\rho_\delta}M,
 \qquad
 CE_\delta\frac{\varepsilon m_0/\rho_\delta}{m_0}M.
\]
Because \(m_0=\rho_\delta+\varepsilon/\rho_\delta^2\le C\), both are
bounded by
\begin{equation}
 \eta_{\rm col}M,\qquad
 \eta_{\rm col}=CE_\delta\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\frac{\delta^{1-2\vartheta}}{S_\delta}=o(1). \label{eq:phase-action-collar-feedback-number}
\end{equation}
The already bounded current-loop and strict-delay resolvents preserve this
estimate, so one final Neumann inversion closes
\(\gamma=\mathfrak C_ep(0)\).

The attracting finite rows vanish.  On the repelling branch,
\[
 \frac{|\varepsilon P(S_r)|}{\varepsilon m(r_{\rm out})}
 \le C\frac{\delta}{r_{\rm out}m(r_{\rm out})}A
 \le C\frac{\delta}{r_{\rm out}^2}A.
\]
Consequently the current-loop formulation, followed by the strict-delay
and collar resolvents, gives
\[
 M\le C\left(1+\frac{\delta}{r_{\rm out}^2}\right)A
       +\{\beta_{\sigma,m}
          +\mathbf1_{\{\sigma=r\}}\eta_{\rm col}\}M.
\]
After decreasing \(\delta_0\), the last coefficient is below \(1/2\), so
\(M\le CA\).  Construct the fixed point in the continuous finite-seam
space, rather than merely an \(L^\infty\) quotient, because \(p(0)\) is
evaluated.  The integral equations and the continuous, piecewise
\(W^{1,\infty}\) sources give a continuous, piecewise \(W^{1,\infty}\)
solution; the same contraction proves uniqueness.  This establishes
\cref{eq:structured-phase-state-bound}.

For reconstruction, current-history reads are \(O(\varepsilon A)\).  A
repelling old-history read is supported in the first window and is bounded
by
\[
 CE_\delta\frac{\varepsilon m_0}{\rho_\delta}A
 \le Cm(s)A,
\]
because \(m(s)\asymp m_0\) there and
\(E_\delta\varepsilon/\rho_\delta=o(1)\).  Hence
\[
 |\mathscr D_{c,N}^{\rm lin}[\gamma,Z](s)|\le Cm(s)A.
\]
Together with \(|p|/\varepsilon\le mA\),
\(\|\ell_N\|\le CR\), and \(\|Z\|/\varepsilon\le A\), the algebraic row
gives \(|\widehat Q|\le CmA\), proving
\cref{eq:structured-phase-speed-bound}.  Finally,
\[
 \int_{\rho_\delta}^{r_{\rm out}}m_\delta(r)\,\dd r
 +\sup_{\rho_\delta\le r\le r_{\rm out}}r m_\delta(r)
 \le C\left(r_{\rm out}^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right),
\]
which proves \cref{eq:structured-phase-action-bound}.  The shift identity
gives the final operator statement, and every constant is dimension
uniform by the common layer-sum bounds.  The scope statement follows from
\cref{eq:normalized-phase-response-obstruction}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{lem:raw-phase-boundary-column-assembly}]
The derivative of an autonomous scalar flow with respect to its initial
value gives
\begin{equation}
 \partial_e\Phi_{q_0}^{\delta\theta}(e)
 =\frac{q_0(r_e^0(\theta))}{q_0(e)}.              \label{eq:raw-phase-flow-initial-derivative}
\end{equation}
Since \(D_rV_{0,N}=e_{0,N}\), it follows that
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal L_e\phi_e^0(\theta)
 =q_0(r_e^0(\theta))e_{0,N}(r_e^0(\theta)).       \label{eq:formal-collar-phase-tangent}
\end{equation}
An entry displacement
\(\xi(0)=q_0(e)E_{0,\sigma}a\) therefore differentiates the formal collar
to the first term on the left of
\cref{eq:raw-phase-collar-exact-assembly}.  Differentiating the
raw-compatible family instead gives
\(E_{0,\sigma}a\,\mathcal L_e\phi_e^{\rm rc}\).
Their difference is exactly
\(E_{0,\sigma}a\,\gamma_e^{\rm ph}\), by
\cref{eq:moving-core-phase-collar}.  This proves the assembly identity and
also shows why its multiplier is \(a(e)\), rather than \(q_0(e)a(e)\) or
\(\delta a(e)\).

For the resource normal coordinate, the corresponding additive collar
trace is
\[
 E_{0,\sigma}a\{
   \mathcal L_ew_{\rm seed,N}
        -\mathcal L_e\omega_e^{\rm rc}\}
 =E_{0,\sigma}a\,p_e^{\rm ph}(0)=0.
\]
The voltage trace is zero as well because
\(\gamma_e^{\rm ph}(0)=0\).  Thus no finite scalar or transverse boundary
row is created.  On the repelling branch the independent \(p(0)\)-column
of the compatible chart is \(\mathfrak C_ep(0)\); linearization gives
\cref{eq:raw-phase-repelling-history-column} with no mixed first-order
term.  Notice that this argument does not set the total \(p(0)\) to zero.

The cap estimate \cref{eq:moving-core-cap-bound}, at order zero, dominates
the frozen sharp norm and gives
\[
 \|c\gamma_\sigma^{\rm ph}\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^{\sharp}
 \le C\varepsilon|c|.
\]
Apply \cref{eq:fixed-phase-C0-inverse}, using its closed-feedback version
on the repelling branch.  After division by \(\varepsilon\), this proves
\cref{eq:raw-phase-collar-response-bound}.  The action estimate
\cref{eq:raw-phase-collar-action-bound} now follows directly from
\cref{eq:normal-phase-H0-coupling}.

The formal bulk response already contains the affine-residual sources in
\cref{eq:shifted-phase-structured-sources}; the collar response corrects
only the old-history boundary column.  Linear superposition therefore
gives both identities in
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-true-action}, without deleting or duplicating the
bulk source.  Combining
\cref{eq:structured-phase-action-bound,%
eq:raw-phase-collar-action-bound} and
\(|E_{0,\sigma}a|\le\|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1}\) proves
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-action-smallness}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse}]
We first justify the normal graph domain used in the statement.  If
\(y_n\to y\) in the hybrid state norm and
\(\mathscr N_\sigma y_n\to f\) in the normal-data norm, then the
variation-of-constants identities for
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} pass to the limit.  The finite seam
traces and the algebraic row pass to the limit as well; on the repelling
branch this uses the boundedness of \(\mathfrak C_e\).
Consequently \(y\in\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\) and
\(\mathscr N_\sigma y=f\).  Thus \(\mathscr N_\sigma\) is closed and its
domain with \cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-domain-graph-norm} is Banach.
The coordinate map
\[
 (x,a,\widehat\tau)\longmapsto
 (x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a,a,\widehat\tau)
\]
is an isometry from \(\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rc}\) onto the
corresponding product Banach space, proving completeness of the full graph
domain.

The inverse estimate
\cref{eq:phase-event-inverse-bound} at \(j=0\) gives a constant
\(M_\phi\), independent of \(N\) and \(\delta\), such that
\(\|\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{-1}\|\le M_\phi\).
By \cref{eq:raw-compatible-action-smallness}, one may first choose
\(r_{\rm out}\) so that \(CM_\phi r_{\rm out}^2<1/4\), and then decrease
\(\delta_0\) so that the remaining two terms contribute less than \(1/4\).
This proves \cref{eq:raw-schur-smallness}.  Hence
\begin{equation}
 (\mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm rc})^{-1}
 =\left\{I-\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{-1}
       (\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc},0,0)\right\}^{-1}
       \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{-1}              \label{eq:raw-schur-neumann-inverse}
\end{equation}
exists and has dimension-uniform norm.

Let \(f\) and \(g\) be prescribed.  The fixed-phase inverse and
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-C0-inverse} give
\[
 y=G_\sigma f,\qquad
 \|y\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 \le C\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}.
\]
Since \(\mathscr N_\sigma y=f\), this also gives
\begin{equation}
 \|y\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)}
 \le C\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}.     \label{eq:raw-schur-fixed-normal-graph-bound}
\end{equation}
The normal-to-phase estimate gives, with the two scalar rows set to zero,
\begin{equation}
 \|(\mathfrak D_\sigma y,0,0)\|_{
       \mathsf H_\phi^0\times\R^2}
 \le C\{r_{\rm out}^2+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}
       \|y\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}.   \label{eq:raw-schur-normal-action-data}
\end{equation}
Define \((a,\widehat\tau)\) and \(x\) by
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-schur-inverse}.  The first row of
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-coupled-block} follows immediately from
\(\mathscr N_\sigma y=f\).  For the second row, use
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-true-action}:
\begin{align*}
 &\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}(a,\widehat\tau)
  -(\mathfrak D_\sigma x
       +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,0,0)\\
 &\quad
 =\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}(a,\widehat\tau)
       -(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a,0,0)
       -(\mathfrak D_\sigma y,0,0)
 =g.
\end{align*}
Thus the displayed formula is a solution.  Conversely, every solution has
\(y=x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a=G_\sigma f\), after which the phase equation is
exactly
\[
 \mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm rc}(a,\widehat\tau)
   =(\mathfrak D_\sigma y,0,0)+g.
\]
The invertibility already proved gives uniqueness.

Combining \cref{eq:raw-schur-fixed-normal-graph-bound} with the bounds for
\((\mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm rc})^{-1}\), and
\cref{eq:raw-schur-normal-action-data} proves
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-schur-bound}.  On the attracting branch the actual
old history is the prescribed normal history plus
\(E_{0,a}a\,\gamma_a^{\rm ph}\).  On the repelling branch it is
\[
 \gamma_{\rm normal}
 +E_{0,r}a\,\gamma_r^{\rm ph}
 +\mathfrak C_ep_{\rm total}(0).
\]
The shifted terminal row
\(p^\sharp(S_r)=b_r+\varepsilon P(S_r)\) is already included in
\(T_r^{\rm form}a+y\).  Hence no boundary row is lost in the graph
factorization.  The final exclusions follow because every operator used
here is the differential at the fixed reduced base and only order-zero
norms were inverted.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{cor:diagonal-raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse}]
For fixed \(\varkappa>0\), the diagonal scales obey
\begin{align}
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{R_\delta}
 &=\frac{\sqrt\delta}
        {2\alpha\varkappa S_\delta^{1/2}}\longrightarrow0,
 &
 \frac{\delta}{R_\delta}
 &=\frac{\sqrt\delta}
        {\varkappa S_\delta^{3/2}}\longrightarrow0,\notag\\
 \frac{\delta}{R_\delta^2}
 &=\frac1{\varkappa^2S_\delta^3}\longrightarrow0.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-diagonal-raw-scale-ratios}
\end{align}
Thus the two spatial intervals remain nonempty and lie in the fixed
coefficient collar.  The sign bounds and weighted critical-coefficient
bounds used by
\cref{prop:principal-tracker-endpoint-green} are inherited on every
smaller interval.  Its transverse perturbation and block loop are
\(O(R_\delta)\) and \(O(R_\delta^2)\), respectively, while its only
singular inner ratios remain
\(\varepsilon/\rho_\delta\) and
\(\varepsilon/\rho_\delta^2\).  These are the same uniformly small ratios
as in the fixed-outer proof.

The order-zero fixed-phase proof uses no unweighted high outer jet.  In its
first-delay old-history estimate, comparability at the attracting outer
endpoint follows from \(\delta/R_\delta=o(1)\); at the repelling entry it
follows from \(\delta/\rho_\delta=O(S_\delta^{-1})\).  Its current and
old-history feedback numbers remain bounded by
\[
 C\left\{\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}
          +\frac{\varepsilon^2}{\rho_\delta^2}\right\},
 \qquad
 C\delta^{-2\vartheta}
 \left\{\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}
          +\frac{\varepsilon^2}{\rho_\delta^2}\right\},
\]
respectively, and tend to zero.  Hence the constants in
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-C0-inverse} and in the closed compatible feedback
remain uniform.  The compatible collar chart is uniform for every
\(\rho_\delta\le|e|\le R_\delta\), and the explicit phase-event inverse
uses only the common two-sided bound on \(q_0\).

We next re-audit the structured phase column; this step cannot be obtained
by merely substituting a moving endpoint into a fixed-outer quantifier.
The four contraction and terminal numbers in the proof of
\cref{prop:structured-phase-action-green} now satisfy
\begin{align}
 \kappa_\delta
 &\le R_\delta^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}
 =\varkappa^2\delta S_\delta^3
       +\frac{2\alpha\delta}{S_\delta}=o(1),\notag\\
 \beta_{\sigma,m}
 &\le C\left\{\varepsilon
       +\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right\}
 =o(1),\notag\\
 \eta_{\rm col}
 &\le C\frac{\delta^{1-2\vartheta}}{S_\delta}=o(1),
 &\frac{\delta}{R_\delta^2}
 &=\frac1{\varkappa^2S_\delta^3}=o(1).
                                                        \label{eq:proof-diagonal-structured-ledger}
\end{align}
Here the first line closes the current loop, the second the strict-delay
series, the third the compatible repelling collar, and the fourth the
repelling terminal lift.  Delay translation is uniform because its two
worst relative displacements are
\(\delta/R_\delta=o(1)\) at the outer endpoint and
\(\delta/\rho_\delta=O(S_\delta^{-1})\) at the inner endpoint.
All weighted scalar-kernel estimates in that proof depend only on
\(\varepsilon/R^2\le\varepsilon/\rho_\delta^2=O(S_\delta^{-2})\).
Thus the same fixed-point argument, now with the explicit ledger above,
proves uniformly on the diagonal
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm form}\|
 \le C\left\{R_\delta^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-diagonal-structured-action}
\end{equation}

The normal-to-phase estimate is also uniform after
\(r_{\rm out}=R_\delta\), since its proof gives directly
\[
 \|Q\|_{\mathsf H_\phi^0}
 \le C\{R_\delta^2+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}
       \|(\gamma,Z,p)\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}.
\]
Apply it to the raw-compatible collar response, whose cap datum is
\(O(\varepsilon)\) uniformly by the collar chart and whose normal response
is controlled by the diagonal fixed-phase inverse just proved.  This
repeats the proof of
\cref{eq:raw-phase-collar-action-bound} with \(R_\delta\) and yields
\[
 \|Q_{\sigma,\rm col}c\|_{\mathsf H_\phi^0}
 \le C\{R_\delta^2+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}|c|.
\]
Adding this collar action to
\cref{eq:proof-diagonal-structured-action} gives the diagonal version of
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-action-smallness}.  Since
\(R_\delta^2=\varkappa^2\delta S_\delta^3\), it is precisely
\cref{eq:diagonal-raw-schur-smallness}.  Its right-hand side tends to zero,
so the Neumann inverse
\cref{eq:raw-schur-neumann-inverse} is uniform after decreasing
\(\delta\).  The graph-domain closedness argument is unchanged.  This
proves all asserted order-zero conclusions and no moving-duration or
higher-jet statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{lem:causal-entry-prefix-action-core}]
The formula in \cref{eq:causal-entry-prefix-action-inverse} is forced by
the differential row and the entry value.  The common speed bounds give
\[
 \frac1{\lambda_\delta}
 \sup\frac{|a'|}{m_\delta}
 \le C\frac1{\lambda_\delta}
 \sup\frac{|k|}{m_\delta},
 \qquad
 \frac{\|a\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta}
 \le\frac{|b_0|}{\rho_\delta}
   +\frac{\|\mathcal P_\sigma k\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta}.
\]
This proves the inverse estimate and uniqueness.  In the other direction,
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal P_\sigma(q_0^2a')(r)=a(r)-a(e_{\sigma,0}),
\end{equation*}
so the prefix part of the data norm is at most
\(2\|a\|_\infty/\rho_\delta\); the pointwise part follows again from
the two-sided bounds on \(q_0\).  Completeness follows either from the
explicit inverse or directly from the weighted uniform norms.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:diagonal-causal-entry-prefix-action-inverse}]
We first estimate the returned phase column in the new norm.  On either
receding interval,
\begin{align}
 \int_{I_\sigma}m_\delta(r)\,\dd r
   +\sup_{I_\sigma}|r|m_\delta(r)&\le C M_\delta,
                                                        \label{eq:proof-prefix-action-weight-integrals}\\
 \frac{\lambda_\delta M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 &\le C_\varkappa.                              \label{eq:proof-prefix-action-scale-ratio}
\end{align}
Indeed, the first estimate is the calculation already used in
\cref{eq:structured-phase-action-bound}.  Expanding the second left-hand
side with
\(R_\delta^2=\varkappa^2\delta S_\delta^3\) and
\(\rho_\delta=\delta S_\delta/(2\alpha)\) gives a bounded multiple of
\begin{equation*}
 \varkappa^2+S_\delta^{-4}
 +\varkappa^3\sqrt\delta S_\delta^{7/2}
 +\varkappa\sqrt\delta S_\delta^{-1/2}.
\end{equation*}

If \(A=\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}\), then
\cref{eq:proof-prefix-action-weight-integrals} gives
\begin{equation}
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}
 \le C\{\rho_\delta+\lambda_\delta M_\delta\}A
 \le C_\varkappa\rho_\delta A.                 \label{eq:proof-prefix-action-A1-embedding}
\end{equation}
By \cref{eq:structured-phase-speed-bound}, the formal returned action
satisfies
\begin{equation}
 |\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm form}a(r)|
 \le C m_\delta(r)
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}.       \label{eq:proof-prefix-action-formal-pointwise}
\end{equation}
Consequently its pointwise part in
\(\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\) is at most
\(C_\varkappa(\rho_\delta/\lambda_\delta+M_\delta)A\),
and its prefix part is at most
\begin{equation*}
 C\frac{M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
    \{\rho_\delta+\lambda_\delta M_\delta\}A
 \le C_\varkappa M_\delta A.
\end{equation*}

It remains to include the raw-compatible entry collar.  Apply
\cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q} to the response in
\cref{lem:raw-phase-boundary-column-assembly}.  Its normalized state size
is \(O(|c|)\), and the frozen-cap factor is at most
\(\delta^{-2\vartheta}\).  Hence, with
\(E_\delta=\varepsilon\delta^{-2\vartheta}\),
\begin{equation}
 |Q_{\sigma,\rm col}(r)c|
 \le C\{|r|+E_\delta\}|c|.                      \label{eq:proof-prefix-action-collar-pointwise}
\end{equation}
For \(c=a(e_{\sigma,0})\), one has \(|c|\le\rho_\delta A\), while
\(m_\delta(r)\ge|r|\ge\rho_\delta\).  Therefore the collar contribution
to the prefix-action norm is at most
\begin{equation*}
 C\left\{
   \frac{\rho_\delta+E_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
   +M_\delta\left(1+\frac{E_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\right)
   \right\}A.
\end{equation*}
Together with the formal estimate, this proves
\cref{eq:prefix-action-phase-column-smallness}.
The four terms in \(\eta_{\mathrm{pa},\delta}\) tend to zero because
\begin{align*}
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
 &\le\delta S_\delta^3/(2\alpha),&
 \frac{E_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
 &\le\delta^{2-2\vartheta}S_\delta^2,\\
 M_\delta&=O_\varkappa(\delta S_\delta^3),&
 M_\delta\frac{E_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 &=O_\varkappa(M_\delta\delta^{1-2\vartheta}/S_\delta).
\end{align*}

By the lemma, the norm of
\(\mathscr B_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}^{-1}\) is uniform.  Thus
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr S_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}^{\rm rc}
 =\mathscr B_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}
       -(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc},0)          \label{eq:proof-causal-entry-phase-Schur}
\end{equation}
is invertible by a Neumann series after decreasing \(\delta\), and its
inverse norm is uniform.

We verify the graph assertion rather than invoking it formally.  For each
fixed \(\delta\), \cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q} and the old normal
inverse show that
\[
 \mathscr Q_\sigma=\mathfrak D_\sigma G_\sigma:
 \widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}\longrightarrow C(I_\sigma)
\]
is continuous.  The embedding
\(\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\hookrightarrow C(I_\sigma)\) is also
continuous for fixed \(\delta\).  If \(f_n\) is Cauchy in the graph norm,
then \(f_n\to f\) in \(\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}\) and
\(\mathscr Q_\sigma f_n\to Q\) in
\(\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\).  Both convergences hold in \(C(I_\sigma)\),
so \(Q=\mathscr Q_\sigma f\).  Thus
\(\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\) is Banach.

The old fixed-normal uniqueness gives both
\[
 \mathscr N_\sigma G_\sigma=I,\qquad
 G_\sigma\mathscr N_\sigma=I
       \quad\hbox{on }\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma).
\]
Consequently \(G_\sigma\) restricts to an isomorphism from
\(\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}\) onto
\(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pa}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)\), the latter is Banach, and
\begin{align*}
 \|G_\sigma f\|_{\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pa}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)}
 &\le C\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}},\\
 \|\mathscr N_\sigma y\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}
 &\le C\|y\|_{\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pa}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)}.
\end{align*}
The second identity also uses
\(\mathscr Q_\sigma\mathscr N_\sigma y=\mathfrak D_\sigma y\).
Together with the forward bound in
\cref{lem:causal-entry-prefix-action-core} and
\cref{eq:prefix-action-phase-column-smallness}, these inequalities show
that \(\mathscr L_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}\) is bounded in both graph
variables.
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathscr L_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}(y,a)\|
 \le C_\varkappa\left\{
   \|y\|_{\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pa}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)}
      +\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}\right\},       \label{eq:proof-causal-entry-forward-bound}
\end{equation}
where the range carries the product norm specified in the theorem.

Its inverse is explicitly
\begin{align}
 y&=G_\sigma f,\\
 a&=(\mathscr S_{\sigma,\mathrm{ent}}^{\rm rc})^{-1}
       \bigl(k+\mathfrak D_\sigma y,b_0\bigr).   \label{eq:proof-causal-entry-phase-normal-inverse}
\end{align}
The definitions of the two graph norms and the uniform scalar inverse give
\cref{eq:causal-entry-phase-normal-bound}.  Conversely, every solution
must have the displayed \(y\) and then the displayed \(a\), proving
uniqueness and surjectivity.  Finally,
\(x=T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+y\) is exactly the raw-compatible reconstruction
from \cref{eq:raw-compatible-schur-inverse}.  No terminal row has been
used in this argument, which proves the stated stop line.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:raw-compatible-terminal-phase-column}]
Write
\[
 \mathcal A=\|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)},\qquad
 P=P_\sigma[a].
\]
The formal part of \(T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\), written in the shifted
coordinate \(p^\sharp=p+\varepsilon P\), satisfies
\cref{eq:structured-phase-state-bound}.  The additive collar part satisfies
\cref{eq:raw-phase-collar-response-bound}, with
\(|E_{0,\sigma}a|\le\mathcal A\).  Because
\(s_{\sigma,T}>\theta_m\), its incoming history on the recut problem is a
current-path segment, not the original entry history.  Consequently, if
\begin{equation}
 \gamma_{\sigma,T}[a](\theta)
 =Z_\sigma[a](s_{\sigma,T}+\theta),\qquad
 -\theta_m\le\theta\le0,                         \label{eq:proof-terminal-phase-incoming-history}
\end{equation}
then
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon^{-1}\left\{
   \|\gamma_{\sigma,T}[a]\|_{C^0}
   +\frac{|b_{\sigma,T}^\sharp[a]|}{m_{\sigma,T}}
             \right\}\le C\mathcal A.          \label{eq:proof-terminal-phase-finite-data}
\end{equation}
Here
\[
 b_{a,T}^\sharp[a]
  =p_a^{\rm form,\sharp}(s_{a,T})+p_{a,\rm col}(s_{a,T}),
 \qquad
 b_{r,T}^\sharp[a]=\varepsilon P(S_r).
\]
The last identity uses the exact rows
\(p_r^{\rm form,\sharp}(S_r)=\varepsilon P(S_r)\) and
\(p_{r,\rm col}(S_r)=0\).  Moreover,
\[
 \frac{|P(S_r)|}{m_{r,T}}
 \le C\frac{\delta}{R_\delta^2}\mathcal A
 \le C\mathcal A.
\]

The distributed data of the shifted sum are exactly
\((F_\phi^\sharp,H_\phi^\sharp)\): the collar response is a zero-source
solution, so it neither deletes nor duplicates the affine-residual phase
source.  On the terminal annulus,
\cref{eq:shifted-phase-source-profile} and
\(r_T^2m_T=r_T^3+\varepsilon\ge\varepsilon\) give
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon^{-1}\sup_{[0,L_b]}
 \left\{\|F_\phi^\sharp\|_N
       +\frac{|H_\phi^\sharp|}{r_Tm_T}\right\}
 \le C\mathcal A,                                \label{eq:proof-terminal-phase-source-data}
\end{equation}
where \(r_T=r_{\sigma,T}\) and \(m_T=m_{\sigma,T}\).
There is no entry-extension seam in this terminal window.  Thus the sources
are continuous there.  The bounds in
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-phase-finite-data,%
eq:proof-terminal-phase-source-data} show directly that the data norm in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-data-norm} is at most \(C\mathcal A\).
The unique local solution is the restriction of the global shifted sum, so
\cref{eq:terminal-component-source-bound} proves
\cref{eq:terminal-shifted-phase-column-bound}.

Now put
\(A_{\rm pa}=\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{pa}}}\).
The embedding already proved in
\cref{eq:proof-prefix-action-A1-embedding} and the entry part of
\cref{eq:prefix-action-phase-norm} give
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal A\le C_\varkappa\rho_\delta A_{\rm pa},
 \qquad |E_{0,\sigma}a|\le\rho_\delta A_{\rm pa}.  \label{eq:proof-terminal-phase-pa-smallness}
\end{equation}
Hence the preceding terminal-data calculation actually bounds the shifted
trace by \(C\rho_\delta A_{\rm pa}\).

It remains to return from \(p^\sharp\) to the physical coordinate \(p\).
This is the only place where the causal-entry derivative weight is
essential.  On the enlarged terminal annulus in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-annuli}, delay translation preserves
\(m_\delta\) up to a common factor and
\begin{equation}
 |a'(r)|\le\lambda_\delta m_\delta(r)A_{\rm pa},\qquad
 |\bar r_\sigma(s)-\bar r_\sigma(s-\theta_k)|\le C\delta.  \label{eq:proof-terminal-phase-local-difference-input}
\end{equation}
Apply these inequalities term by term in
\cref{eq:reduced-flow-phase-delay-defect}.  The coefficient and its
fold-time derivative are uniformly bounded on this annulus.  Differentiating
the two phase values uses only
\(a_s=\delta q_0a'\), not \(a''\).  Therefore
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s_{\sigma,T}\le s\le S_\sigma}
   \{|P(s)|+|P_s(s)|\}
 \le C\delta\lambda_\delta m_{\sigma,T}A_{\rm pa}.  \label{eq:proof-terminal-phase-local-difference}
\end{equation}
Since
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\delta\lambda_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 =2\alpha\frac{R_\delta+S_\delta^{-2}}{S_\delta}
 =2\alpha\{\varkappa(\delta S_\delta)^{1/2}
                  +S_\delta^{-3}\}=o(1),        \label{eq:proof-terminal-phase-shift-ratio}
\end{equation}
the definition of the terminal trace norm yields
\begin{equation}
 \|(0,\varepsilon P)\|_{\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1([0,L_b])}
 \le C\delta\lambda_\delta A_{\rm pa}
 \le C\rho_\delta A_{\rm pa}.                  \label{eq:proof-terminal-phase-physical-shift}
\end{equation}
Subtracting this shift from
\cref{eq:terminal-shifted-phase-column-bound} with
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-phase-pa-smallness} proves
\cref{eq:terminal-physical-phase-column-bound}.

In the shifted equation the algebraically reconstructed local speed is
precisely
\(Q_{\rm n}[T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a]+P
 =\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\).
The two estimates in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-local-action}, with terminal data norm
\(C\rho_\delta A_{\rm pa}\), therefore prove
\cref{eq:terminal-phase-column-local-action}.

Finally isolate the additive collar response.  Its distributed sources
vanish.  The global collar bound gives terminal-interface data of size
at most \(C|E_{0,\sigma}a|\); on the repelling branch its terminal scalar
row is exactly zero.  Apply
\cref{eq:terminal-component-rough-flush} and then
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-phase-pa-smallness}.  This proves
\cref{eq:terminal-phase-collar-flush}.  The physical-coordinate argument
above also explains the scope statement: the older
\(\mathsf A_\phi^1\)-bound gives only
\(|P|/m_{a,T}=O(S_\delta)\) on the attracting terminal window, exactly as
in the witness for
\cref{eq:normalized-phase-response-obstruction}.  Thus no stronger
full-action-space assertion has been used.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:diagonal-bulk-terminal-prefix-trace}]
Put
\[
 D=\|f\|_{\mathsf B_{\sigma,\delta}^{\lambda,\mathrm{tf}}},
 \qquad D_0=\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,0}}
             =\lambda_\delta D.
\]
The diagonal fixed-phase inverse and its closed repelling-feedback version
give
\begin{equation}
 \|y_f\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 \le CD_0=C\lambda_\delta D,                    \label{eq:proof-bulk-global-normal}
\end{equation}
which proves \cref{eq:bulk-global-normal-bound}.

We prove the prefix estimate directly from these old-data bounds, before
using any prefix-action graph space.  Put
\(E_\delta=\varepsilon\delta^{-2\vartheta}\).  The pointwise algebraic-row
estimate in \cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q}, followed by
\cref{eq:proof-bulk-global-normal}, yields
\begin{equation}
 |Q_f(r)|\le C\lambda_\delta
       \{|r|+E_\delta\}D.                       \label{eq:proof-bulk-pointwise-speed}
\end{equation}
Since \(m_\delta(r)\ge|r|\) and
\(m_\delta(r)\ge\rho_\delta\) on either reduced interval,
\begin{equation}
 \frac1{\lambda_\delta}
  \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|Q_f|}{m_\delta}
 \le C\left(1+\frac{E_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\right)D
 \le CD.                                        \label{eq:proof-bulk-weighted-speed}
\end{equation}
For the primitive, the uniform two-sided speed bounds and
\cref{eq:proof-bulk-pointwise-speed} give
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\|\Pi_{\sigma,f}\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\frac{\lambda_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
       \{R_\delta^2+E_\delta R_\delta\}D.       \label{eq:proof-bulk-prefix-preledger}
\end{equation}
Now
\begin{equation}
 \frac{E_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 =O\left(\frac{\delta^{1-2\vartheta}}{S_\delta}\right)=o(1),
 \qquad
 \frac{E_\delta R_\delta}{\varepsilon/\rho_\delta}
 =\frac{\varkappa}{2\alpha}
    \delta^{3/2-2\vartheta}S_\delta^{5/2}=o(1),
 \qquad E_\delta R_\delta\le\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}
                                                        \label{eq:proof-bulk-cap-scale}
\end{equation}
for all sufficiently small \(\delta\).  Hence the braces in
\cref{eq:proof-bulk-prefix-preledger} are bounded by \(CM_\delta\), and
direct expansion gives
\begin{align}
 \frac{\lambda_\delta M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 &=2\alpha\varkappa^2
   +2\alpha\varkappa^3\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta^{7/2}
   +4\alpha^2\varkappa\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta^{-1/2}
   +4\alpha^2S_\delta^{-4}\notag\\
 &\le C_\varkappa.                              \label{eq:proof-bulk-prefix-ratio}
\end{align}
This proves \cref{eq:bulk-direct-prefix-bound} without assuming graph
membership.  The definition in
\cref{eq:prefix-action-normal-data-norm}, together with
\(D_0=\lambda_\delta D\) and \(\lambda_\delta=o(1)\), now proves
\cref{eq:bulk-direct-graph-embedding} as a conclusion.

We next take the terminal interface.  By the window geometry, after one
further decrease of \(\delta\), the segment
\([s_{\sigma,T}-\theta_m,s_{\sigma,T}]\) is a generated current segment.
Thus \cref{eq:proof-bulk-global-normal} gives
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon^{-1}\|\Gamma_{\sigma,T}f\|_{C^0}
 \le C\lambda_\delta D.                         \label{eq:proof-bulk-interface-history}
\end{equation}
On the attracting branch,
\(|\bar r_a(s_{a,T})|\le C\rho_\delta\), so the same global estimate gives
\begin{equation}
 \frac{|b_{a,T}(f)|}{\varepsilon m_{a,T}}
 \le C\frac{\rho_\delta}{m_{a,T}}
          \lambda_\delta D
 \le C\lambda_\delta D.                        \label{eq:proof-bulk-attracting-interface-row}
\end{equation}
On the repelling branch the local scalar row is the prescribed terminal
row \(b_r=0\).  This proves
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-interface-bound}.  By
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-free-data}, the distributed local sources also
vanish, so the data norm in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-data-norm} is at most
\(C\lambda_\delta D\).

The restriction of \(y_f\) to the terminal window solves that local mixed
problem.  Local uniqueness and
\cref{eq:terminal-component-source-bound} prove
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-full-trace-bound}.  Since the local distributed
sources vanish and the repelling terminal row is zero,
\cref{eq:terminal-component-rough-flush} gives
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-final-flush}.  Finally,
\cref{eq:terminal-component-local-action}, with the same local data norm,
proves \cref{eq:bulk-terminal-local-action}.

For completeness, expand the nonvanishing coefficient in the prefix
upper-bound ledger:
\begin{align*}
 \frac{\lambda_\delta R_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}
 &=\frac{R_\delta^3}{\rho_\delta}
   +\frac{S_\delta^{-2}R_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}\\
 &=2\alpha\varkappa^3\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta^{7/2}
       +2\alpha\varkappa^2
 =2\alpha\varkappa^2+o(1).
\end{align*}
This proves \cref{eq:bulk-prefix-nonsmall-scale} and records why no
\(\delta\)-small contraction is claimed from this upper-bound ledger.
If \(b_r\ne0\),
then \(p_r(S_r)=b_r\) is visible at the final endpoint with normalized size
\(|b_r|/(\varepsilon m_{r,T})\), so it cannot acquire the vanishing factor
\(\mathcal E_{r,T}\).  The remaining exclusions now follow from the direct
data class and the fixed-phase, fixed-reduced-path operators used above.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:diagonal-boundary-prefix-terminal-trace}]
Put \(D=D_\sigma^{\rm bd}\).  The data in
\cref{eq:boundary-column-direct-norm} have zero distributed sources, and
their old fixed-normal norm is exactly \(D\).  The diagonal specialization
of the fixed-phase inverse, including the closed repelling collar feedback,
therefore gives
\begin{equation}
 \|y_\sigma^{\rm bd}\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 \le CD.                                         \label{eq:proof-boundary-global-state}
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 |p_\sigma(r)|\le C\varepsilon |r|D.             \label{eq:proof-boundary-p-pointwise}
\end{equation}

The pointwise algebraic-row estimate
\cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q} gives
\begin{equation}
 |Q_\sigma^{\rm bd}(r)|
 \le C\{|r|+E_\delta\}D,\qquad
 E_\delta=\varepsilon\delta^{-2\vartheta}.       \label{eq:proof-boundary-Q-preweight}
\end{equation}
The cap definition gives the displayed value of \(E_\delta\).  Also
\(m_\delta(r)=|r|+\varepsilon/|r|^2\) is bounded below by a fixed
multiple of \(\varepsilon^{1/3}=\delta^{2/3}\), whereas
\(E_\delta=\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\) and
\(\vartheta<1/4\).  Hence \(E_\delta\le Cm_\delta(r)\) for all small
\(\delta\), uniformly on both intervals.  This proves the weighted-speed
part of \cref{eq:boundary-column-global-action}.

The sharper primitive estimate uses the fact that the distributed scalar
source vanishes.  The scalar differential row and the algebraic row in
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} then give, on both branches,
\begin{equation}
 Q_\sigma^{\rm bd}(r)
 =\frac{q_0(r)p_\sigma'(r)}
        {w_{0,N}'(r)+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N(r)}.            \label{eq:proof-boundary-Q-from-p}
\end{equation}
This is the same exact recovery identity used separately in
\cref{eq:attracting-Q-from-p,eq:repelling-Q-from-p}.  Define the scalar
action coefficient
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}(r)=\frac1{q_0(r)
        \{w_{0,N}'(r)+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N(r)\}}.         \label{eq:proof-boundary-action-coefficient}
\end{equation}
The two-sign estimates
\cref{eq:principal-green-coefficients,%
eq:phase-column-q0-two-jets,eq:phase-column-residual-jets} give
\begin{equation*}
 |q_0|\asymp1,\qquad |q_0'|\le C,\qquad
 |w_{0,N}'+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N|\asymp |r|,
 \qquad
 |w_{0,N}''+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N'|\le C.
\end{equation*}
Here the bound on \(\mathfrak d_N'\) on both branches follows by applying
the weighted physical-backtrack calculus through order two to
\(\mathcal D_{0,c,N}\), exactly as in the derivation of
\cref{eq:phase-column-D0-two-jets,eq:phase-column-residual-jets}; the factor
\(\varepsilon/|r|\) is absorbed because
\(\varepsilon/\rho_\delta=o(1)\).  Direct differentiation of
\cref{eq:proof-boundary-action-coefficient} therefore gives
\begin{equation}
 |\mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}(r)|\le\frac C{|r|},\qquad
 |\mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}'(r)|\le\frac C{|r|^2}.          \label{eq:proof-boundary-action-coefficient-bounds}
\end{equation}
These bounds hold on either same-sign interval; no branch orientation is
used in them.

For an oriented primitive from \(e_{\sigma,0}\) to \(r\), integration by
parts is legitimate cellwise.  The seam contributions telescope because
\(p_\sigma\) is continuous.  Equations
\cref{eq:proof-boundary-Q-from-p,%
eq:proof-boundary-action-coefficient} give
\begin{align}
 \Pi_\sigma^{\rm bd}(r)
 ={}&\mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}(r)p_\sigma(r)
   -\mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}(e_{\sigma,0})
       p_\sigma(e_{\sigma,0})\notag\\
 &{}-\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r
       \mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}'(u)p_\sigma(u)\,\dd u .      \label{eq:proof-boundary-action-integration-by-parts}
\end{align}
Use
\cref{eq:proof-boundary-p-pointwise,%
eq:proof-boundary-action-coefficient-bounds} and forget the orientation
only after taking absolute values.  Uniformly for every partial prefix,
\begin{equation}
 |\Pi_\sigma^{\rm bd}(r)|
 \le C\varepsilon
       \left\{1+\log\frac{R_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\right\}D.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-boundary-prefix-bound}
\end{equation}
This proves the last line of
\cref{eq:boundary-column-global-action}.  Since
\begin{equation*}
 \log(R_\delta/\rho_\delta)\le CS_\delta^2,\qquad
 \frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}=\frac{2\alpha\delta}{S_\delta},
\end{equation*}
one has \(\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}\le C\delta S_\delta=o(1)\),
which proves \cref{eq:boundary-column-action-smallness}.

For the causal phase lift, the first two identities in
\cref{eq:boundary-column-causal-phase-lift} follow directly from its
definition.  The primitive estimate gives its normalized \(C^0\) bound.
Furthermore,
\begin{align*}
 \int_{I_\sigma}|(a_\sigma^{\rm bd})'(r)|\,\dd r
 +\sup_{I_\sigma}|r(a_\sigma^{\rm bd})'(r)|
 &\le C\int_{\rho_\delta}^{R_\delta}m_\delta(r)\,\dd r\,D
    +C\sup_{\rho_\delta\le r\le R_\delta}r m_\delta(r)D\\
 &\le C\left\{R_\delta^2
          +\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right\}D.
\end{align*}
The same quantity dominates the unnormalized \(C^0\) bound for small
\(\delta\).  The definition in \cref{eq:phase-event-spaces} therefore
proves \cref{eq:boundary-column-phase-lift-bound}.

It remains to pass to the terminal recut.  The global state estimate gives
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon^{-1}\|\Gamma_{\sigma,T}^{\rm bd}\|_{C^0}
 \le CD.                                         \label{eq:proof-boundary-terminal-history}
\end{equation}
On the attracting branch,
\(|\bar r_a(s_{a,T})|\le C\rho_\delta\) and
\(m_{a,T}\asymp S_\delta^{-2}\), so
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{|p_a(s_{a,T})|}{\varepsilon m_{a,T}}
 \le C\frac{\rho_\delta}{m_{a,T}}D\le CD.
\end{equation*}
On the repelling branch the terminal scalar datum is \(b_r\), and
\(m_{r,T}\asymp R_\delta\), so its normalized local size is also at most
\(CD\).  The local distributed sources vanish.  Thus the restriction of
the global solution has local data norm at most \(CD\) in
\cref{eq:terminal-component-data-norm}.  Local uniqueness and
\cref{eq:terminal-component-source-bound,%
eq:terminal-component-local-action} prove
\cref{eq:boundary-column-terminal-trace}.

For the attracting solution, the first line of
\cref{eq:terminal-component-rough-flush} applies to all the displayed
boundary data.  For the repelling solution, apply its second line to the
entry-history column in \cref{eq:boundary-column-repelling-split}, whose
terminal scalar row is zero.  This proves
\cref{eq:boundary-column-final-flush}.  For the other repelling column,
the exact endpoint row is \(p_r^{\rm ter}(S_r)=b_r\).  Since the endpoint
belongs to the final history window, the zeroth term of the terminal trace
norm gives
\begin{equation*}
 \|(Z_{r,T}^{\rm ter},p_{r,T}^{\rm ter})\|_
       {\mathsf T_{r,T}^1([L_b-\theta_m,L_b])}
 \ge\frac{|b_r|}{\varepsilon m_{r,T}}.
\end{equation*}
Finally \(m_{r,T}=R_\delta+\varepsilon/R_\delta^2\asymp R_\delta\),
which proves \cref{eq:boundary-terminal-column-nonflush} and the stated
scope boundary.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-source-extension}]
We use the component Green formulas directly on \(L^\infty\) data.  This is
important: continuous functions are not dense in \(L^\infty\), so no
density argument is available.  The current-loop kernels are integrable
and hence bounded on \(L^\infty\).  The strict-delay component series from
the proof of \cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green} remains
absolutely convergent because
\begin{equation}
 \beta_{a,T}\le C\frac{\varepsilon}{m_{a,T}}=o(1),
 \qquad
 \beta_{r,T}\le C\frac{\varepsilon}{m_{r,T}}=o(1).          \label{eq:proof-terminal-source-generation}
\end{equation}
It defines a continuous, piecewise \(W^{1,\infty}\) solution, agrees with
the original Green formula whenever the source is continuous, and is
the unique solution of
\cref{eq:terminal-source-ae-normal-equation} with the displayed zero
boundary data by the same component contraction.  This proves the asserted
solution-operator statement.

On the attracting branch, zero initial data and terminal support imply
\begin{equation*}
 (Z_T,p_T)=0\qquad(0\le s\le s_{a,T}).
\end{equation*}
Thus the recut has zero incoming history and zero initial scalar datum.
Apply the \(L^\infty\) version of the local component estimate with the
data norm in \cref{eq:terminal-source-direct-norm}.  It gives
\cref{eq:terminal-source-state-bound,eq:terminal-source-speed-bound,%
eq:terminal-source-interface-bound,eq:terminal-source-trace-bound} on this
branch.  The local-action estimate in
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green} gives directly
\begin{equation*}
 \rho_\delta^{-1}\sup|\Pi_{a,T}|
 \le Cm_{a,T}\|f_T\|.
\end{equation*}

On the repelling terminal annulus,
\(
|\bar r_r|\asymp R_\delta
\)
and
\(
r_{r,T}m_{r,T}\asymp R_\delta^2
\).
Consequently the terminal source norm embeds uniformly into the old
repelling normal-data norm.  The component-kernel proof of the old global
inverse, again read on \(L^\infty\) data, yields the prefix part of
\cref{eq:terminal-source-state-bound}.  Its pointwise algebraic-row bound
gives
\begin{equation}
 |Q_T(r)|\le C\{|r|+E_\delta\}\|f_T\|,
 \qquad E_\delta=\varepsilon\delta^{-2\vartheta}
       \le Cm_\delta(r),                                  \label{eq:proof-terminal-source-Q}
\end{equation}
which proves \cref{eq:terminal-source-speed-bound}.  Restriction to the
terminal recut and local uniqueness give
\cref{eq:terminal-source-interface-bound,eq:terminal-source-trace-bound}.

The repelling action needs a sharper argument than integration of
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-source-Q}.  The scalar differential row and the
algebraic row give, a.e.,
\begin{equation}
 \{w_{0,N}'(r)+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N(r)\}Q_T(r)
 =q_0(r)p_T'(r)-\varepsilon^{-1}H_T(r).           \label{eq:proof-terminal-source-Q-recovery}
\end{equation}
Use the action coefficient
\(
\mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}
\)
from \cref{eq:proof-boundary-action-coefficient}.  Cellwise integration
by parts, with telescoping seam terms, gives every oriented prefix as
\begin{align}
 \Pi_{r,T}(r)={}&
 \mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}(r)p_T(r)
 -\mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}(e_{r,0})p_T(e_{r,0})\notag\\
 &-\int_{e_{r,0}}^r
       \mathcal A_{{\rm act},N}'(u)p_T(u)\,\dd u\notag\\
 &-\frac1\varepsilon\int_{e_{r,0}}^r
   \frac{H_T(u)}{q_0(u)^2
       \{w_{0,N}'(u)+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N(u)\}}\,\dd u.
                                                               \label{eq:proof-terminal-source-action-ibp}
\end{align}
The global state bound and
\cref{eq:proof-boundary-action-coefficient-bounds} bound the first three
terms by
\begin{equation*}
 C\varepsilon\left(1+\log\frac{R_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\right)
       \|f_T\|
 =C\rho_\delta\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}\|f_T\|.
\end{equation*}
The last integral is supported on a terminal \(r\)-window of width
\(O(\delta L_b)=O(\rho_\delta)\).  There
\begin{equation*}
 |H_T|\le\varepsilon R_\delta m_{r,T}\|f_T\|,
 \qquad
 |w_{0,N}'+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N|\asymp R_\delta,
\end{equation*}
so it is bounded by
\(
C\rho_\delta m_{r,T}\|f_T\|
\).
Therefore
\begin{equation}
 \rho_\delta^{-1}\sup|\Pi_{r,T}|
 \le C\{m_{r,T}+\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}\}\|f_T\|.           \label{eq:proof-terminal-source-safe-action}
\end{equation}
The scale ledger gives
\(
\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}=O(\delta S_\delta),\qquad
\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}=o(m_{a,T}),\qquad
\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}=o(m_{r,T})
\), so this is \cref{eq:terminal-source-action-bound}.

Finally,
\begin{equation*}
 m_{a,T}=\rho_\delta+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2}
 =\frac{4\alpha^2}{S_\delta^2}(1+o(1)),
 \qquad
 m_{r,T}=R_\delta+\frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta^2}
 =R_\delta(1+o(1)).
\end{equation*}
This proves \cref{eq:terminal-source-small-factors} and all scope
statements.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:diagonal-bulk-pw-extension}]
Use the same branchwise component Green formulas as in
\cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer}, with Lebesgue integrals for
the two distributed source rows.  A bounded source on each side of
\(s_{\sigma,T}\) produces an absolutely continuous response on each open
piece.  The integral formulas have matching one-sided values at the
interface, so their sum is continuous and piecewise \(W^{1,\infty}\).
Closing the repelling compatible feedback is the same finite-rank
contraction as for continuous data: it uses only current evaluation of the
Green response and hence is bounded for the essential-supremum source norm.
This constructs \(G_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\infty}\) and proves the a.e. normal
equation.

If two such responses have the same data, their difference has zero
distributed rows, zero prescribed baseline history, and zero finite normal
row.  It is therefore the zero solution by the homogeneous mixed-problem
uniqueness already used in
\cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer}.  This proves uniqueness and
also shows agreement with \(G_\sigma\) on continuous sources.  No closure
of a continuous-source subspace in \(L^\infty\) is involved.

Every estimate in the proof of
\cref{prop:diagonal-bulk-terminal-prefix-trace} is obtained from an
integral kernel bound, a finite-rank feedback estimate, or the exact
scalar recovery identity.  Replacing a supremum of a continuous source by
its essential supremum leaves those estimates unchanged.  In particular,
the global Green estimate gives
\cref{eq:bulk-pw-global-bound}; the weighted-speed estimate followed by
the oriented primitive calculation gives
\cref{eq:bulk-pw-prefix-bound}.  Restricting the same response at
\(s_{\sigma,T}\) supplies the incoming data for
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green} and proves
\cref{eq:bulk-pw-terminal-bound}.

The source vanishes a.e. on the complete terminal buffer.  Hence the
method-of-steps solution there is the homogeneous continuation of its
incoming interface data.  The component-generation proof of
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-final-flush} applies verbatim, including its
one-sided interface traces.  The algebraic speed row and its primitive
then give the local speed/action estimate.  This proves all asserted
extensions.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{lem:source-split-hybrid-phase-core}]
The formula
\(
 a(r)=b_0+(\mathcal P_\sigma k)(r)
\)
is forced by \(q_0^2a'=k\) and the causal entry value.  It gives
\begin{align*}
 \frac{\|a\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta}
 &\le \frac{|b_0|}{\rho_\delta}
   +\frac{\|\mathcal P_\sigma k\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta},\\
 \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|q_0^2a'|}{m_\delta}
 &=\sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|k|}{m_\delta}.
\end{align*}
Conversely,
\(
 \mathcal P_\sigma(q_0^2a')=a-a(e_{\sigma,0})
\), so the two-sided speed bounds give the forward estimate.  These
formulas also prove completeness and the uniform isomorphism assertion.

Put \(A=\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}\).  The weight integral
in \cref{eq:proof-prefix-action-weight-integrals} and the definition of
the hybrid norm imply
\begin{equation}
 \sup|a|+\int_{I_\sigma}|a'|\,\dd r
       +\sup_{I_\sigma}|r a'(r)|
 \le C\{\rho_\delta+M_\delta\}A.               \label{eq:proof-hybrid-A1-embedding}
\end{equation}
This is \cref{eq:source-split-hybrid-A1-embedding}.

By \cref{eq:structured-phase-speed-bound}, the formal part of the returned
action satisfies
\begin{align}
 \sup\frac{|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm form}a|}{m_\delta}
 &\le C(\rho_\delta+M_\delta)A,\notag\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}
  \sup|\mathcal P_\sigma(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm form}a)|
 &\le C\frac{M_\delta(\rho_\delta+M_\delta)}
                 {\rho_\delta}A.                \label{eq:proof-hybrid-formal-return}
\end{align}
For the additive raw-compatible collar, the pointwise estimate
\cref{eq:proof-prefix-action-collar-pointwise} and
\(|a(e_{\sigma,0})|\le\rho_\delta A\) give
\begin{align}
 \sup\frac{|Q_{\sigma,\rm col}a(e_{\sigma,0})|}{m_\delta}
 &\le C(\rho_\delta+E_\delta)A,\notag\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}\sup
  |\mathcal P_\sigma\{Q_{\sigma,\rm col}a(e_{\sigma,0})\}|
 &\le CM_\delta
       \left(1+\frac{E_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\right)A.       \label{eq:proof-hybrid-collar-return}
\end{align}
Here the \(E_\delta\)-integral is bounded by
\(CM_\delta E_\delta/\rho_\delta\), because
\(R_\delta\rho_\delta\le R_\delta^2\le M_\delta\).
Since \(\rho_\delta\le C M_\delta\), the sum of
\cref{eq:proof-hybrid-formal-return,eq:proof-hybrid-collar-return} is
bounded by the right-hand side of
\cref{eq:source-split-hybrid-phase-smallness}.

It remains to verify that the displayed number vanishes.  Direct expansion
of the diagonal scales gives
\begin{align*}
 \frac{M_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}
 &=O_\varkappa\left(
    \delta S_\delta^5+\delta S_\delta
       +\frac{\delta}{S_\delta^3}\right),\\
 \frac{M_\delta E_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 &=O_\varkappa(S_\delta^2\delta^{2-2\vartheta}),
\end{align*}
and \(M_\delta,E_\delta\to0\).  Every term tends to zero, proving
\(\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}=o(1)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:diagonal-source-split-hybrid-core}]
The direct-sum coordinates in
\cref{eq:source-split-normal-range} make
\(\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{\sigma,\mathrm{split}}\) a Banach space before
any inverse is applied.  The old normal uniqueness gives
\begin{equation*}
 \mathscr N_\sigma G_\sigma=I,
 \qquad G_\sigma\mathscr N_\sigma=I
 \quad\hbox{on }\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma),
\end{equation*}
so its restriction is an isomorphism onto
\(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{split}}(\mathscr N_\sigma)\) with the stated graph
norm.
The old graph-domain estimates and the direct bulk/boundary bounds also give
\begin{equation}
 \|G_\sigma f\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)}
 \le C\{\lambda_\delta\|f_{\rm B}\|+\|f_{\rm bd}\|\}
 \le C\|f\|_{\mathrm{split}},                   \label{eq:proof-source-split-old-graph-embedding}
\end{equation}
so this transported domain is continuously embedded in the original normal
graph domain.

Let \(f=f_{\rm B}+f_{\rm bd}\), \(y=G_\sigma f\), and
\(Q_y=\mathfrak D_\sigma y\).  The direct bulk estimate
\cref{eq:bulk-direct-prefix-bound} and the boundary estimate
\cref{eq:boundary-column-global-action} give
\begin{align}
 \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|Q_y|}{m_\delta}
 &\le C\{\lambda_\delta\|f_{\rm B}\|
                  +\|f_{\rm bd}\|\},\notag\\
 \frac1{\rho_\delta}\sup_{I_\sigma}
       |\mathcal P_\sigma Q_y|
 &\le C\{\|f_{\rm B}\|
           +\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}\|f_{\rm bd}\|\}.       \label{eq:proof-source-split-normal-action}
\end{align}
All unlabeled data norms in this proof are the two summand norms in
\cref{eq:source-split-normal-range}.  Thus
\(\mathfrak D_\sigma G_\sigma\) maps the split range uniformly into
\(\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}\).

By the preceding lemma and
\(\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}=o(1)\), the phase Schur operator
\begin{equation*}
 \mathscr S_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}^{\rm rc}
 =\mathscr B_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
       -(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc},0)
\end{equation*}
is a uniform isomorphism by a Neumann series.  Consequently the formulas
in \cref{eq:source-split-square-inverse} define a bounded solution.
Conversely, the first row forces \(y=G_\sigma f\), after which the last
two rows force the displayed \(a\).  This proves injectivity,
surjectivity, and the uniform inverse estimate.  The forward bound follows
from \cref{eq:proof-source-split-normal-action} and
\cref{eq:source-split-hybrid-phase-smallness}.  The identity
\(x=T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+y\) is the raw-compatible reconstruction already
proved in \cref{thm:raw-compatible-phase-normal-inverse}.

For the normal terminal observation, add the estimates
\cref{eq:bulk-terminal-interface-bound,eq:bulk-terminal-full-trace-bound,eq:bulk-terminal-local-action}
to
\cref{eq:boundary-column-terminal-trace}.  Linearity gives exactly
\cref{eq:source-split-terminal-observation}; no terminal value has been
used as range data.

It remains to check the physical terminal trace of the phase column in the
hybrid scale.  The first part of
\cref{prop:raw-compatible-terminal-phase-column}, together with
\cref{eq:proof-hybrid-A1-embedding}, gives the shifted trace bound
\begin{equation*}
 \|(Z_{\sigma,T},p_{\sigma,T}^{\sharp})\|_{\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1}
 \le C(\rho_\delta+M_\delta)A.
\end{equation*}
On the terminal annulus, the same delay-difference calculation as in
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-phase-local-difference} now uses
\(|a'|\le Cm_\delta A\) and yields
\(
 \sup(|P|+|P_s|)\le C\delta m_{\sigma,T}A
\).
Therefore
\begin{equation*}
 \|(0,\varepsilon P)\|_{\mathsf T_{\sigma,T}^1}
 \le C\delta A
 \le C(\rho_\delta+M_\delta)A.
\end{equation*}
Adding the normal trace first proves the shifted half of
\cref{eq:source-split-reconstructed-terminal-observation}; subtracting the
shift proves its physical half.  Finally,
\(|a(e_{\sigma,1})|/\rho_\delta\le A\), so
\cref{eq:source-split-terminal-event-row} is a bounded observation.  The
argument never supplied a right inverse for an arbitrary final history,
which proves the scope statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{cor:terminal-source-split-hybrid-core}]
The three input coordinates are unique before applying an inverse: bulk and
terminal distributed sources have disjoint a.e. supports, while the
boundary summand has zero distributed rows.  Each summand is complete in
its displayed direct norm, so
\cref{eq:terminal-source-three-summand-range} is a Banach space.  The bulk
and terminal Green formulas solve their respective a.e. normal equations,
while the boundary summand uses the old zero-source Green operator.
Uniqueness of the component construction therefore gives
\begin{equation*}
 \mathscr N_\sigma^\infty G_\sigma^\infty=I
\end{equation*}
on the three-summand range.  Thus the transported normal domain is
isomorphic to that range.

For
\(
y=G_\sigma^\infty(f_{\rm B}+f_{\rm bd}+f_T)
\), add
\cref{eq:bulk-pw-prefix-bound,%
eq:boundary-column-global-action,%
eq:terminal-source-speed-bound,%
eq:terminal-source-action-bound}.
This gives exactly
\cref{eq:terminal-source-returned-action-ledger}; in particular
\(
\mathfrak D_\sigma y\in\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}
\)
with a uniform bound.  The phase Schur operator is unchanged from
\cref{thm:diagonal-source-split-hybrid-core}, so its Neumann inverse and
the formulas in \cref{eq:terminal-source-extended-inverse} prove
bijectivity and the uniform forward and inverse estimates.

Add
\cref{eq:bulk-pw-terminal-bound,%
eq:terminal-source-interface-bound,%
eq:terminal-source-trace-bound}
to the boundary-summand estimate in
\cref{eq:boundary-column-terminal-trace}.  The local weighted-speed and
action terms follow from
\cref{eq:terminal-source-speed-bound,%
eq:terminal-source-action-bound}
on the terminal annulus.  This proves
\cref{eq:terminal-source-extended-observation}.

For the last assertion take pure terminal data and zero phase and entry
rows.  The causal entry value of the returned phase is zero, hence its
event value satisfies
\begin{equation}
 a(e_{\sigma,1})
 =\mathcal P_\sigma(\mathfrak D_\sigma y)(e_{\sigma,1})
  +\mathcal P_\sigma(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a)
       (e_{\sigma,1}).                            \label{eq:proof-terminal-source-event-prefix}
\end{equation}
The first term is bounded by
\(
C\rho_\delta m_{\sigma,T}\|f_T\|
\)
and the uniform phase inverse gives
\(
\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}\le C\|f_T\|
\).
The second term is therefore at most
\(
C\rho_\delta\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}\|f_T\|
\).
Direct expansion of the diagonal scales gives
\begin{equation*}
 \eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}=o(m_{a,T}),\qquad
 \eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}=o(m_{r,T}),
\end{equation*}
which proves \cref{eq:terminal-source-event-smallness}.  No nonlinear
range membership or moving endpoint was used.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{cor:conditional-event-aligned-source-split-inverse}]
Only the repelling branch requires an estimate.  Apply the raw inverse to
the scalar boundary datum \((0,K_p,0,0)\), with zero phase and entry data,
and write the response as \((y_p,a_p)\).  Its boundary norm is
\begin{equation*}
 D_p=\frac{|K_p|}{\varepsilon R_\delta}
     =\frac{\kappa_{p,\delta}}{\rho_\delta}.
\end{equation*}
The causal entry value of \(a_p\) is zero.  Hence its terminal value is
the oriented prefix of its phase equation:
\begin{equation}
 a_p(e_{r,1})
 =\mathcal P_r(\mathfrak D_ry_p)(e_{r,1})
   +\mathcal P_r(\mathfrak A_r^{\rm rc}a_p)(e_{r,1}).       \label{eq:proof-event-alpha-prefix}
\end{equation}
The boundary-prefix estimate bounds the first term by
\(C\rho_\delta\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}D_p\).  The uniform hybrid phase
inverse gives \(\|a_p\|_{\mathsf A_{r,\mathrm{hyb}}}\le CD_p\), and
\cref{eq:source-split-hybrid-phase-smallness} bounds the second term by
\(C\rho_\delta\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}D_p\).  Thus
\begin{equation*}
 |a_p(e_{r,1})|
 \le C\kappa_{p,\delta}
       \{\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}
           +\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}\},
\end{equation*}
which is \cref{eq:source-split-event-alpha}.  On the attracting branch it
is immediate because \(\mathcal K_{p,a}=0\).

The rank-one resolvent identity now gives
\cref{eq:source-split-Sherman-Morrison}; its denominator stays uniformly
away from zero because \(\alpha_{p,\sigma}=o(1)\).  Direct substitution
shows that the lower triangular history block in
\cref{eq:terminal-history-augmented-event-core} has inverse
\cref{eq:terminal-history-augmented-inverse}.  Thus its invertibility
depends only on the scalar core.  The history shear affects the inverse
norm but never the rank-one denominator.

For completeness, the selector formula gives
\begin{equation*}
 (K_{Z,L}\beta)(\theta)
 =\frac{q_0(e)}{\delta q_{*,e}}
       Z_*'(S_*-L+\theta)\,\beta.
\end{equation*}
If \(|\beta|/\rho_\delta=1\), differentiation in \(\theta\) and the
definition of \(\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1\) give
\begin{equation*}
 \|K_{Z,L}\beta\|_{\mathcal H_{Z,T}^1}
 \le C\frac{\rho_\delta}{\varepsilon}
   \left\{\frac{\|Z_*'\|_{C^0}}{\delta}
                  +\|Z_*''\|_{C^0}\right\}.
\end{equation*}
This proves \cref{eq:terminal-history-shear-jet-bound}; the two conditions
in \cref{eq:terminal-history-slow-jet-gate} make its right-hand side
uniform.  None of these estimates upgrades a terminal observation to an
arbitrarily prescribable RFDE boundary history, and no generic
\(\mathsf T^1\)-bound supplies the required selected-window derivative
bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:rough-normal-prefix-action-obstruction}]
Let \(\bar r_a\) be the attracting reduced path and take the hybrid normal
direction
\begin{equation}
 \gamma_\delta=0,\qquad Z_\delta=0,\qquad
 p_\delta(s)=\varepsilon\bar r_a(s).              \label{eq:proof-rough-action-test-direction}
\end{equation}
The delay row vanishes, and the algebraic equation in
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} gives
\begin{equation}
 Q_\delta(r)=r.                                   \label{eq:proof-rough-action-test-speed}
\end{equation}
Choose the source package to be the exact residual of this direction:
\begin{align}
 b_{a,\delta}&=\varepsilon R_\delta,
 &F_\delta&=z_{0,N}'p_\delta,\\
 H_\delta&=\delta(p_\delta)_s-w_{0,N}'p_\delta
             -\varepsilon^2\mathfrak d_NQ_\delta,
 &f_\delta&=(0,b_{a,\delta},F_\delta,H_\delta).
                                                        \label{eq:proof-rough-action-test-data}
\end{align}
Then \(\mathscr N_ay_\delta=f_\delta\) exactly.  Since
\((p_\delta)_s=\varepsilon\delta q_0(\bar r_a)\), the coefficient bounds
in \cref{eq:principal-green-coefficients,eq:principal-green-q-bounds,%
eq:fixed-phase-coefficient-bounds}
give
\begin{equation*}
 \|F_\delta(r)\|_N\le C\varepsilon r^2,\qquad
 |H_\delta(r)|\le C\{\varepsilon r^2+\varepsilon^2\}.
\end{equation*}
Because \(\varepsilon/\rho_\delta^2=O(S_\delta^{-2})\), the definition
in \cref{eq:raw-schur-normal-data-norm} yields
\begin{equation}
 \|f_\delta\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{a,0}}
 \le C\left(1+R_\delta^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2}\right)
 \le C.                                          \label{eq:proof-rough-action-old-data-bound}
\end{equation}
Uniqueness of the fixed normal inverse implies
\(G_af_\delta=y_\delta\), so
\(\mathscr Q_af_\delta=Q_\delta\).

At the inner endpoint, the speed bounds give
\begin{align}
 |(\mathcal P_aQ_\delta)(\rho_\delta)|
 &=\left|\int_{R_\delta}^{\rho_\delta}
                 \frac{r}{q_0(r)^2}\,\dd r\right|\\
 &\ge\frac{R_\delta^2-\rho_\delta^2}{2q_+^2}
 \ge cR_\delta^2                              \label{eq:proof-rough-action-prefix-lower}
\end{align}
for all sufficiently small \(\delta\), because
\(\rho_\delta/R_\delta\to0\).  This proves
\cref{eq:rough-normal-prefix-action-loss} and the exact identity
\(R_\delta^2/\rho_\delta=2\alpha\varkappa^2S_\delta^2\).
Conversely,
\begin{equation*}
 \frac1{\lambda_\delta}\sup\frac{|Q_\delta|}{m_\delta}
 \le\frac1{\lambda_\delta}\le S_\delta^2,\qquad
 \frac{\|\mathcal P_aQ_\delta\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\frac{R_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C_\varkappa S_\delta^2.
\end{equation*}
This gives the asserted matching upper bound for
\(\|\mathscr Q_af_\delta\|_{\mathsf H_{a,\mathrm{pa}}}\).

For the coupled entry solution, the second and third rows of
\cref{eq:rough-normal-prefix-action-test} read
\begin{equation*}
 q_0^2a_\delta'=Q_\delta+
             \mathfrak A_a^{\rm rc}a_\delta,\qquad
 a_\delta(R_\delta)=0.
\end{equation*}
Integrate to \(\rho_\delta\), use
\cref{eq:proof-rough-action-prefix-lower}, and then apply
\cref{eq:prefix-action-phase-column-smallness}.  One obtains
\begin{equation*}
 cR_\delta^2
 \le \rho_\delta\|a_\delta\|_{\mathsf A_{a,\mathrm{pa}}}
    +C_\varkappa\rho_\delta\eta_{\mathrm{pa},\delta}
       \|a_\delta\|_{\mathsf A_{a,\mathrm{pa}}}.
\end{equation*}
Since \(\eta_{\mathrm{pa},\delta}\to0\), this is the lower bound in
\cref{eq:rough-normal-causal-phase-loss}.  On the other hand,
\[
 \|f_\delta\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf Y}_{a,\mathrm{pa}}}
 \le C_\varkappa\{1+S_\delta^2\},
\]
so \cref{eq:causal-entry-phase-normal-bound} gives the matching upper
bound.  The leading witness has
\(\mathscr D_N^{\rm lin}[0,0]=0\), so its normal-block obstruction is
present without a delayed edge.  The delay-dependent returned phase and
collar columns remain in \(\mathfrak A_a^{\rm rc}a_\delta\), but
\cref{eq:prefix-action-phase-column-smallness} makes them an \(o(1)\)
relative correction and therefore unable to cancel the leading action.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart}]
For the scalar autonomous flow,
\begin{equation}
 \partial_t\Phi_{q_0}^t(r)=q_0(\Phi_{q_0}^t(r)),\qquad
 \partial_r\Phi_{q_0}^t(r)
 =\frac{q_0(\Phi_{q_0}^t(r))}{q_0(r)}.             \label{eq:anchored-phase-flow-derivatives}
\end{equation}
Choose \(c_\phi\) so that \(q_+c_\phi<\chi_0\) and
\(q_+c_\phi<1/2\).  For
\(\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}<c_\phi\), the flow displacement up to time
\(a(r)\) is at most \(q_+|a(r)|<\chi_0|r|\).  A continuation argument
therefore keeps the entire flow segment in
\(\mathcal C_{\sigma,\chi_0}\), while
\[
 \overline{\mathcal C_{\sigma,\chi_0}}
 \subset\mathcal C_{\sigma,2\chi_0}
 \subset\mathcal C_{\sigma,4\chi_0}
 \subset J_\sigma.
\]
Thus its image is compactly contained in \(J_\sigma\).
The standard parameter-dependent scalar-flow theorem and the Banach-space
Nemytskii chain rule now show that
\(a\mapsto\Theta_a\) is \(C^1\) into \(C^1(I_\sigma)\).
The chain rule gives
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-chart-a-derivative,%
eq:nonlinear-phase-chart-derivative}; subtraction gives
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-row-identity}.  Differentiating the nonlinear
action with respect to \(a\) proves
\cref{eq:anchored-nonlinear-phase-action-derivative,%
eq:anchored-nonlinear-phase-linearization} and the asserted \(C^1\)
action map.  If
\(\partial_s\bar r=\delta q_0(\bar r)\) and
\(R(s)=\Theta_a(\bar r(s))\), then
\[
 \partial_sR
 =\delta q_0(\Theta_a(\bar r))
   +\delta\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a)(\bar r).
\]
This proves the exact collective-equation equivalence.

The anchored action equation is the scalar initial-value problem
\begin{equation}
 a'(r)=\frac{\widehat Q(r)}
 {q_0(r)q_0(\Phi_{q_0}^{a(r)}(r))},\qquad
 a(e_{\sigma,*})=0.                               \label{eq:anchored-phase-explicit-IVP}
\end{equation}
On a fixed relative collar its right-hand side is continuous in \(r\),
locally Lipschitz in \(a\), and has denominator bounded away from zero.
It therefore has a unique local solution.

Put \(x=|r|\).  As long as the solution remains in that collar, integration
from the inner anchor gives on either branch
\begin{align}
 |a(r)|
 &\le CD\int_{\rho_\delta}^{x}
       \left(u+\frac{\varepsilon}{u^2}\right)\dd u\notag\\
 &=CD\left\{\frac{x^2-\rho_\delta^2}{2}
       +\varepsilon\left(\frac1{\rho_\delta}-\frac1x\right)\right\}.
                                                        \label{eq:anchored-phase-primitive-bound}
\end{align}
The same formula applies on the negative branch because absolute value
reverses its integration orientation.  It follows that
\[
 \sup|a|+\int_{I_\sigma}|a'|\,\dd r
       +\sup|r|\,|a'|
 \le CD\left(r_{\rm out}^2+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right),
\]
which proves \cref{eq:anchored-phase-A1-bound}.  Moreover,
\begin{align*}
 \frac{|a(r)|}{|r|}
 &\le CD\left(|r|+\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2}\right),\\
 |q_0(r)a'(r)|
 &=\frac{|\widehat Q(r)|}{|q_0(\Theta_a(r))|}
 \le CD\left(|r|+\frac{\varepsilon}{|r|^2}\right).
\end{align*}
Taking suprema proves \cref{eq:anchored-phase-relative-bound}.

Since
\[
 \Theta_a(r)-r
 =\int_0^{a(r)}q_0(\Phi_{q_0}^t(r))\,\dd t,
\]
the preceding relative estimate yields
\[
 \frac{|\Theta_a(r)-r|}{|r|}\le CD\Lambda_\delta.
\]
Choose \(D_*\) so that the right-hand side above is smaller than
\(\min\{\chi_0,c_\phi/C\}\).  Then
\(\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}<c_\phi\), the image remains strictly inside
\(\mathcal C_{\sigma,4\chi_0}\), and
\(|\Theta_a(r)|\asymp|r|\).  The weighted derivative
bound on \(q_0\) then gives
\[
 \left|\frac{q_0(\Theta_a(r))}{q_0(r)}-1\right|
 \le C\frac{|\Theta_a(r)-r|}{|r|}
 \le CD\Lambda_\delta.
\]
Substitution in \cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-chart-derivative} proves the
derivative part of \cref{eq:anchored-phase-chart-smallness}.  These
estimates improve the bootstrap collar, so continuation closes the local
solution on all of \(I_\sigma\).  They also prove uniqueness and,
after one final decrease of \(D_*\),
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-orientation}.

Combining the action equation with
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-row-identity} proves
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-speed-sign}.  Finally,
\(\varepsilon=\delta^2\) and
\(\rho_\delta=\delta S_\delta/(2\alpha)\) give
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-small-parameters}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:inner-anchor-endpoint-recut-obstruction}]
By \cref{eq:anchored-phase-small-parameters},

\[
 \Lambda_\delta=r_{\rm out}+4\alpha^2S_\delta^{-2}.
\]

Choose \(r_{\rm out}\) and then \(d_0\) so that
\(D_\delta\Lambda_\delta\le D_*\) for all sufficiently small
\(\delta\).  Since \(|r|\le m_\delta(r)\),
\cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-source} then satisfies
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-speed-class}.  The anchored solution therefore
exists and remains in the common phase collar.  In particular,

\[
 q_-^2
 \le q_0(r)q_0(\Theta_{a_{\sigma,\delta}}(r))
 \le q_+^2.
\]

\Cref{eq:anchored-phase-explicit-IVP} gives
\begin{align}
 a_{a,\delta}(r_{\rm out})
 &=\int_{\rho_\delta}^{r_{\rm out}}
   \frac{D_\delta u}
   {q_0(u)q_0(\Theta_{a_{a,\delta}}(u))}\,\dd u>0,
                                                        \label{eq:proof-endpoint-obstruction-attracting-integral}\\
 a_{r,\delta}(-r_{\rm out})
 &=\int_{-\rho_\delta}^{-r_{\rm out}}
   \frac{D_\delta|u|}
   {q_0(u)q_0(\Theta_{a_{r,\delta}}(u))}\,\dd u<0.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-endpoint-obstruction-repelling-integral}
\end{align}
Bounding the denominators between \(q_-^2\) and \(q_+^2\) proves
\cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-phase-signs,eq:endpoint-obstruction-phase-size}.

For every admissible \(r\),
\begin{equation}
 \Theta_a(r)-r
 =\int_0^{a(r)}q_0(\Phi_{q_0}^t(r))\,\dd t.       \label{eq:proof-endpoint-obstruction-flow-displacement}
\end{equation}
Because \(q_0<0\), the signs just obtained prove
\cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-sidedness}.  By
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-orientation}, each chart is orientation preserving,
and its inner endpoint is fixed.  Hence its whole image lies between that
fixed inner endpoint and the displaced outer endpoint.  Neither original
outer endpoint belongs to the image.  The reconstructed speed remains
negative by \cref{eq:anchored-phase-speed-sign}; it therefore cannot create
the attracting endpoint in future time, while on the repelling branch the
outer endpoint lies beyond the constructed terminal point.

The identities in
\cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-rows} follow directly from the inner anchors
and \cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-row-reanchoring}.  They are precisely the
entry and terminal rows of the scalar bordered bookkeeping; no claim is
made here that constant reanchoring preserves the nonlinear phase action.

For sufficiently small \(\delta\), one has
\(\rho_\delta<r_{\rm out}/2\).  Thus
\cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-phase-size} yields
\begin{equation}
 |\widehat\tau_{\sigma,\delta}|
 \ge c d_0(r_{\rm out}+S_\delta^{-2})r_{\rm out}^2.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-endpoint-obstruction-tau-lower}
\end{equation}
The right-hand side converges to \(cd_0r_{\rm out}^3>0\), whereas

\[
 \rho_\delta=\frac{\delta S_\delta}{2\alpha}\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 \delta S_\delta=2\alpha\rho_\delta.
\]

This proves both limits in
\cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-scale-divergence}.  Finally,

\[
 |\widetilde a_{a,\delta}(\rho_\delta)|
 =|\widehat\tau_{a,\delta}|,
\]

so the value term in the relative norm proves
\cref{eq:endpoint-obstruction-relative-norm}.  The last conclusions are
exactly the logical consequences of these scale and sidedness failures.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:receding-collar-causal-flight-time}]
Fix \(\varkappa_{\rm ep}>0\).  We first record the joint scale ledger.
After decreasing \(\delta_{\rm ep}(\varkappa_{\rm ep})\),
\begin{align}
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{r_{{\rm out},\delta}}
 &=\frac{\delta^{1/2}}
        {2\alpha\varkappa_{\rm ep}S_\delta^{1/2}},
 &
 \frac{\delta}{r_{{\rm out},\delta}^2}
 &=\frac1{\varkappa_{\rm ep}^2S_\delta^3},        \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-geometric-ratios}\\
 r_{{\rm out},\delta}S_\delta^2
 &=\varkappa_{\rm ep}\delta^{1/2}S_\delta^{7/2}
 \longrightarrow0.                              \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-lambda-ratio}
\end{align}
For each fixed positive \(\varkappa_{\rm ep}\), the first two ratios tend
to zero.  This is why the theorem chooses
\(\varkappa_{\rm ep}\) before
\(\delta_{\rm ep}(\varkappa_{\rm ep})\); no common small-\(\delta\)
threshold is asserted as \(\varkappa_{\rm ep}\downarrow0\).
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}\le CS_\delta^{-2},\qquad
 \sup_{\rho_\delta\le |r|\le r_{{\rm out},\delta}}
        m_\delta(r)
 \le r_{{\rm out},\delta}
       +\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2}
 \le C\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}.                   \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-source-maximum}
\end{equation}
The source hypothesis therefore gives
\[
 \|\widehat Q_\sigma\|_\infty
 \le CC_Q(\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep})^2=o(1).
\]
Decrease \(\delta_{\rm ep}\) so that this is at most \(q_-/2\).
This proves the strict sign of \(q_\sigma=q_0+\widehat Q_\sigma\).

The flight-time definitions immediately give
\[
 \beta_\sigma'
 =\frac1{q_0}-\frac1{q_\sigma}
 =\frac{\widehat Q_\sigma}{q_0q_\sigma},
 \qquad
 \beta_\sigma(e_{\sigma,0})=0.
\]
They also give
\[
 \beta_\sigma(e_{\sigma,1})
 =\theta_\sigma^0(e_{\sigma,1})
       -\theta_\sigma(e_{\sigma,1})
 =-\widehat\tau_\sigma.
\]
Solving the first identity algebraically for
\(\widehat Q_\sigma\) proves
\cref{eq:receding-flight-bordered-problem,%
eq:receding-flight-beta-prime}.  Reversing the limits in the last
identity proves \cref{eq:receding-flight-duration}.  Conversely, the bulk
row determines \(\beta_\sigma'\), the causal row fixes its integration
constant, and the terminal row fixes \(\widehat\tau_\sigma\).  This proves
existence and uniqueness of the bordered solution.

The strict speed bounds imply
\begin{equation}
 |\beta_\sigma'(r)|\le CD_\delta m_\delta(r).
                                                        \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-derivative-bound}
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
 \int_{I_\sigma}m_\delta(r)\,\dd r
 \le C\left\{r_{{\rm out},\delta}^2
                   +\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right\}
 =CM_\delta^{\rm ep}.                             \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-action-integral}
\end{equation}
Since \(|r|\ge\rho_\delta\), integration from the causal endpoint and
\cref{eq:receding-flight-duration} yield
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|\beta_\sigma(r)|}{|r|}
       +\frac{|\widehat\tau_\sigma|}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C D_\delta\frac{M_\delta^{\rm ep}}{\rho_\delta}.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-pre-scale}
\end{equation}
Now
\begin{align}
 \frac{\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}M_\delta^{\rm ep}}
      {\rho_\delta}
 &=
 2\alpha\{r_{{\rm out},\delta}+S_\delta^{-2}\}
 \left\{\varkappa_{\rm ep}^2S_\delta^2
                    +\frac{2\alpha}{S_\delta^2}\right\}\notag\\
 &\le C\{\varkappa_{\rm ep}^2+S_\delta^{-4}\}.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-normalized-action}
\end{align}
Indeed, the two omitted cross terms are absorbed by the right-hand side
using
\(\delta^{1/2}S_\delta^{7/2}\to0\), uniformly for
\(0<\varkappa_{\rm ep}\le1\).  Combining
\cref{eq:proof-receding-flight-derivative-bound,%
eq:proof-receding-flight-pre-scale,%
eq:proof-receding-flight-normalized-action}
with \(D_\delta\le C_Q\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}\) proves
\cref{eq:receding-flight-relative-bounds}.  Multiplication by
\(\rho_\delta/\delta=S_\delta/(2\alpha)\) proves
\cref{eq:receding-flight-fold-time-bound}.

We next prove the nonlinear Schur assertion.  The open set on which
\(\|q_0+\widehat Q\|_\infty\) stays uniformly separated from zero is open
in \(C^0(I_\sigma)\).  Reciprocal Nemytskii substitution is real analytic
there.  Integration and endpoint evaluation are bounded linear maps.
Thus
\(\widehat Q\mapsto(\beta,\widehat\tau)\) is real analytic.
Differentiating \cref{eq:receding-flight-duration} under the integral sign
gives \cref{eq:receding-flight-duration-differential}.
Differentiating the bordered map at a solution gives
\begin{equation}
 (b,\zeta)\longmapsto
 \left(
   \frac{q_0^2b'}{(1-q_0\beta')^2},
   b(e_{\sigma,0}),\
   b(e_{\sigma,1})+\zeta\right).                 \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-differential}
\end{equation}
The multiplier in the first row and its reciprocal are uniformly bounded,
because
\[
 1-q_0\beta'=\frac{q_0}{q_0+\widehat Q}.
\]
For data \((h,b_0,b_E)\), the unique inverse is therefore
\begin{align}
 b'(r)&=\frac{(1-q_0(r)\beta'(r))^2}{q_0(r)^2}h(r),
 \qquad b(e_{\sigma,0})=b_0,\notag\\
 \zeta&=b_E-b(e_{\sigma,1}).                     \label{eq:proof-receding-flight-linear-inverse}
\end{align}
If \(H=\|h\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,{\rm ep}}}\), then
\[
 |b'(r)|\le C\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}m_\delta(r)H,
\]
and hence
\[
 \sup_{I_\sigma}\frac{|b(r)|}{|r|}
 \le C\left\{\frac{|b_0|}{\rho_\delta}
       +\frac{\lambda_\delta^{\rm ep}M_\delta^{\rm ep}}
              {\rho_\delta}H\right\}.
\]
The same estimate at \(e_{\sigma,1}\), followed by the last row of
\cref{eq:proof-receding-flight-linear-inverse}, bounds
\(|\zeta|/\rho_\delta\).  Use
\cref{eq:proof-receding-flight-normalized-action} and
\(\varkappa_{\rm ep}\le\varkappa_{\rm ep}^*\le1\) to obtain
\cref{eq:receding-flight-bordered-inverse}.  The displayed formulas also
show that the differential and its inverse are bounded in the stated
norms, proving the isomorphism.

Finally, \(\theta_\sigma\) is strictly increasing along the oriented
interval from \(e_{\sigma,0}\) to \(e_{\sigma,1}\), with range
\([0,\delta S_\sigma]\).  Its inverse gives the unique solution of
\cref{eq:receding-flight-physical-ivp} by
\[
 r_\sigma(s)=\theta_\sigma^{-1}(\delta s).
\]
The nonvanishing continuous speed makes this solution \(C^1\), strictly
decreasing, and unique even though no Lipschitz assumption on
\(\widehat Q_\sigma\) was imposed.  At \(s=S_\sigma\) it equals
\(e_{\sigma,1}\), which proves the final assertion.  Every construction
in this proof was scalar and Eulerian; no diagonal-uniform RFDE inverse or
endpoint history has been inferred.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:reference-pulled-causal-endpoint}]
We first construct one fixed terminal envelope.  The scalar flow estimate
\[
 |\Phi_{q_0}^t(e)-e|\le q_+|t|
\]
and \(q_+c_T<1/4\) show that the terminal tube in
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-terminal-tube} remains in the same-sign relative
