collar.  On the attracting branch its smallest modulus is at least
\(3\rho_\delta/4\).  On the repelling branch the terminal modulus is
\(R_\delta\), and
\(\rho_\delta/R_\delta\to0\), so the same conclusion follows after
decreasing \(\delta\).  Enlarging constants gives the first two assertions
in \cref{eq:reference-pulled-tube-geometry}.  The added interval has length
at most \(2q_+c_T\rho_\delta\), on which
\(m_\delta\le C\{R_\delta+\varepsilon/\rho_\delta^2\}\).
Together with \cref{eq:proof-receding-flight-action-integral}, this proves
the last assertion there.

For fixed \(Q^{\rm ref}=Q_\sigma^{\rm ref}\), the first row in
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-causal-phase} is the scalar initial-value problem
\begin{equation}
 a'(r)=\frac{Q^{\rm ref}(r)}
  {q_0(r)q_0(\Phi_{q_0}^{a(r)}(r))},
 \qquad a(e_{\sigma,0})=0.                       \label{eq:proof-reference-pulled-IVP}
\end{equation}
On the relative phase ball from
\cref{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart}, its right-hand side is
continuous in (r), locally Lipschitz in (a), and bounded by
\(CD_\delta m_\delta(r)\).  Picard continuation on the compact envelope,
followed by integration from the causal endpoint, gives a unique small
solution and
\begin{equation}
 \sup\frac{|a'|}{m_\delta}\le CD_\delta,
 \qquad
 \|a\|_\infty\le CD_\delta M_\delta.             \label{eq:proof-reference-pulled-a-bound}
\end{equation}
The right-hand side stays in the chosen relative phase ball because
\(D_\delta M_\delta/\rho_\delta
 \le CC_Q\{\varkappa_{\rm ep}^2+S_\delta^{-4}\}\).
This also proves that no continuation boundary is reached.

Put
\begin{equation*}
 F(\tau)=a(\Phi_{q_0}^{\tau}(e_{\sigma,1}))+\tau.
\end{equation*}
For \(|\tau|\le c_T\rho_\delta\),
\begin{equation}
 F'(\tau)=1+q_0(\Phi_{q_0}^{\tau}(e_{\sigma,1}))
       a'(\Phi_{q_0}^{\tau}(e_{\sigma,1})).       \label{eq:proof-reference-pulled-terminal-derivative}
\end{equation}
The source bound, tube geometry, and
\(D_\delta\le C_Q\lambda_\delta\) make the second term \(o(1)\),
uniformly after first fixing
\(0<\varkappa_{\rm ep}\le\varkappa_{\rm rp}^*\).
Choose \(\varkappa_{\rm rp}^*\) and then \(\delta\) so that
\(1/2\le F'\le3/2\) and
\(\|a\|_\infty\le c_T\rho_\delta/2\).
The values of (F) at
\(\tau=\pm c_T\rho_\delta\) then have opposite signs.  The intermediate
value theorem and strict monotonicity give the unique root
\(\tau_\sigma\), and
\begin{equation}
 |\tau_\sigma|\le2|a(e_{\sigma,1})|
       \le CD_\delta M_\delta.                    \label{eq:proof-reference-pulled-tau-bound}
\end{equation}
Equations \cref{eq:proof-reference-pulled-a-bound,eq:proof-reference-pulled-tau-bound}
and \cref{eq:proof-receding-flight-normalized-action} prove
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-relative-bound}.  Multiplication by
\(\rho_\delta/\delta=S_\delta/(2\alpha)\) proves
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-fold-time-bound}.

We next prove the exact conjugacy.  The nonlinear phase identity
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-row-identity} and
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-causal-phase} give
\[
 q_0(r)\Theta_a'(r)
 =q_0(\Theta_a(r))+Q^{\rm ref}(r)
 =q^E(\Theta_a(r)),
\]
which is \cref{eq:reference-pulled-speed-conjugacy}.  It also shows that
\(\Theta_a\) is orientation preserving and that \(q^E<0\), after the same
smallness choice.  Differentiating
\(\theta_E(\Theta_a(r))\) now gives
\[
 \frac{\Theta_a'(r)}{q^E(\Theta_a(r))}=\frac1{q_0(r)}.
\]
Both sides vanish at the causal endpoint after integration, proving the
first identity in \cref{eq:reference-pulled-flight-conjugacy}.  The scalar
flow definition gives
\(\theta_0(\Phi_{q_0}^{a(r)}(r))=\theta_0(r)+a(r)\), which proves the
second identity and hence
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-beta-conjugacy}.  Indeed,
\(r_{\sigma,\tau}=\Phi_{q_0}^{\tau_\sigma}(e_{\sigma,1})\) and
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-terminal-row} imply
\[
 \Theta_a(r_{\sigma,\tau})
 =\Phi_{q_0}^{a(r_{\sigma,\tau})+\tau_\sigma}
       (e_{\sigma,1})=e_{\sigma,1}.
\]
The chain rule applied to
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-physical-path} proves its differential equation.
Finally,
\[
 \theta_0(r_{\sigma,\tau})
 =\theta_0(e_{\sigma,1})+\tau_\sigma
 =\delta S_\sigma^0+\tau_\sigma,
\]
which proves \cref{eq:reference-pulled-duration} and the endpoint hit.

It remains to prove the bordered inverse.  At a solution, differentiation
of \cref{eq:reference-pulled-bordered-map} gives
\begin{equation}
 (h,\zeta)\longmapsto
 \left(A_a h'+B_a h,\ h(e_{\sigma,0}),
        h(r_{\sigma,\tau})+c_{\sigma,{\rm ter}}\zeta\right),
                                                        \label{eq:proof-reference-pulled-differential}
\end{equation}
where
\begin{align}
 A_a(r)&=q_0(r)q_0(\Theta_a(r)),\notag\\
 B_a(r)&=q_0(r)q_0'(\Theta_a(r))
             q_0(\Theta_a(r))a'(r),              \label{eq:proof-reference-pulled-linear-coefficients}\\
 c_{\sigma,{\rm ter}}
 &=1+q_0(r_{\sigma,\tau})a'(r_{\sigma,\tau}).
\end{align}
The last coefficient is the derivative in
\cref{eq:proof-reference-pulled-terminal-derivative}, so it lies in
\([1/2,3/2]\).  Evaluating
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-speed-conjugacy} at
\(r_{\sigma,\tau}\) and using
\(\Theta_a(r_{\sigma,\tau})=e_{\sigma,1}\) proves the quotient identity
in \cref{eq:reference-pulled-terminal-coefficient}.

For data \((k,b_0,b_E)\), put
\begin{align}
 \mu_a(r)&=\exp\left\{\int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r
          q_0'(\Theta_a(u))a'(u)\,\dd u\right\},\notag\\
 h(r)&=\mu_a(r)^{-1}\left\{b_0+
   \int_{e_{\sigma,0}}^r
    \mu_a(u)\frac{k(u)}
      {q_0(u)q_0(\Theta_a(u))}\,\dd u\right\},\notag\\
 \zeta&=\frac{b_E-h(r_{\sigma,\tau})}
                 {c_{\sigma,{\rm ter}}}.         \label{eq:proof-reference-pulled-explicit-inverse}
\end{align}
These formulas solve all three rows of
\cref{eq:proof-reference-pulled-differential} and prove uniqueness.
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
 \int_{I_\sigma^\sharp}
  |q_0'(\Theta_a)a'|\,\dd r
 \le CD_\delta\left\{R_\delta+
              \frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2}\right\}=o(1),            \label{eq:proof-reference-pulled-integrating-factor}
\end{equation}
because \(|q_0'(u)|\le C/|u|\), the relative collar makes
\(|\Theta_a(r)|\asymp|r|\), and
\(m_\delta(r)/|r|=1+\varepsilon/|r|^3\).
Thus \(\mu_a\) and \(\mu_a^{-1}\) are uniformly bounded.

If \(K=\|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,{\rm ce}}}\), then
\[
 \int_{I_\sigma^\sharp}|k(r)|\,\dd r
 \le \lambda_\delta M_\delta K,
 \qquad
 \frac{\lambda_\delta M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\le C.
\]
The explicit inverse therefore controls
\(\|h\|_\infty/\rho_\delta\).  From
\(A_ah'+B_ah=k\), the coefficient bounds, and
\(|h(r)|\le\rho_\delta\|h\|_\infty/\rho_\delta\),
\begin{equation*}
 \frac1{\lambda_\delta}
 \sup\frac{|h'|}{m_\delta}
 \le C\left\{K+\frac{\|h\|_\infty}{\rho_\delta}\right\};
\end{equation*}
here \(|q_0'(\Theta_a(r))|\rho_\delta\le C\) on the tube.
The terminal formula for \(\zeta\) gives the remaining row and proves
\cref{eq:reference-pulled-bordered-inverse}.  This completes the theorem,
including its stated scope boundary.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:inner-anchored-relative-phase-schur}]
The relative phase space is Banach, and
\begin{equation}
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1(I_\sigma)}
 \le Cr_{\rm out}\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}.        \label{eq:relative-phase-A1-embedding}
\end{equation}
For data \((h,b)\), the explicit inverse of the unperturbed anchored
operator is
\begin{equation}
 a(r)=b+\int_{e_{\sigma,*}}^r\frac{h(u)}{q_0(u)^2}\,\dd u.   \label{eq:inner-anchored-linear-explicit-inverse}
\end{equation}
On either same-sign branch,
\(|r-e_{\sigma,*}|\le |r|\).  The bounds on \(q_0\) therefore give
\[
 \sup_r\frac{|a(r)|}{|r|}+\sup_r|a'(r)|
 \le C\left\{\|h\|_{\phi,\infty}
                  +\frac{|b|}{\rho_\delta}\right\}.
\]
This proves the first isomorphism with its uniform inverse.

We next prove the stronger action estimate.  Combining
\cref{eq:structured-phase-speed-bound,eq:relative-phase-A1-embedding}
and
\(\sup_{I_\sigma}m_\delta
 \le r_{\rm out}+\varepsilon/\rho_\delta^2\)
gives
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm form}a\|_{\phi,\infty}
 \le Cr_{\rm out}\left\{
       r_{\rm out}+\frac1{S_\delta^2}\right\}
       \|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}.                 \label{eq:relative-formal-action-smallness}
\end{equation}
For the collar response, put \(c=E_{0,\sigma}a\).  The corresponding
branch estimate in \cref{eq:raw-phase-collar-response-bound}, followed by
the pointwise normal-to-phase bound
\cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q}, gives
\[
 \|Q_{\sigma,\rm col}c\|_{\phi,\infty}
 \le C\{r_{\rm out}+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}|c|.
\]
Since \(|c|\le r_{\rm out}\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}\) on either branch,
the last display and
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-true-action,eq:relative-formal-action-smallness}
prove \cref{eq:relative-raw-action-smallness}.  Notice that on the
repelling branch \(e_{r,0}=e_{r,*}\), so its collar multiplier is even the
anchored value.

Let \(M_*\) be a common inverse bound for
\(\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^0\).  Choose \(r_{\rm out}\) and then
\(\delta_0\) so that the right-hand side of
\cref{eq:relative-raw-action-smallness}, multiplied by \(M_*\), is at most
\(1/2\).  The Neumann formula
\begin{equation}
 (\mathscr S_{\sigma,*}^{\rm rc})^{-1}
 =\left\{I-(\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^0)^{-1}
       (\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc},0)\right\}^{-1}
       (\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^0)^{-1}         \label{eq:inner-anchored-schur-neumann}
\end{equation}
proves the asserted isomorphism and uniform bound.

For \(y\in\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\),
\cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q} gives
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty y\|_{\phi,\infty}
 \le C\{r_{\rm out}+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}
       \|y\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)}.      \label{eq:inner-anchored-normal-action-bound}
\end{equation}
Define \(y,a,x\) by \cref{eq:inner-anchored-schur-inverse}.  The first
coupled row is immediate.  In the second, use the exact identity
\(\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}
 =\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty T_\sigma^{\rm rc}
      +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}\) to obtain
\begin{align*}
 &\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma,*}^0a
 -(\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty x
       +\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,0)\\
 &\quad
 =\mathscr S_{\sigma,*}^{\rm rc}a
       -(\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty y,0)=g.
\end{align*}
Conversely, every solution has \(y=G_\sigma f\), after which the same
identity reduces the second row to the invertible anchored Schur equation;
this proves uniqueness.

Completeness follows from the coordinate isometry
\((x,a)\mapsto(x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a,a)\), exactly as in
\cref{eq:raw-compatible-full-graph-norm}.  Finally,
\cref{eq:raw-schur-fixed-normal-graph-bound,%
eq:inner-anchored-normal-action-bound} and the anchored Schur inverse
bound prove \cref{eq:inner-anchored-schur-bound}.  The construction uses
one inner phase row and no terminal-time variable, which proves the scope
statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:relative-structured-phase-response}]
Write \(\widehat a(s)=a(e_{\sigma,0})\) for \(s<0\) and
\(\widehat a(s)=a(\bar r_\sigma(s))\) for \(s\ge0\), as in
\cref{eq:formal-phase-collar-extension}.  Since
\(|a(r)|\le |r|A\), \(|a'(r)|\le A\), and
\(|\partial_s\bar r_\sigma|\le C\delta\), the continuous function
\(\widehat a\) is globally Lipschitz and
\[
 |\widehat a(s)-\widehat a(t)|\le C\delta A|s-t|,
 \qquad |\partial_s\widehat a(s)|\le C\delta A
\]
on every open cell of the finite delay-seam partition.  In
\cref{eq:reduced-flow-phase-delay-defect}, write
\[
 c_k(s)=q_0(\bar r_\sigma(s-\theta_k))
       e_{0,N}(\bar r_\sigma(s-\theta_k)).
\]
The retained coefficient bounds give
\(\|c_k\|_N+\|\partial_sc_k\|_N\le C(1+\delta)\).
Consequently
\(\|\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a(s)\|_N\le C\delta A\), and, cellwise,
\[
 \partial_s\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a
 =K\sum_kL_{k,N}\left[
   c_k'\{\widehat a-\widehat a(\,\cdot-\theta_k)\}
  +c_k\{\widehat a'-\widehat a'(\,\cdot-\theta_k)\}\right].
\]
This proves \cref{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile}.  Only the first
derivative of \(a\) is used: the two values of \(\widehat a\) are each
differentiated once, and \(a'\) is never differentiated.  At a seam the
one-sided derivatives may jump, but
\(\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a\) is continuous, so no seam measure is created.

Use the original-variable phase sources from
\cref{eq:exact-phase-normal-source-column}.  By
\cref{eq:phase-column-residual-jets,eq:fixed-phase-coefficient-bounds}
and \cref{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile},
\begin{align}
 \|\mathscr C_{Z,\sigma}a(r)\|_N
 &\le C\varepsilon(|r|+\delta)A,\notag\\
 |\mathscr C_{p,\sigma}a(r)|
 &\le C\{\varepsilon^2
       +\varepsilon\delta|r|+\varepsilon^2\delta\}A.
                                                        \label{eq:relative-phase-normal-source-profile}
\end{align}
Indeed,
\(\|R_{z,N}'\|\le C\varepsilon(1+\delta/|r|)\),
\(|R_{q,N}'|\le C\varepsilon/|r|\),
\(\|z_{0,N}'\|+\|\ell_N\|\le C|r|\),
\(|w_{0,N}'|\le C|r|\), and
\(|\mathfrak d_N|\le C\).
Since \(|r|\ge\rho_\delta\),
\begin{align}
 \varepsilon^{-1}
 \|(\mathscr C_{Z,\sigma}a,\mathscr C_{p,\sigma}a)\|_{
       \mathsf Y_{\sigma,0}}
 &\le C\left\{r_{\rm out}+\delta
 +\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2}
 +\frac{\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 +\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta^2}\right\}A\notag\\
 &\le C\kappa_\delta A.                           \label{eq:relative-phase-normal-data-bound}
\end{align}
Here
\(\varepsilon/\rho_\delta^2=O(S_\delta^{-2})\) and
\(\delta/\rho_\delta=O(S_\delta^{-1})\).

Apply the fixed-phase \(C^0\) inverse to these original-variable sources.
Its finite scalar row is zero on both branches:
\(p(0)=0\) on the attracting branch and \(p(S_r)=0\) on the repelling
branch.  The attracting old normal history is zero.  On the repelling
branch use the already closed feedback
\(\gamma=\mathfrak C_ep(0)\).  Then
\cref{eq:relative-phase-normal-data-bound,eq:fixed-phase-C0-inverse} give
\begin{equation}
 \|T_\sigma^{\rm form}a\|_{\widetilde{\mathsf X}_{\sigma,0}}
 \le C\kappa_\delta A.                            \label{eq:relative-formal-phase-state-bound}
\end{equation}
By the exact shift equivalence and uniqueness, this original-variable
solution is precisely the previously defined \(T_\sigma^{\rm form}a\).
Because
\(|\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a|\le C\delta A\) and
\(\delta/|r|\le C/S_\delta\), the shifted \(p^\sharp\)-row has the same
bound.  The displayed shifted boundary rows follow from
\(p^\sharp=p+\varepsilon\mathscr P_c a\).  At entry every difference
\(\widehat a(0)-\widehat a(-\theta_k)\) vanishes, so
\(\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a(0)=0\).

For the formal true action, apply
\cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q} to
\cref{eq:relative-formal-phase-state-bound} and add
\(\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a\):
\begin{equation}
 |\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm form}a(r)|
 \le C\{|r|+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}\kappa_\delta A
       +C\delta A
 \le C|r|\kappa_\delta A.                         \label{eq:relative-formal-phase-action-pointwise}
\end{equation}
The last inequality uses
\[
 \frac{\delta}{\rho_\delta}=O(S_\delta^{-1}),\qquad
 \frac{\delta^{2-2\vartheta}}{\rho_\delta}
 =O(\delta^{1-2\vartheta}/S_\delta).
\]

Now put \(c=E_{0,\sigma}a\).  Since
\(|c|\le r_{\rm out}A\),
\cref{eq:raw-phase-collar-response-bound} gives a raw collar response of
normalized hybrid size at most \(Cr_{\rm out}A\).  Its reconstructed action obeys,
by \cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q},
\[
 |Q_{\sigma,\rm col}c(r)|
 \le C\{|r|+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}|c|
 \le C|r|r_{\rm out}A.
\]
Adding this response to the formal one proves
\cref{eq:relative-phase-state-bound,%
eq:relative-phase-action-pointwise} for \(T_\sigma^{\rm rc}\).
The repelling estimate uses the actual total history after closing the
\(p(0)\)-feedback, as in
\cref{eq:raw-phase-collar-response-bound}.

Finally write \(x=T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+y\), where
\(y\in\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\).  Its domain graph norm controls
\(\varepsilon^{-1}\|\gamma_y\|^\sharp\),
\(\varepsilon^{-1}\|Z_y\|_\infty\), and
\(\sup|p_y|/(\varepsilon|r|)\).  Moreover,
\cref{eq:normal-phase-pointwise-Q} and
\(\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\le C|r|\) give
\[
 |\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty y(r)|
 \le C|r|\|y\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)}.
\]
Combine these estimates with
\cref{eq:relative-phase-state-bound,%
eq:relative-phase-action-pointwise} and
\[
 p_{x,a}^\sharp
 =(p_{T^{\rm rc}a}+\varepsilon\mathscr P_ca)+p_y,
 \qquad
 Q_{x,a}^\sharp
 =\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm rc}a+\mathfrak D_\sigma^\infty y.
\]
This proves the upper comparison in
\cref{eq:relative-graph-action-equivalence}; the lower one is immediate.
Completeness follows from equivalence with the already Banach anchored
graph norm.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{lem:nonlinear-reduced-flow-phase-delay}]
Put
\[
 r_k(s)=\bar r_\sigma(s-\theta_k)
       =\Phi_{q_0}^{-\delta\theta_k}(\bar r_\sigma(s)),
 \qquad
 u(s)=\widehat a_\sigma(s),\qquad
 v_k(s)=\widehat a_\sigma(s-\theta_k).
\]
The current terms in
\(\mathcal D_N^{\rm ft}[R_{\sigma,a},0]\) and in the reduced delay
\(\mathcal D_{0,N}(R_{\sigma,a}(s))\) cancel.  Indeed, the
\(q_0\)-backtrack of
\[
 R_{\sigma,a}(s)
 =\Phi_{q_0}^{u(s)+\delta\theta_k}(r_k(s))
\]
by the \(k\)-th delay is
\(\Phi_{q_0}^{u(s)}(r_k(s))\), whereas the actual delayed history is
\(\Phi_{q_0}^{v_k(s)}(r_k(s))\).  Thus the flow group law gives the exact
identity
\begin{equation}
 \Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)(s)
 =K\sum_kL_{k,N}(\eta)
 \left[
  x_{0,N}\!\left(\Phi_{q_0}^{u(s)}(r_k(s))\right)
 -x_{0,N}\!\left(\Phi_{q_0}^{v_k(s)}(r_k(s))\right)
 \right].                                        \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-exact-flow-form}
\end{equation}
This also proves exact annihilation when the complete phase is constant.

For fixed \(k,s\), define
\[
 F_{k,s}(z)=x_{0,N}\!\left(\Phi_{q_0}^{z}(r_k(s))\right),
 \qquad R(s)=|\bar r_\sigma(s)|.
\]
All phase-flow points in the fixed ball have modulus comparable with
\(R(s)\).  The critical-curve \(C^3\) estimate,
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-two-jets}, and the scalar flow identities
give
\begin{equation}
 \|F_{k,s}'\|_N+\|F_{k,s}''\|_N
       +R(s)\|F_{k,s}'''\|_N\le C.                \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-flow-jets}
\end{equation}
Moreover, because \(r_k\) itself follows the \(q_0\)-flow,
\begin{equation}
 \partial_sF_{k,s}(z)=\delta\,\partial_zF_{k,s}(z)
 \quad\hbox{with \(z\) held fixed}.               \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-flow-transport}
\end{equation}
Finally,
\[
 F_{k,s}'(0)=q_0(r_k(s))e_{0,N}(r_k(s)).
\]
The differential at zero of
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-exact-flow-form} is therefore exactly
\cref{eq:reduced-flow-phase-delay-defect}, proving
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-linearization}.

Let \(A=\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}\) and
\(d_k=u-v_k\).  Relative anchoring, delay comparability, and the constant
old-history extension give, including the first delay window,
\begin{align}
 |u|+|v_k|&\le CR A,&
 |d_k|&\le C\delta A,\notag\\
 |\dot u|+|\dot v_k|+|\dot d_k|
 &\le C\delta A                                  \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-relative-increments}
\end{align}
on every seam cell.  For two inputs \(a,b\), with the notation in the
statement, the corresponding differences satisfy
\begin{align}
 |u_a-u_b|+|v_{k,a}-v_{k,b}|&\le CRH,\notag\\
 |d_{k,a}-d_{k,b}|&\le C\delta H,\notag\\
 |\dot u_a-\dot u_b|+|\dot v_{k,a}-\dot v_{k,b}|
       +|\dot d_{k,a}-\dot d_{k,b}|
 &\le C\delta H.                                  \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-relative-differences}
\end{align}

Suppress \(k,s\), write \(v=v_k\), \(d=d_k\), and subtract the
linear term.  The remainder of one summand is
\begin{align}
 E_{k,s}(v,d)
 &=F_{k,s}(v+d)-F_{k,s}(v)-F_{k,s}'(0)d\notag\\
 &=d\int_0^1
   \{F_{k,s}'(v+td)-F_{k,s}'(0)\}\,\dd t.          \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-summand-remainder}
\end{align}
By
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-flow-jets,%
eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-relative-increments},
\begin{equation}
 \|E_{k,s}(v,d)\|_N\le C\delta R A^2.              \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-summand-value}
\end{equation}
Using \((v,d)\), rather than the two undifferenced phases, retains the
delay factor.  Along the segment joining two inputs,
\[
 \|\partial_dE\|\le CR(A+B),\qquad
 \|\partial_vE\|\le C\delta(A+B).
\]
Together with
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-relative-differences}, this proves the
value part of the two-point remainder estimate.

Differentiate
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-summand-remainder} on one seam cell.
The transport identity
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-flow-transport} yields the exact formula
\begin{align}
 \partial_sE={}&
 \delta\{F'(v+d)-F'(v)-F''(0)d\}\notag\\
 &+\{F'(v+d)-F'(0)\}\dot u
   -\{F'(v)-F'(0)\}\dot v.                        \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-summand-derivative}
\end{align}
The first brace equals
\[
 d\int_0^1\{F''(v+td)-F''(0)\}\,\dd t.
\]
The weighted third-derivative bound in
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-flow-jets} makes this
\(O(\delta A^2)\); its prefactor in
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-summand-derivative} makes the contribution
\(O(\delta^2A^2)\).  Since
\(|\bar r_\sigma|\ge\rho_\delta\gg\delta\), this is
\(O(\delta R A^2)\).  The last two terms have the same bound by
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-flow-jets,%
eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-relative-increments}.  Hence
\begin{equation}
 \|\partial_sE_{k,s}\|_N\le C\delta R A^2.         \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-summand-derivative-bound}
\end{equation}
The mean-value theorem applied to
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-summand-derivative}, using
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-flow-jets,%
eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-relative-increments,%
eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-relative-differences}, gives
\begin{equation}
 \|\partial_sE_k(a)-\partial_sE_k(b)\|_N
 \le C\delta R(A+B)H.                             \label{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-summand-derivative-two-point}
\end{equation}

The linear summand \(F'(0)d\) and its derivative
\[
 \delta F''(0)d+F'(0)\dot d
\]
are \(O(\delta A)\) and Lipschitz with constant \(C\delta\).  Summation
with the common layer bound now proves
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-quadratic-bound,%
eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-two-point}.

It remains only to check seams.  At \(s=0\), every positive-delay
difference has
\(u=v_k=a(e_{\sigma,0})\), so \(\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)(0)=0\).  At
\(s=\theta_k\), the old definition gives
\(v_k=a(e_{\sigma,0})\), while the current definition gives
\(v_k=a(\bar r_\sigma(0))\), the same value.  Hence
\(\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)\), its linearization, and its remainder are continuous.
Their one-sided derivatives may jump, but continuity creates no
distributional seam measure.  A zero-delay term has \(u=v_k\) identically
and vanishes before any estimate.  Uniform boundedness of
\(\pi_N^T\) and \(P_{\perp,N}\) proves the projected assertions.  The
definition itself shows that no nonlinear raw-compatible history has been
constructed.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{lem:second-jet-raw-history-assembler}]
We first justify the relative endpoint enlargement.  Since
\(\widetilde I_{\sigma,\chi}\Subset J_\sigma\), all coefficient estimates
used in the finite collar recursion hold there after decreasing
\(\delta_0\).  Moreover,
\[
 |\Phi_{q_0}^{t}(e_*)-e_*|\le q_+|t|.
\]
Thus \cref{eq:raw-assembler-relative-width} gives
\cref{eq:raw-assembler-relative-flow-bound}.  Since
\[
 \frac{\delta\theta_m}{|e_*|}
 \le\frac{2\alpha\theta_m}{S_\delta}\longrightarrow0,
\]
we may also require
\(q_+\delta\theta_m\le q_+\chi|e_*|\).  Applying the flow estimate once
more proves
\cref{eq:raw-assembler-flow-backtrack-tube}.  In particular every point of
the enlarged collar has \(|e|\ge\rho_\delta/2\), remains of the same sign,
and stays in a common coefficient domain.  The nested-annulus construction
in the proofs of
\cref{lem:raw-compatible-moving-core-collar,%
lem:raw-compatible-base-collar-refinement}
therefore repeats verbatim on the enlargement.  This is the promised
extension at all four closed endpoints; it uses neither a backward physical
orbit nor an identification of two fading norms.

We next add the missing second endpoint derivative to that finite
compatibility recursion.  Retain the critical and backtrack estimates
through order \(h+3\).  The definitions of the two residuals, the weighted
critical estimates, and the complete joint-difference estimate
\cref{eq:backtrack-joint-difference} give
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal L_e^nR_{z,N}(e)\|_N
 \le C_h\varepsilon|e|^{1-n},\qquad
 |\mathcal L_e^nR_{q,N}(e)|
 \le C_h\varepsilon|e|^{-n},
 \quad 0\le n\le h+1.                            \label{eq:second-endpoint-residual-jets}
\end{equation}
Indeed, this is
\cref{eq:base-collar-residual-size} with two further retained derivatives,
translated from weighted \(\partial_e\)-jets to powers of
\(\mathcal L_e=q_0(e)\partial_e\).  The delay gain here belongs to the
complete current-minus-backtrack difference in
\cref{eq:backtrack-joint-difference}; no individual backtrack derivative
is claimed to carry a factor \(\delta\).

Along the formal core, \(\partial_\theta=\delta\mathcal L_e\).
Consequently, for \(0\le m\le h-1\) and \(\ell=0,1,2\),
\begin{align*}
 \delta^{m+1}
 \|\mathcal L_e^\ell\partial_\theta^mE_v^0(0)\|_N
 &=\delta^{2m}
   \|\mathcal L_e^{m+\ell}R_{z,N}(e)\|_N\\
 &\le C_h\varepsilon|e|^{1-\ell}
       \left(\frac{\delta^2}{|e|}\right)^m,\\
 \delta^{m+1}
 |\mathcal L_e^\ell\partial_\theta^mE_w^0(0)|
 &=\delta^{2m+2}
   |\mathcal L_e^{m+\ell}R_{q,N}(e)|\\
 &\le C_h\varepsilon|e|^{1-\ell}
       \left(\frac{\delta^2}{|e|}\right)^{m+1}.
\end{align*}
Because \(|e|\ge\rho_\delta/2\), both parentheses are at most one for
small \(\delta\).  Hence
\begin{align}
 \max_{0\le m\le h-1}\delta^{m+1}
 \bigl\{&
  \|\mathcal L_e^\ell
          \partial_\theta^mE_v^0(0)\|_N
 +|\mathcal L_e^\ell
          \partial_\theta^mE_w^0(0)|\bigr\}
 \le C_h\varepsilon |e|^{1-\ell}.                \label{eq:second-endpoint-formal-defect-jets}
\end{align}

Apply \(\mathcal L_e^\ell\), \(\ell=0,1,2\), to the exact resource
recursion
\cref{eq:moving-core-resource-defect-recursion} and to the triangular
voltage recursion used in
\cref{eq:moving-core-voltage-defect-recursion}.  At level \(j\), the
top jet occurs only on the left.  On the right, every term contains either
a formal defect from
\cref{eq:second-endpoint-formal-defect-jets} or a lower compatibility
defect.  Leibniz' rule distributes the endpoint derivatives among bounded
coefficients and those lower defects.  Thus, with
\[
 M_{j,\ell}
 =\max_{0\le i\le j}\delta^i
   \{\|\mathcal L_e^\ell b_{i,e}\|_N
                   +|\mathcal L_e^\ell d_{i,e}|\},
\]
the finite recursion has the estimate
\begin{equation}
 M_{j,\ell}\le C_h\left\{
   \varepsilon|e|^{1-\ell}
  +\sum_{i<j}\sum_{q=0}^{\ell}
       |e|^{q-\ell}M_{i,q}\right\}.               \label{eq:second-endpoint-triangular-induction}
\end{equation}
The base values vanish, and the first rows are the exact defects in
\cref{eq:moving-core-first-defects}.  Induction first in \(j\) and then
in \(\ell\) proves
\cref{eq:second-endpoint-collar-jets}.

The Hermite operator is independent of \(e\), and
\[
 \Delta\phi_e
 =\mathcal E_{\delta,h}(b_{0,e},\ldots,b_{h,e}).
\]
If \(|e|\asymp|e_*|\), its support remains in
\([-c_H\delta,0]\).  On this short interval the fixed weight
\(e^{2\kappa_{e_*}\theta}\) is at most one, so the Hermite estimate applied
to
\cref{eq:second-endpoint-collar-jets} proves
\cref{eq:second-endpoint-collar-cap}.  Notice that this argument compares
only compactly supported corrections; it does not assert equivalence of
the moving and fixed fading norms on the whole old-history interval.

We next construct the fixed-reference chart without first invoking the
moving-weight domain of \(\Gamma_e^h\).  Given \(U=(Z,p,\zeta)\), start with
\[
 a_{0,e}(U)=V_{0,N}(e)+Z,\qquad
 c_{0,e}(U)=w_{\rm seed,N}(e)-p.
\]
For \(1\le j\le h\), set successively
\begin{align}
 \psi_{j-1,e}(U)
 ={}&\widehat\gamma_e^0+\zeta
   +\mathcal E_{\delta,h}
      (a_{0,e}(U),\ldots,a_{j-1,e}(U),0,\ldots,0),\notag\\
 a_{j,e}(U)
 ={}&\mathfrak V_{j,N}
   (\psi_{j-1,e}(U),c_{0,e}(U);
                         \delta,\nu,\eta),        \label{eq:fixed-reference-collar-jet-recursion}
\end{align}
and let \(c_{j,e}(U)\) be the resource jet forced at the same level, using
\(\mathfrak G_{N,\delta,\nu}\) whenever a resource derivative occurs.
Finally set
\begin{equation}
 \phi_{e;e_*}^{\rm fr}(U)
 =\widehat\gamma_e^0+\zeta
      +\mathcal E_{\delta,h}
          (a_{0,e}(U),\ldots,a_{h,e}(U)),\qquad
 \Gamma_{e;e_*}^{h,\rm fr}(U;\nu,\eta)
 =(\phi_{e;e_*}^{\rm fr}(U),c_{0,e}(U)).          \label{eq:fixed-reference-chart-recursion}
\end{equation}
At a strict delay, the Hermite lift vanishes with all required derivatives,
so this finite triangular definition is not circular.  On its intersection
with the old moving-cap domain it satisfies the same compatibility
recursion and therefore equals \(\Gamma_e^h\) by uniqueness.

Set
\[
 b_{j,e}(U)=a_{j,e}(U)-a_{j,e}^0,\qquad
 d_{j,e}(U)=c_{j,e}(U)-c_{j,e}^0.
\]
For \(j\ge1\), collect the strictly delayed data directions in
\begin{equation}
 \mathfrak E_{j,e_*}\zeta
 =\left(
   \delta^{r+2}\partial_\theta^r\zeta(-\theta_k)
   \right)_{\substack{\theta_k>0\\0\le r\le j-1}}.
                                                               \label{eq:fixed-reference-delayed-direction-map}
\end{equation}
The fixed-reference cap norm gives
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathfrak E_{j,e_*}\zeta\|
 \le C_h\delta^2e^{2\kappa_{e_*}\theta_m}
       \|\zeta\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}
 \le C_h\delta^{2-2\vartheta}
       \|\zeta\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}
 \le C_h\|\zeta\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,e_*}.           \label{eq:fixed-reference-delayed-core-bound}
\end{equation}
The map \(\mathfrak E_{j,e_*}\) is independent of \(e\).  This is the
strict-delay compensation that permits a fixed-reference ball; no
equivalence of the fixed and moving fading weights is used.

Put
\[
 Y_{j,e}(U)
 =(\delta^jb_{j,e}(U),\delta^jd_{j,e}(U)),\qquad
 \|(x,y)\|_\times=\|x\|_N+|y|.
\]
After multiplying the order-\(j\) compatibility identities by
\(\delta^j\), retardedness gives a finite recursion of the form
\begin{equation}
 Y_{j,e}(U)
 =\mathfrak F_{j,e}\left(
   Z,\frac p{|e_*|},\mathfrak E_{j,e_*}\zeta,
   Y_{0,e}(U),\ldots,Y_{j-1,e}(U)\right),          \label{eq:mixed-scaled-compatibility-map}
\end{equation}
where the factor \(|e_*|\) multiplying the normalized \(p\)-coordinate is
included in \(\mathfrak F_{j,e}\).  On a fixed bounded scaled-jet ball,
the polynomial current field, the resource recursion, the weighted
coefficient estimates, and
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-delayed-core-bound} give
\begin{equation}
 |e_*|^q\|\,\mathcal L_e^qD^\ell
              \mathfrak F_{j,e}\,\|
 \le C_h,\qquad q+\ell\le2.                       \label{eq:mixed-scaled-compatibility-coefficients}
\end{equation}
Here \(D^\ell\) denotes any \(\ell\)-fold derivative in the displayed data
and lower-jet variables, with their product norms.  In particular, no
endpoint derivative falls on \(\mathfrak E_{j,e_*}\zeta\).

To expose both the domain and the derivative induction, define
\begin{align}
 B_j^q(U)
 &=|e_*|^q\max_{0\le i\le j}
       \|\mathcal L_e^qY_{i,e}(U)\|_\times,
       &&0\le q\le2,                              \label{eq:mixed-background-majorants}\\
 M_j^{q,\ell}(U;H_1,\ldots,H_\ell)
 &=|e_*|^q\max_{0\le i\le j}
 \|\mathcal L_e^qD_U^\ell Y_{i,e}(U)
       [H_1,\ldots,H_\ell]\|_\times,
       &&1\le\ell\le2.                            \label{eq:mixed-data-majorants}
\end{align}
Since \(h\) is fixed, the finite algebraic recursion and
\cref{eq:mixed-scaled-compatibility-coefficients} give, for an integer
\(m_h\) independent of \(N,\delta,e_*\),
\begin{equation}
 1+\sum_{q=0}^2B_j^q(U)
 \le C_h\left(1+\sum_{q=0}^2B_{j-1}^q(U)\right)^{m_h}.
                                                               \label{eq:mixed-background-recurrence}
\end{equation}
The level-zero rows are \(b_{0,e}(U)=Z\) and \(d_{0,e}(U)=-p\).
Therefore the recursion stays in a common bounded jet ball on an open
dimension-uniform set
\(\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}<r_h\), and
\(\max_{0\le q\le2}B_h^q(U)\le C_h\) on a smaller closed subball.
This proves that \cref{eq:fixed-reference-chart-recursion} defines the
asserted fixed-reference extension and, by uniqueness on the overlap, the
agreement with \(\Gamma_e^h\).

With the background bounds absorbed into \(C_h\), the chain rule applied
to \cref{eq:mixed-scaled-compatibility-map} gives exactly the three
recurrences needed here:
\begin{align}
 M_j^{0,1}(H)
 &\le C_h\left\{\|H\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}
   +\sum_{i<j}M_i^{0,1}(H)\right\},\notag\\
 M_j^{0,2}(H_1,H_2)
 &\le C_h\left\{
   \prod_{\ell=1}^2\|H_\ell\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}
   +\sum_{i<j}M_i^{0,2}(H_1,H_2)\right.\notag\\[-1mm]
 &\hspace{28mm}\left.
   +\sum_{i,k<j}
      M_i^{0,1}(H_1)M_k^{0,1}(H_2)\right\},\notag\\
 M_j^{1,1}(H)
 &\le C_h\left\{\|H\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}
   +\sum_{i<j}
      \bigl(M_i^{1,1}(H)+M_i^{0,1}(H)\bigr)\right\}.
                                                               \label{eq:mixed-data-recurrences}
\end{align}
The second line displays the product of two lower first variations.  In the
last line, \(\mathcal L_e\) may fall either on a coefficient or on a lower
background jet; \cref{eq:mixed-background-recurrence} controls both.
At level zero,
\[
 M_0^{0,1}(H)\le C\|H\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*},\qquad
 M_0^{0,2}(H_1,H_2)=M_0^{1,1}(H)=0.
\]
Finite induction in \(j\) yields
\begin{align}
 M_h^{0,1}(H)&\le C_h\|H\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*},\notag\\
 M_h^{0,2}(H_1,H_2)
 &\le C_h\prod_{i=1}^2\|H_i\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*},\notag\\
 M_h^{1,1}(H)&\le C_h\|H\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}.     \label{eq:mixed-majorants-closed}
\end{align}
Undoing the factor \(|e_*|^q\) gives, uniformly for
\(e=\Phi_{q_0}^c(e_*)\), \(|c|\le\chi|e_*|\), and \(U\) in that smaller
closed subball,
\begin{align}
 &\max_{0\le j\le h}\delta^j
 \left\{
  \|\mathcal L_e^qD_U^\ell b_{j,e}(U)
            [H_1,\ldots,H_\ell]\|_N\right.\notag\\[-1mm]
 &\hspace{43mm}\left.
  +|\mathcal L_e^qD_U^\ell d_{j,e}(U)
            [H_1,\ldots,H_\ell]|
 \right\}\notag\\
 &\qquad\le C_h|e_*|^{-q}
       \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\|H_i\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*},
 \quad 1\le\ell\le2,\quad q+\ell\le2.             \label{eq:mixed-endpoint-collar-jet-bound}
\end{align}
These are precisely the cases
\((q,\ell)=(0,1),(0,2),(1,1)\).  This is the mixed
\(e\)--\(p\) and \(e\)--core estimate not supplied by fixed-\(e\)
smoothness alone; no case with \(q+\ell>2\) is asserted.

Subtracting the two Hermite decompositions gives the exact fixed-chart
identity
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,U)
 =\zeta+\mathcal E_{\delta,h}
       (b_{0,e_c}(U),\ldots,b_{h,e_c}(U)).         \label{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-exact-Hermite}
\end{equation}
Because
\(\frac{\dd}{\dd c}=\mathcal L_{e_c}\),
\cref{eq:second-endpoint-collar-jets,%
eq:mixed-endpoint-collar-jet-bound} and the Hermite estimate prove that
this map is \(C^2\) and give
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-C2-bounds}.  At \(U=0,c=0\),
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-exact-Hermite} is
\(\Delta\phi_{e_*}\), while its \(c\)-derivative is
\[
 \mathcal L_{e_*}(\phi_{e_*}^{\rm rc}-\phi_{e_*}^0)
 =\gamma_{e_*}^{\rm ph}.
\]
This proves
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-first-jets}.

Use the exact decomposition
\begin{align*}
 &\mathscr H(c,U)-\mathscr H(0,0)
       -c\partial_c\mathscr H(0,0)-D_U\mathscr H(0,0)U\\
 &\quad=
 \{\mathscr H(c,0)-\mathscr H(0,0)
             -c\partial_c\mathscr H(0,0)\}\\
 &\qquad+
 \{\mathscr H(c,U)-\mathscr H(c,0)-D_U\mathscr H(c,0)U\}\\
 &\qquad+
 \{D_U\mathscr H(c,0)-D_U\mathscr H(0,0)\}U .
\end{align*}
The integral Taylor formula and
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-C2-bounds} bound these three braces,
respectively, by
\[
 C_h\frac{\varepsilon c^2}{|e_*|},\qquad
 C_h\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}^2,\qquad
 C_h\frac{|c|}{|e_*|}\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}.
\]
This proves
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-taylor}.  The cap norm dominates the frozen
sharp norm.  If
\(\|U\|_{\mathrm{col},e_*}\le\varepsilon R\), divide the last estimate by
\(\varepsilon\), use \(|e_*|\le1\) and \(\varepsilon\le1\), and absorb the
three terms into
\(C_h(|c|/|e_*|+R)^2\).  This proves
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-graph-remainder}.  Every construction took
place on a prescribed compatible history segment; no backward orbit or
tracker existence has been inferred.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual}]
We first verify the exact assembly, before making any estimate.  Extend the
current normal path by
\[
 \widehat Z_x(s)=
 \begin{cases}
  \gamma_x(s),&-\theta_m\le s<0,\\
  Z_x(s),&s\ge0.
 \end{cases}
\]
The seam condition in \(\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\) makes this
extension continuous.  On the old segment \(\widehat Z_x=\gamma_x\) is
an additive voltage correction; it is not asserted to lie in \(E_N\).
The formal part of the complete voltage history is
the \(q_0\)-flow history used in
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-functional}; its current phase is
\(r_a(s)=\Theta_a(r_0(s))\).  Since the network delay is linear in the
voltage history, adding the normal history gives the identity
\begin{align}
 &K\sum_kL_{k,N}
    \{\widehat v_{x,a}(s)-\widehat v_{x,a}(s-\theta_k)\}
          -\mathcal D_{0,N}(r_a(s))\notag\\
 &\qquad=\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)(s)
  +K\sum_kL_{k,N}
       \{\widehat Z_x(s)-\widehat Z_x(s-\theta_k)\}\notag\\
 &\qquad=\Pi_{\sigma,N}(a)(s)+D_N(x)(s).          \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-delay-split}
\end{align}
No Taylor expansion is used here.  This proves
\cref{eq:fixed-section-exact-delay-splitting} whenever the boundary row is
imposed.

Substitute \cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-delay-split} into
\cref{eq:fold-time-speed-reconstruction}.  The result is exactly
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-speed}.  Next subtract the fixed-base
linear \(Z\)-row in \cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} from
\cref{eq:fold-time-tracker-Z}.  The uncancelled terms are
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-Z-source}.  Subtracting the corresponding
\(p\)-row gives
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-p-source}; in particular the last brace is
\[
 \varepsilon^2\{(q_0(r_a)+Q^{\rm nl})d_a-d_0Q_{\rm n}\}.
\]
Keeping both terms is essential: their \(x\)-differentials cancel at the
base.  Finally, the exact scalar identity
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-row-identity} says that the collective
row is precisely
\[
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a)
             -Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}(x,a)=0.
\]
This proves that \cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-residual} is the exact
fixed-section residual of the fold-time equations.

We next check that the boundary package has neither a missing nor a
duplicated row.  The exact Hermite identity
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-exact-Hermite} gives
\[
 \mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(c,(0,p,0))(0)=0.
\]
Thus the equation for the first normal-data component, together with the
seam already built into \(\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)\), forces
\(Z_x(0)=\gamma_x(0)=0\).  On the attracting branch the second component
of \(\mathscr N_a\) independently imposes \(p_x(0)=0\).  On the repelling
branch, the equation
\[
 \gamma_x-\mathfrak C_ep_x(0)
 =\mathscr H_{-\rho_\delta}^{\rm rc}
       (c_r,(0,p_x(0),0))-\mathfrak C_ep_x(0)
\]
is equivalent to the single history equation
\(\gamma_x=\mathscr H_r^{\rm can}(x,a)\), while the remaining finite row
is \(p_x(S_r)=0\).  Hence the seam is not imposed a second time.

We now compute the differential.  At \(u=(x,a)=0\),
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
 \Pi_N(0)=0,\qquad
 D\Pi_N(0)a=\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a,\qquad
 DQ_\sigma^{\rm nl}(0)(x,a)
 =Q_{\rm n}[x]+\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a.
\end{gathered}                                    \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-speed-linearization}
\end{equation}
The quadratic maps \(h_{c,N}\) and \(h_{\perp,N}\) have zero differential
at \(Z=0\).  Differentiating
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-Z-source} in \(a\) therefore gives
\[
 -q_0R_{z,N}'a
 +\varepsilon\{\mathscr P_{\perp,\sigma,N}a
       -z_{0,N}'\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a\}
 =\mathscr C_{Z,\sigma}a.
\]
Its \(x\)-differential is zero.  In the \(p\)-source, the \(x\)-part of
the last brace is
\[
 \varepsilon^2\{d_0Q_{\rm n}[x]-d_0Q_{\rm n}[x]\}=0.
\]
The \(a\)-part, including the derivative of \(q_0(r_a)d_a\), is
\begin{align*}
 &\varepsilon w_0'\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a
 +\varepsilon^2d_0\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a
 +\varepsilon^2q_0(q_0d_0)'a\\
 &\qquad=\varepsilon\{w_0'+\varepsilon d_0\}
       \mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a
       +\varepsilon q_0R_{q,N}'a
 =\mathscr C_{p,\sigma}a.
\end{align*}
Here \(R_{q,N}=\varepsilon q_0d_0\).  Thus the bulk differential is the
formal phase column of
\cref{eq:exact-phase-normal-source-column}.

For the history row,
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-first-jets} gives
\[
 \partial_c\mathscr H_{e_*}^{\rm rc}(0,0)
       =\gamma_{e_*}^{\rm ph}.
\]
By the definition of the closed compatible feedback in
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-collar-feedback}, the \(p\)-column of the repelling
assembler is
\[
 D_p\mathscr H_{-\rho_\delta}^{\rm rc}(0,0)p
       =\mathfrak C_ep.
\]
It cancels the explicit subtraction in
\cref{eq:canonical-nonlinear-history-rows}.  The attracting package has no
variable endpoint data.  Hence the complete differential of
\(\mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm nl}\) is
\begin{equation}
 D\mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm nl}(0)(x,a)
       =\mathscr N_\sigma T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a,     \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-source-linearization}
\end{equation}
and contains no \(x\)-column.  Combining
\cref{eq:anchored-nonlinear-full-linearization,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-speed-linearization,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-source-linearization}
proves \cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-linearization}.  Its inverse is
the one in
\cref{eq:inner-anchored-schur-inverse}, with the equivalent graph--action
norm from \cref{eq:relative-graph-action-equivalence}.

It remains to prove that the exact formulas define a tame map in that
space.  Put
\[
 A=\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}},\qquad
 R=\|(x,a)\|_{\mathcal G_{\sigma,\delta}^{\rm rga}},
 \qquad P=\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a,
\]
and use the shifted quantities
\[
 p^\sharp=p_x+\varepsilon P,\qquad
 Q^\sharp=Q_{\rm n}[x]+P.
\]
By definition of the graph--action norm,
\begin{equation}
 \|Z_x(r)\|_N\le\varepsilon R,\qquad
 |p^\sharp(r)|\le\varepsilon|r|R,\qquad
 |Q^\sharp(r)|\le|r|R,\qquad
 \|\gamma_x\|_{N,e_\sigma,\delta}^\sharp
       \le\varepsilon R.                         \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-rga-scales}
\end{equation}
The formal entry extension gives \(P(0)=0\) on the repelling branch.
Consequently
\begin{equation}
 |p_x(0)|\le\varepsilon\rho_\delta R,\qquad
 \|(0,p_x(0),0)\|_{\mathrm{col},-\rho_\delta}
       \le\varepsilon R.                         \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-repelling-data-scale}
\end{equation}
This is the reason for using \(p^\sharp\).  Replacing
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-rga-scales} by the older
\(m_\delta\)-bound would introduce the unbounded ratio
\(m_\delta(r)/|r|\) at \(r=\rho_\delta\).

The phase chart and the coefficient estimates
\cref{eq:principal-green-coefficients,eq:principal-green-q-bounds}
give, on a fixed small ball,
\begin{equation}
 |r_a-r_0|\le C|r_0|A,\qquad
 |b(r_a)-b(r_0)|\le C\omega_b(r_0)A,              \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-moving-coefficients}
\end{equation}
where one may take
\[
 \omega_{\mathcal C}=\omega_{z_0'}=\omega_{w_0'}
   =\omega_\ell=|r_0|,\qquad \omega_d=1.
\]
Taylor's formula gives the corresponding quadratic and two-point
remainders after subtracting \(q_0b'a\).  For the affine residuals those
remainders satisfy
\begin{align}
 \|R_{z,N}(r_a)-R_{z,N}(r_0)
          -q_0R_{z,N}'(r_0)a\|_N
 &\le C\varepsilon|r_0|A^2,\notag\\
 |R_{q,N}(r_a)-R_{q,N}(r_0)
          -q_0R_{q,N}'(r_0)a|
 &\le C\varepsilon A^2.                          \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-affine-remainders}
\end{align}
The same bounds with \(A^2\) replaced by
\((A+B)\|a-b\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}\) hold for two inputs.  These are
direct consequences of
\cref{eq:second-endpoint-residual-jets}; all intermediate phase points
remain in the enlarged same-sign collar.

The polynomial current field and the common node norm give
\begin{equation}
 |h_{c,N}(r,Z)|+\|h_{\perp,N}(r,Z)\|_N
 \le C\|Z\|_N^2,                                 \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-quadraticity}
\end{equation}
together with the usual two-point bound, uniformly in \(N\).  Moreover,
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-quadratic-bound} gives
\begin{equation}
 \Pi_N(a)=\mathscr P_{\sigma,N}a+\mathcal R_{\Pi,\sigma,N}(a),\qquad
 \|\mathcal R_{\Pi,\sigma,N}(a)(s)\|_N
       \le C\delta|r_0(s)|A^2,                   \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-Pi-scale}
\end{equation}
and its two-point analogue.  Subtracting \(Q^\sharp\) from
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-speed} now gives the exact cancellation
\begin{equation}
 Q_\sigma^{\rm nl}-Q^\sharp
 =-\varepsilon^{-1}\{(\ell_a-\ell_0)Z_x
                  +h_{c,N}(r_a,Z_x)\}
       +\mathcal R_{\Pi,c,\sigma,N}(a).           \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-speed-remainder}
\end{equation}
Using
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-rga-scales,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-moving-coefficients,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-quadraticity,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-Pi-scale}
and
\(\varepsilon/\rho_\delta\le C\delta/S_\delta\), we obtain
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}
   \frac{|Q_\sigma^{\rm nl}(u)(r)-Q^\sharp(u)(r)|}{|r|}
 \le CR^2,                                       \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-speed-quadratic-bound}
\end{equation}
again with the corresponding two-point estimate.

For completeness, we record the scale ledger for the two normal source
rows after their affine value and linear phase column have been removed:
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\|\operatorname{Rem}S_Z(u)(r)\|_N}{\varepsilon}
 +\frac{|\operatorname{Rem}S_p(u)(r)|}
             {\varepsilon|r|^2}
 \le CR^2.                                       \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-source-ledger}
\end{equation}
Indeed, coefficient--state products use
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-moving-coefficients} and
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-rga-scales}; the \(h\)-terms use
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-quadraticity}; the phase-delay terms
use \cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-Pi-scale}; and the collective linear
delay is recovered from the algebraic shifted row as
\[
 D_{c,N}(x)=Q^\sharp-\varepsilon^{-1}
                   (p^\sharp-\ell_0Z_x).
\]
The only apparently singular terms occur in the \(p\)-row.  There
\begin{itemize}
\item
\((w_a'-w_0')p^\sharp=O(\varepsilon|r|^2R^2)\);
\item
the affine remainder contributes
\(O(\varepsilon^2R^2)\), whose normalized size is controlled by
\(\varepsilon/\rho_\delta^2=O(S_\delta^{-2})\);
\item
the last brace is harmless because its entire \(x\)-linear part cancels,
as shown above, and
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-speed-quadratic-bound} controls what remains.
\end{itemize}
Every other term has an additional factor \(|r|\), \(\delta\), or
\(\varepsilon/|r|\).  Applying the same decomposition on the segment
between two inputs proves the two-point version of
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-source-ledger}.

The phase action has the exact remainder
\[
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a)-q_0^2a'
 =q_0(r)\{q_0(\Theta_a(r))-q_0(r)\}a'(r).
\]
The relative phase norm and
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-q-class} therefore give
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(a)-q_0^2a'\|_{\phi,\infty}
 \le CA^2,                                       \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-phase-action-remainder}
\end{equation}
with a two-point constant \(C(A+B)\).  Combining this with
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-speed-quadratic-bound} controls the phase
component of the residual.

Finally, the attracting history remainder is
\(\operatorname{Rem}\mathscr H(c_a,0)\).  On the repelling branch it is
\[
 \operatorname{Rem}\mathscr H
       (c_r,(0,p_x(0),0));
\]
the linear \(p_x(0)\)-term has already cancelled against
\(\mathfrak C_ep_x(0)\).  \Cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-graph-remainder,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-repelling-data-scale}
give, in both cases,
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon^{-1}
 \|\operatorname{Rem}B_{\gamma,\sigma}^{\rm nl}(u)\|_N^\sharp
 \le CR^2,                                       \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-history-remainder}
\end{equation}
and the \(C^2\) bounds in
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-C2-bounds} give the two-point version.
\Cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-source-ledger,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-phase-action-remainder,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-history-remainder}
prove both that the map is \(C^1\) on a uniform graph ball and the claimed
estimate \cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-two-point}.

At the origin,
\[
 \mathscr S_\sigma^{\rm nl}(0,0)
 =\bigl(\Delta\phi_{e_{\sigma,0}},0,
                    -R_{z,N}(r_0),\varepsilon R_{q,N}(r_0)\bigr),
\]
which proves \cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-base-residual}.  The collar
estimate gives
\(\varepsilon^{-1}\|\Delta\phi_e\|_N^\sharp\le C|e|\).
Furthermore,
\[
 \varepsilon^{-1}\|R_{z,N}\|_\infty\le Cr_{\rm out},
 \qquad
 \sup_{r\in I_\sigma}\frac{|R_{q,N}(r)|}{|r|^2}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta^2}
 \le CS_\delta^{-2}.
\]
These estimates prove
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-base-bound}.

We record the collar-radius uniformity needed for the quantifiers in the
statement.  Choose \(\bar r_{\rm out}\) inside the common coefficient and
same-sign flow collars used throughout the retained results.  The bounds
for \(q_0\), the critical curve, and the backtracks are then uniform on
\[
 0<r_{\rm out}\le\bar r_{\rm out},
 \qquad \rho_\delta\le |r|\le r_{\rm out}.
\]
The principal Green estimates are written in the normalized
\((Z,p/|r|)\) state and \((F,H/|r|^2)\) source norms; the finite outer
row is likewise normalized by \(r_{\rm out}\).  Hence
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-principal-C0-bound,%
eq:fixed-phase-moving-scalar-weight}
has one common constant as the outer collar is decreased.  For each fixed
\(r_{\rm out}\), decrease \(\delta_0(r_{\rm out})\) so that
\(\rho_\delta\le r_{\rm out}/2\), all occurrences of
\(\delta/r_{\rm out}^2\) satisfy their retained smallness gates, and the
feedback numbers in
\cref{eq:fixed-phase-feedback-smallness} are below their fixed Neumann
thresholds.  This gives one common fixed-phase normal inverse.

The unperturbed inner-anchored phase inverse is the explicit integral in
\cref{eq:inner-anchored-linear-explicit-inverse}; its relative norm is
uniform.  Its Schur perturbation has the improving factor displayed in
\cref{eq:relative-raw-action-smallness}, so after fixing
\(\bar r_{\rm out}\) small enough,
\cref{eq:inner-anchored-schur-neumann} gives the common inverse bound
\(M\).  The fixed-reference history recursion uses the scaled variables
in \cref{eq:mixed-scaled-compatibility-map}; its domain and first two
data derivatives are uniform in the endpoint by
\cref{eq:mixed-scaled-compatibility-coefficients,%
eq:mixed-majorants-closed}.  Choosing \(R_0\) below that common history
ball and the common relative phase ball therefore costs no power of
\(r_{\rm out}\).  The coefficient, phase-delay, and history estimates used
in
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-source-ledger,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-phase-action-remainder,%
eq:proof-fixed-section-history-remainder}
then give common constants \(C_b\) and \(C_2\).  All coefficient estimates
invoked here are uniform on the retained parameter ball.  This proves the
full uniformity assertion; fixed parameter means only that no parameter
derivative has been taken.

If the residual vanishes,
the formal old voltage in
\cref{eq:fixed-section-complete-voltage-history} is
\(\phi_{e_c}^0\), while the boundary equation and
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-assembler} give
\[
 \widehat v_{x,a}(s)=\phi_{e_c}^{\rm rc}(s),
 \qquad -\theta_m\le s\le0,
 \qquad e_c=\Theta_a(e_{\sigma,0}).
\]
The physical old coordinates in
\cref{eq:fixed-section-physical-old-decomposition} satisfy the algebraic
identity
\begin{equation}
 \mathbf1+r_-(s)\mathbf1+z_{0,N}(r_-(s))+Z_-(s)
 =\mathbf1+R_{\sigma,a}(s)\mathbf1
       +z_{0,N}(R_{\sigma,a}(s))+\gamma_x(s).    \label{eq:proof-fixed-section-old-voltage-identity}
\end{equation}
They are continuous and \(Z_-(s)\in E_N\).  At the seam,
\(R_{\sigma,a}(0)=r_a(0)=e_c\) and
\(\gamma_x(0)=0\), hence
\[
 r_-(0)=r_a(0),\qquad Z_-(0)=0=Z_x(0).
\]
The order-(h) compatibility recursion for
\(\phi_{e_c}^{\rm rc}\), together with the exact current residual, gives
the classical seam compatibility required by the fold-time equations.
Thus use \((r_-,Z_-)\) on the old interval and \((r_a,Z_x)\) on the
current interval in
\cref{prop:fold-time-tracker-normal-form}.  The exact delay identity at the
beginning of the proof then gives the asserted forward RFDE segment.
Neither argument extends the canonical old history to a past orbit or fixes
the opposite moving event.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{cor:fixed-section-canonical-zero}]
Put
\[
 L_\sigma=D\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0),
 \qquad b_\sigma=\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0).
\]
On the closed \(r_\delta\)-ball define
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal T_\sigma(u)
 =-L_\sigma^{-1}\{b_\sigma+\mathcal Q_\sigma(u)\}. \label{eq:fixed-section-zero-contraction}
\end{equation}
Since \(\mathcal Q_\sigma(0)=0\),
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-two-point} with one argument zero gives
\(\|\mathcal Q_\sigma(u)\|\le C_2\|u\|^2\).  By
\cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-radii},
\[
 r_\delta<R_{\rm u}\le\frac{R_0}{2},
 \qquad
 MC_2r_\delta\le\frac14.
\]
Consequently, for \(\|u\|\le r_\delta\),
\[
 \|\mathcal T_\sigma(u)\|
 \le MC_b\lambda_\delta+MC_2r_\delta^2
 \le\frac{r_\delta}{2}+\frac{r_\delta}{4}
 <r_\delta,
\]
whereas for two points in that ball,
\[
 \|\mathcal T_\sigma(u)-\mathcal T_\sigma(v)\|
 \le2MC_2r_\delta\|u-v\|
 \le\frac12\|u-v\|.
\]
The contraction theorem proves existence and
\cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-size}.  If \(u,v\) are two zeros in the open
\(R_{\rm u}\)-ball, then
\[
 \|u-v\|
 \le MC_2(\|u\|+\|v\|)\|u-v\|
 <\frac12\|u-v\|,
\]
so \(u=v\).  At the fixed point,
\[
 u_{\sigma,\delta}+L_\sigma^{-1}b_\sigma
 =-L_\sigma^{-1}\mathcal Q_\sigma(u_{\sigma,\delta}),
\]
which proves \cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-newton-jet}.
Since \(\mathcal Q_\sigma\) is \(C^1\), its two-point bound gives
\[
 \|D\mathcal Q_\sigma(u_{\sigma,\delta})\|
 \le2C_2\|u_{\sigma,\delta}\|.
\]
Therefore
\[
 \|L_\sigma^{-1}
       D\mathcal Q_\sigma(u_{\sigma,\delta})\|
 \le2MC_2r_\delta\le\frac12.
\]
The Neumann series for
\(D\mathcal F_\sigma(u_{\sigma,\delta})
 =L_\sigma+D\mathcal Q_\sigma(u_{\sigma,\delta})\)
proves \cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-inverse}.

Let \(R=\|u_{\sigma,\delta}\|\).  The graph--action scales in
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-rga-scales} and the exact speed remainder in
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-speed-quadratic-bound} give
\[
 |Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}(u_{\sigma,\delta})(r)|
 \le (R+CR^2)|r|
 \le D_\delta m_\delta(r),
 \qquad D_\delta\le Cr_\delta.
\]
Moreover
\(\Lambda_\delta\le C\lambda_\delta\), so
\(D_\delta\Lambda_\delta\le C\lambda_\delta^2\).
Choose the uniform \(\lambda_*\le\lambda_{\rm N}\) in the statement so
that this lies below the uniform speed-class threshold in
\cref{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart}.  The zero phase row and
inner anchor are exactly
\[
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl}
       (a_{\sigma,\delta})
 =Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}(u_{\sigma,\delta}),
 \qquad
 a_{\sigma,\delta}(e_{\sigma,*})=0.
\]
That lemma therefore gives
\cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-speed-sign}.  Finally,
\cref{thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual} identifies the assembled
zero with the asserted forward RFDE segment.  The ordered choice of
\(r_{\rm out}\) and \(\delta\) follows directly from
\(\lambda_\delta=r_{\rm out}+S_\delta^{-2}\).
This reconstruction is forward from the compatible collar only; it neither
extends that collar to a past RFDE orbit nor relates the two values of
\(\sigma\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{lem:joint-diagonal-wedge-kinetic}]
We first separate a genuine geometric restriction from an artificial
\(\varkappa^{-1}\) loss.  Write
\(\mathfrak w=\mathfrak w_{\delta,\varkappa}\).  On the wedge in
\cref{eq:joint-diagonal-admissible-wedge}, direct substitution gives
\begin{align}
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{R_\delta}
 &=\frac{\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta\sqrt{\mathfrak w}}{2\alpha},&
 \frac{\delta}{R_\delta}&=\sqrt{\delta\mathfrak w},&
 \frac{\delta}{R_\delta^2}&=\mathfrak w,\notag\\
 \frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta^3}
 &=\sqrt\delta\,\mathfrak w^{3/2},&
 \frac{\delta S_\delta}{R_\delta}
 &=\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta\sqrt{\mathfrak w},&
 \frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta}
 &=\delta^{3/2}\sqrt{\mathfrak w},               \label{eq:proof-joint-wedge-ratios}\\
 \frac{m_{r,T}}{R_\delta}
 &=1+\sqrt\delta\,\mathfrak w^{3/2}.\notag
\end{align}
The dimensionless combinations containing a negative power of \(R_\delta\)
in the retained component Green, terminal-observation, boundary-column,
terminal-source, and speed-row estimates are exhausted by
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\delta}{R_\delta^2},\quad
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{R_\delta},\quad
 \frac{\delta}{R_\delta},\quad
 \frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta^3},\quad
 \frac{\delta S_\delta}{R_\delta},\quad
 \frac{\varepsilon}{R_\delta},\quad
 \left|\frac{m_{r,T}}{R_\delta}-1\right|.        \label{eq:proof-joint-wedge-exhaustive-ratios}
\end{equation}
For traceability, the dependence used to upgrade each earlier fixed-
\(\varkappa\) estimate is listed step by step, with inherited dependencies
understood.  The diagonal fixed-base inverse uses
\(\rho_\delta/R_\delta\), \(\delta/R_\delta\), and
\(\delta/R_\delta^2\); see
\cref{eq:proof-diagonal-raw-scale-ratios}.  The terminal component Green
step additionally uses the annulus displacement ratios
\(\rho_\delta/R_\delta\), \(\delta/R_\delta\), and
\(\delta S_\delta/R_\delta\), the generation ratio
\(\varepsilon/R_\delta\), and
\(|m_{r,T}/R_\delta-1|\); see
\cref{eq:terminal-component-annuli,%
eq:terminal-component-generation-numbers,%
eq:terminal-component-flush-errors}.  The localized boundary columns add
\(\log(R_\delta/\rho_\delta)\), while the terminal-source extension adds no
new negative power beyond the terminal-weight ratio.  The linear speed-row
rank-one inverse uses
\(R_\delta^2+\varepsilon/R_\delta+\mathcal E_{r,T}\); the new nonlinear
speed-row remainder is the only step that additionally uses
\(\varepsilon/R_\delta^3\).  Finally, the raw-history recursion and hybrid
Schur step introduce no additional negative power of \(R_\delta\) or
\(\varkappa\).  These remaining dependencies occur explicitly in
\cref{eq:boundary-column-small-numbers,%
eq:terminal-source-small-factors,%
eq:speed-aligned-row-coefficient,%
eq:proof-fixed-envelope-speed-remainder-row}; positive powers of \(R_\delta\) are
bounded by the fixed \(\varkappa_0\).  Choose \(\mathfrak w_*\) below all
fixed Neumann and annulus thresholds in those estimates.  The first
quantity in \cref{eq:proof-joint-wedge-exhaustive-ratios} is then at most
\(\mathfrak w_*\), and all the remaining quantities tend to zero uniformly
by \cref{eq:proof-joint-wedge-ratios}.  On the attracting branch the same
listed estimates contain no negative power of \(\varkappa\).  Finally,
\[
 \log\frac{R_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\le C S_\delta^2
\]
on the same wedge.  Indeed, \(\varkappa\le\varkappa_0\) gives the upper
bound after using \(\log(1/\delta)=S_\delta^2/(2p)\), while the wedge lower
bound ensures \(R_\delta/\rho_\delta>1\) for small \(\delta\).
Thus the logarithm in \(\eta_{{\rm bd},\delta}\) introduces no hidden
joint loss.  Every Neumann threshold and constant in the cited steps depends
only on uniform upper bounds for the listed ratios, the fixed
\(\varkappa_0\), and the fixed model data, and not on the particular
\(\varkappa\).  The explicit hypotheses of the cited estimates are therefore
uniform on the wedge, and their Neumann and finite-generation sums prove the
asserted common linear bounds.

We record the two small-number calculations because they are used in the
nonlinear theorem.  Since
\[
 M_\delta=\varkappa^2\delta S_\delta^3
                  +\frac{2\alpha\delta}{S_\delta},
\]
one has the exact identity
\begin{align}
 \frac{\lambda_\delta M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 ={}&2\alpha\varkappa^2
 +2\alpha\varkappa^3\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta^{7/2}\notag\\
 &+4\alpha^2\varkappa\sqrt\delta\,S_\delta^{-1/2}
 +4\alpha^2S_\delta^{-4}.                       \label{eq:proof-joint-affine-prefix}
\end{align}
Moreover,
\begin{align*}
 \frac{M_\delta^2}{\rho_\delta}
 ={}&2\alpha\varkappa^4\delta S_\delta^5
   +8\alpha^2\varkappa^2\delta S_\delta
   +8\alpha^3\delta S_\delta^{-3},\\
 \frac{M_\delta E_\delta}{\rho_\delta}
 ={}&\left(2\alpha\varkappa^2S_\delta^2
              +4\alpha^2S_\delta^{-2}\right)
       \delta^{2-2\vartheta}.
\end{align*}
Thus every term in \(\eta_{\mathrm{hyb},\delta}\) contains only a
nonnegative power of \(\varkappa\) and tends to zero uniformly for
\(0<\varkappa\le\varkappa_0\).  The corrected speed projection uses
\[
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
 \le\frac{\delta S_\delta^3}{2\alpha},
\]
by \cref{eq:proof-speed-phase-uniform-ratio}, so the same is true of
\(\eta_{\mathrm{sp},\delta}\).  Combining
\cref{eq:proof-joint-affine-prefix} with the other three terms in
\cref{eq:affine-predictor-small-number} proves
\cref{eq:joint-diagonal-affine-budget}.  Young's inequality applied to
\(\varkappa\sqrt\delta S_\delta^{3/2}\), followed by a decrease of
\(\delta_*\), proves
\cref{eq:joint-diagonal-small-scales}.

It remains to prove the kinetic observation.  For the normal response
\(y\), the bulk estimates give the factor
\(\lambda_\delta B_\sigma(y)\) before the terminal recut and in the full
terminal trace.  The boundary and terminal-source estimates give,
respectively, \(D_\sigma(y)\) and \(T_\sigma(y)\).  The terminal-source
state/speed estimates control both its possible pre-support future-Green
tail and its terminal trace by \(T_\sigma(y)\).  The rank-one speed-row
replacement has the same bounds by
\cref{eq:speed-aligned-row-inverse}.  This proves
\cref{eq:piecewise-kinetic-bound} when \(a=0\).

Now suppose \(a(e_{\sigma,0})=0\).  The entry collar column in
\cref{lem:raw-phase-boundary-column-assembly} vanishes.  By
\cref{eq:source-split-hybrid-A1-embedding},
\[
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_\phi^1}
 \le C(\rho_\delta+M_\delta)
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 \le C\lambda_\delta
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}.
\]
The structured phase-state, speed, and terminal estimates therefore
bound \(T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\) in
\cref{eq:piecewise-kinetic-observable} by the last term of
\cref{eq:piecewise-kinetic-bound}.  The speed projection adds a boundary
column of size
\[
 C\frac{\rho_\delta}{\lambda_\delta}
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 \le C\lambda_\delta
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}},
\]
where the last inequality follows from
\(\delta S_\delta^5=o(1)\).  This proves the phase part, and linearity
gives the difference statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:fixed-envelope-speed-row-zero}]
We first verify the exact residual and its differential.  The history and
distributed components are the exact identities proved in
\cref{thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual}; multiplying those
distributed rows by the two complementary indicators changes neither
equation a.e. nor their sum.  On the repelling branch,
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-nonlinear-finite-source} gives the exact finite
row \(\varepsilon Q_r^{\rm nl,\flat}(e_{r,1})\).  Hence
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-exact-residual} is the exact fixed-envelope
boundary-value residual for the unmodified RFDE equations, not a Taylor
model.

At the origin its normal value is the negative of
\cref{eq:affine-normal-source-decomposition}; every other row vanishes.
For the non-speed normal rows, the proof of
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-normal-source-linearization} gives the
old phase column \(T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\).  The difference
\(T_r^{\rm sp}-T_r^{\rm rc}\) is a pure speed-row right inverse and has
zero history and distributed data.  Thus substitution of
\(x=T_\sigma^{\rm sp}a+y\) leaves precisely the corresponding rows of
\(\mathscr N_\sigma^{\rm sp,\infty}y\).  The speed row itself satisfies
\begin{align*}
 D\{\varepsilon Q_r^{\rm nl,\flat}(e_{r,1})\}(0)
       [T_r^{\rm sp}a+y,a]
 &=\varepsilon\{\mathfrak D_rx
             +\mathscr P_{c,r,N}a\}(e_{r,1})\\
 &=\mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}y
       +\varepsilon(\mathfrak A_r^{\rm sp}a)(e_{r,1})
 =\mathscr B_r^{\rm sp}y,
\end{align*}
by \cref{eq:speed-projected-phase-kernel}.  The phase, entry, and slack
rows linearize to
\[
 q_0^2a'-\mathfrak D_\sigma y
       -\mathfrak A_\sigma^{\rm sp}a,\qquad
 a(e_{\sigma,0}),\qquad a(e_{\sigma,1})+\tau.
\]
This proves both identities in
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-base-and-linearization}.

Put \(b=\mathfrak b_{\delta,\varkappa}\) for the rest of the proof.
We next justify the star inverse.  If the normal datum is decomposed as
\(f_{\rm B}+f_{\rm bd}+f_T\), the direct Green bounds and the sharper
oriented-prefix calculation give
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathfrak D_\sigma
       G_\sigma^{\rm sp,\infty}f\|_
          {\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 \le C\left\{
   \left(\lambda_\delta+
       \frac{\lambda_\delta M_\delta}{\rho_\delta}\right)
          \|f_{\rm B}\|
       +\|f_{\rm bd}\|+\|f_T\|\right\}.           \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-refined-action}
\end{equation}
The first coefficient is at most \(Cb\) by
\cref{eq:proof-joint-affine-prefix}; this is the refinement of the
coarser unit bulk-prefix bound needed here.  The phase Schur formula and
\(\lambda_\delta\le Cb\) now give
\[
 \frac{\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}}b
 \le C\left\{\|f_{\rm B}\|
   +\frac{\|f_{\rm bd}\|+\|f_T\|}{\lambda_\delta}
   +\frac{\|k\|_{\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}}b\right\}.
\]
The triangular last row has
\(\tau=b_E-a(e_{\sigma,1})\), while the causal range slice \(b_0=0\)
gives \(a(e_{\sigma,0})=0\).  The hybrid endpoint bound therefore gives
directly
\[
 \frac{|\tau|}{\rho_\delta b}
 \le \frac{|b_E|}{\rho_\delta b}
      +C\frac{\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}}b.
\]
Together with the unchanged transported normal coordinates, these
observations prove the asserted uniform restricted star inverse.

The affine bounds in
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-affine-box} follow from
\cref{eq:affine-source-summand-bounds,%
eq:affine-terminal-source-sharp-bound,%
eq:affine-predictor-phase-slack-bound,%
eq:joint-diagonal-affine-budget}; note that
\(R_\delta,\rho_\delta,r_{\sigma,T}\le C\lambda_\delta\).
Consequently, for
\(u=u_\sigma^{\rm aff}+h\) in a fixed star ball on the causal slice,
\begin{align}
 B_\sigma(y)&\le C,&
 D_\sigma(y)+T_\sigma(y)&\le C\lambda_\delta,&
 \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}&\le Cb,            \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-affine-tube}\\
 \mathfrak K_\sigma(x,a)&\le C\lambda_\delta.               \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-kinetic-tube}
\end{align}
For two such points the corresponding differences are bounded by
\begin{equation}
 B_\sigma(\Delta y)\le\|\Delta h\|_{\sigma,*},\quad
 D_\sigma(\Delta y)+T_\sigma(\Delta y)
       \le\lambda_\delta\|\Delta h\|_{\sigma,*},\quad
 \|\Delta a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
       \le b\|\Delta h\|_{\sigma,*}.             \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-star-differences}
\end{equation}
In particular,
\(\mathfrak K_\sigma(\Delta x,\Delta a)
\le C\lambda_\delta\|\Delta h\|_{\sigma,*}\).

We now prove the three nonlinear source estimates.  This is the point at
which the hard source split and the anisotropic norm are both essential.
The hybrid phase norm gives
\begin{equation}
 \|a\|_{C^0}\le\rho_\delta\|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}},
 \qquad
 |q_0^2a'|\le m_\delta
       \|a\|_{\mathsf A_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}.   \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-phase-scales}
\end{equation}
Thus a moving coefficient may be estimated by the stronger
\(C\rho_\delta b\), rather than by the rough \(C|r|b\).
Before the terminal interface,
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-kinetic-tube} gives
\begin{equation}
 \|Z_x\|_N\le C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta,\qquad
 |p_x^\sharp|\le C\varepsilon|r|\lambda_\delta,\qquad
 |Q_x^\sharp|\le C m_\delta(r)\lambda_\delta.     \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-state}
\end{equation}
The last estimate is not sharp enough for the bulk scalar-source norm near
the attracting endpoint.  The linear delay row and the first two bounds
give
\begin{equation}
 |D_{c,N}(x)|\le C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta,\qquad
 Q_x^\sharp=\varepsilon^{-1}
       (p_x^\sharp-\ell_0Z_x)+D_{c,N}(x),\qquad
 |Q_x^\sharp|\le C|r|\lambda_\delta,             \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-speed-sharpening}
\end{equation}
because \(\|\ell_0(r)\|\le C|r|\) and
\(\varepsilon\le\rho_\delta\le|r|\).  This algebraic sharpening is what
removes the otherwise divergent factor \(m_\delta(r)/|r|\).

Write
\[
 A_0=w_0'+\varepsilon d_0,\qquad
 A_a=w_a'+\varepsilon d_a.
\]
The hybrid phase bound, the causal embedding
\cref{eq:source-split-hybrid-A1-embedding}, and the coefficient jets give
\begin{align}
 |r_a-r|&\le C\rho_\delta b,\notag\\
 \|\Delta\mathcal C\|+\|\Delta z_0'\|
   +|\Delta w_0'|+\|\Delta\ell\|&\le C|r|b,
 &|\Delta d|&\le Cb,\notag\\
 |A_a|&\le C|r|,& |A_a-A_0|&\le C|r|b.           \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-coefficient-ledger}
\end{align}
Here \(\varepsilon|\Delta d|\le C|r|b\); this is why no false
\(O(|r|b)\) claim is made for \(\Delta d\) itself.  In addition,
\begin{align}
 |\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a|&\le
        C\frac{\delta\lambda_\delta}{|r|}b,
 &\|\mathcal R_{\Pi,\sigma,N}(a)\|_N
      &\le C\delta|r|b^2,\notag\\
 |h_{c,N}(r_a,Z_x)|+
   \|h_{\perp,N}(r_a,Z_x)\|_N
      &\le C\varepsilon^2\lambda_\delta^2.       \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-auxiliary-ledger}
\end{align}

After subtraction of the affine value and linear phase column, the
\(Z\)-source consists of the six combined classes
\begin{equation}
 \Delta\mathcal C\,Z_x,\quad
 \Delta z_0'\,p_x^\sharp,\quad
 \operatorname{Rem}R_{z,N},\quad
 z_a'h_{c,N}-h_{\perp,N},\quad
 \varepsilon\{\mathcal R_{\Pi,\perp,N}
       -z_a'\mathcal R_{\Pi,c,N}\},\quad
 \varepsilon\Delta z_0'D_{c,N}(x).              \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-Z-term-classes}
\end{equation}
The combination
\(-\Delta z_0'p_x-\varepsilon\Delta z_0'\mathscr P_{c,N}a
=-\Delta z_0'p_x^\sharp\) is kept exact; its two terms must not be
estimated separately.  Equations
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-affine-remainders,%
eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-state,%
eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-coefficient-ledger,%
eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-auxiliary-ledger}
bound these classes, respectively, by
\begin{equation}
 C\left\{
 \varepsilon\lambda_\delta|r|b,\,
 \varepsilon\lambda_\delta|r|^2b,\,
 \varepsilon|r|b^2,\,
 \varepsilon^2\lambda_\delta^2,\,
 \varepsilon\delta|r|b^2,\,
 \varepsilon^2|r|\lambda_\delta b
 \right\}.                                      \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-Z-class-sizes}
\end{equation}
Each is at most \(C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta b\) on the diagonal
wedge.

For the scalar source one should not estimate its original displayed
summands separately.  Put
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal R_{Q,\sigma}
 =Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}-Q_x^\sharp
 =-\varepsilon^{-1}
   \{(\ell_a-\ell_0)Z_x+h_{c,N}(r_a,Z_x)\}
       +\mathcal R_{\Pi,c,\sigma,N}(a).           \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-RQ-definition}
\end{equation}
Direct substitution into
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-p-source} gives the cancellation-safe
exact remainder identity
\begin{align}
 \operatorname{Rem}S_{p,\sigma}^{\rm nl}
={}&\varepsilon(A_a-A_0)Q_x^\sharp
   +\varepsilon A_a\mathcal R_{Q,\sigma}
   +\varepsilon^2\operatorname{Rem}_{a}
       \{q_0d_0\},                               \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-exact-p-remainder}
\end{align}
where
\[
 \operatorname{Rem}_{a}\{q_0d_0\}
 =(q_0d_0)(r_a)-(q_0d_0)(r)
       -q_0(r)(q_0d_0)'(r)a(r).
\]
The first two terms in
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-exact-p-remainder} are the exact regrouping
of the \(p_x\), \(D_{c,N}(x)\), phase-delay, and \(d\)-terms; no one of
those original displayed terms is estimated alone.  By
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-RQ-definition},
\begin{equation}
 |\mathcal R_{Q,\sigma}|
 \le C\{\rho_\delta\lambda_\delta b
       +\varepsilon\lambda_\delta^2
       +\delta|r|b^2\}.                          \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-speed-remainder}
\end{equation}
The weighted coefficient jets give
\(|\operatorname{Rem}_{a}\{q_0d_0\}|\le Cb^2\).
Consequently every term in
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-exact-p-remainder} is bounded by
\(C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta|r|^2b\); here the first term uses the
sharpened bulk bound
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-speed-sharpening}, not the coarser
\(m_\delta\)-weighted estimate.  For the remaining apparent losses one uses
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon\lambda_\delta\le C|r|b,\qquad
 \varepsilon b\le C\lambda_\delta|r|^2,\qquad
 \delta b\le C\lambda_\delta.                    \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-closing-scales}
\end{equation}
whose worst case is \(|r|=\rho_\delta\), where
\(\lambda_\delta\rho_\delta^2\ge c\varepsilon\).  More explicitly, at
that endpoint,
\[
 \frac{\varepsilon\lambda_\delta}{\rho_\delta b}
 \le C\frac{\delta}{S_\delta},\qquad
 \frac{\varepsilon b}{\lambda_\delta\rho_\delta^2}
 \le 4\alpha^2\frac{b}{\lambda_\delta S_\delta^2}
 \le Cb,
 \qquad
 \frac{\delta b}{\lambda_\delta}
 \le \delta S_\delta^2b.
\]
The right-hand sides are uniformly bounded, and the quantities needed as
small factors tend to zero in the order used above.
Thus the exact bulk source belongs to the bulk summand with norm \(Cb\).
Applying the same decompositions on the line segment between two inputs
replaces one factor \(b\) by
\(\|h-k\|_{\sigma,*}\) and proves
\begin{align}
 \|\mathbb Q_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\rm fe}
       (u_\sigma^{\rm aff})\|&\le Cb,\notag\\
 \|\mathbb Q_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\rm fe}
       (u_\sigma^{\rm aff}+h)
  -\mathbb Q_{\sigma,\rm B}^{\rm fe}
       (u_\sigma^{\rm aff}+k)\|
 &\le Cb\|h-k\|_{\sigma,*}.                      \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-remainder}
\end{align}

On the terminal annulus set
\(t=r_{\sigma,T}\) and \(m=m_{\sigma,T}\).  There
\(|r|\asymp t\), and
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-kinetic-tube} and the delay row give
\begin{align}
 \|Z_x\|_N&\le C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta,&
 |p_x|&\le C\varepsilon m\lambda_\delta,&
 |Q_x^\sharp|&\le Cm\lambda_\delta,\notag\\
 |p_x^\sharp|&\le
   C\varepsilon\{m\lambda_\delta+
          \delta\lambda_\delta b/t\},&
 |D_{c,N}(x)|&\le C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta,&
 |\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a|&\le
       C\frac{\delta\lambda_\delta}{t}b,&
 \|\mathcal R_{\Pi,\sigma,N}(a)\|_N
       &\le C\delta t b^2.                       \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-terminal-state-ledger}
\end{align}
The coefficient estimates are
\begin{equation}
 \|\Delta\mathcal C\|+\|\Delta z_0'\|
  +|\Delta w_0'|+\|\Delta\ell\|\le Ctb,\qquad
 |\Delta q_0|+|\Delta d|\le Cb.                 \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-terminal-coefficient-ledger}
\end{equation}
In particular, not every coefficient displacement is claimed to be
\(O(tb)\).  Nevertheless \(|A_a|\le Ct\) and
\(|A_a-A_0|\le Ctb\), because the \(d\)-displacement carries the factor
\(\varepsilon\le t\).

The six \(Z\)-source classes in
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-Z-term-classes}, now using
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-terminal-state-ledger}, are bounded by
\(C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta b\).  This includes
\(\operatorname{Rem}R_{z,N}=O(\varepsilon tb^2)\).  For the scalar row,
the cancellation-safe regrouping
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-exact-p-remainder} gives
\begin{equation}
 |\operatorname{Rem}S_{p,\sigma}^{\rm nl}|
 \le C\{\varepsilon tm\lambda_\delta b
              +\varepsilon^2b^2\}.              \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-terminal-p-ledger}
\end{equation}
The second term is absorbed by the first.  Indeed,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\varepsilon b}{tm\lambda_\delta}
 \le C
 \begin{cases}
  \delta bS_\delta^3,&\sigma=a,\\
  \delta\mathfrak w_{\delta,\varkappa}
       b/\lambda_\delta,&\sigma=r,
 \end{cases}                                     \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-terminal-closing-ratios}
\end{equation}
and both right-hand sides are uniformly bounded, and tend to zero after
the ordered choices in the theorem.  Thus the terminal \(Z\)- and
scalar-source sizes are
\[
 C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta b,\qquad
 C\varepsilon tm\lambda_\delta b,
\]
which are precisely \(C\lambda_\delta b\) in the terminal-source norm.
The same bilinear decompositions prove the two-point bounds.  This
calculation keeps \(m_{a,T}\gg\rho_\delta\); replacing \(m_{a,T}\) by
\(|r|\) on the attracting terminal annulus would be invalid.

On the causal slice the attracting history source has no nonlinear
remainder.  On the repelling branch the only variable collar datum is
\(p_x(0)\), of normalized size \(O(\lambda_\delta)\).
\Cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-C2-bounds} therefore gives
\(O(\lambda_\delta^2)\), hence \(O(\lambda_\delta b)\), in the boundary
norm.  Finally, the nonlinear speed-row remainder is
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon\{Q_r^{\rm nl,\flat}
       -(\mathfrak D_rx+\mathscr P_{c,r,N}a)\}(e_{r,1}).
                                                        \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-speed-remainder-row}
\end{equation}
The exact cancellation
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-speed-remainder}, the terminal kinetic
bounds, \(\rho_\delta/R_\delta\le1\), and
\(\varepsilon/R_\delta^3=o(1)\) bound this by
\(C\lambda_\delta b\) in the speed-row normalization.  The two-point
versions follow from the same \(C^2\) estimates.  We have proved
\begin{align}
 &\|\mathbb Q_{\sigma,\rm bd}^{\rm fe}
       (u_\sigma^{\rm aff})\|
 +\|\mathbb Q_{\sigma,T}^{\rm fe}
       (u_\sigma^{\rm aff})\|
 \le C\lambda_\delta b,\notag\\
 &\|\Delta\mathbb Q_{\sigma,\rm bd}^{\rm fe}\|
 +\|\Delta\mathbb Q_{\sigma,T}^{\rm fe}\|
 \le C\lambda_\delta b\|h-k\|_{\sigma,*}.        \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-boundary-terminal-remainder}
\end{align}
The support indicators give exact membership in the two distributed
summands; no density or cutoff argument is involved.

It remains to estimate the phase row.  The nonlinear phase-action
remainder is a coefficient displacement times \(a'\).  Using
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-phase-scales} first pointwise and then in
the oriented primitive gives \(Cb^2\) in
\(\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}\).  For the speed part use the exact
identity
\begin{equation}
 Q_\sigma^{\rm nl}-Q_x^\sharp
 =-\varepsilon^{-1}
   \{(\ell_a-\ell_0)Z_x+h_{c,N}(r_a,Z_x)\}
       +\mathcal R_{\Pi,c,\sigma,N}(a).           \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-speed-exact-cancellation}
\end{equation}
The pointwise term is \(O(b^2m_\delta)\).  In the prefix estimate one
must retain \(|a|\le\rho_\delta Cb\); integration then gives
\[
 \rho_\delta^{-1}
 \left\|\mathcal P_\sigma
       (Q_\sigma^{\rm nl}-Q_x^\sharp)\right\|_\infty
 \le Cb^2.
\]
The polynomial and phase-delay remainders are smaller.  For differences,
the total phase size is \(O(b)\), the phase difference is
\(O(b\|h-k\|_*)\), the total kinetic size is \(O(\lambda_\delta)\), and
the kinetic difference is
\(O(\lambda_\delta\|h-k\|_*)\).  Since
\(\lambda_\delta\le Cb\), the same calculation yields
\begin{equation}
 \|\Delta\mathbb Q_{\sigma,\phi}^{\rm fe}\|_
       {\mathsf H_{\sigma,\mathrm{hyb}}}
 \le Cb^2\|h-k\|_{\sigma,*}.                     \label{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-phase-remainder}
\end{equation}
The entry and slack rows are linear, so their remainders vanish.
All coefficient evaluations remain in the fixed same-sign phase collar.
The scalar phase flow and polynomial source maps are \(C^1\) there, the
raw-compatible history assembler is \(C^2\) by
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-C2-bounds}, and multiplication by the two
fixed support indicators is bounded on the piecewise-\(L^\infty\) source
spaces.  Hence the exact formulas define a \(C^1\) map on the affine star
tube.  Applying the preceding termwise ledgers to directional
derivatives, or equivalently taking difference quotients on line segments,
proves \cref{eq:fixed-envelope-star-derivative}; it also proves
three-summand membership rather than merely a pointwise bound.
\Cref{eq:proof-fixed-envelope-bulk-remainder,%
eq:proof-fixed-envelope-boundary-terminal-remainder,%
eq:proof-fixed-envelope-phase-remainder}
prove
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-star-two-point,%
eq:fixed-envelope-remainder-components}.

Let \(M_*\) be the joint star-inverse bound and define
\[
\mathcal T_\sigma(h)
=-(\mathbb L_\sigma^{\rm aff})^{-1}
