       \mathbb Q_\sigma^{\rm fe}(u_\sigma^{\rm aff}+h).
\]
Choose \(K>2M_*C_Q\), and choose the \(\varkappa_*\) in the theorem so
that the \(\varkappa^2\) part of \(b\) lies below the phase-chart and
contraction thresholds.  For fixed
\(0<\varkappa_0\le\varkappa_*\), decrease \(\mathfrak w_*\) to the linear
wedge thresholds and then \(\delta_*\).  The number \(b\) is then so small
that
\(\mathcal T_\sigma\) maps the closed star ball
\(\|h\|_{\sigma,*}\le Kb\) into itself and has Lipschitz constant at
most \(1/2\).  Its fixed point gives
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-nonlinear-zero,%
eq:fixed-envelope-zero-star-radius}.  Applying the three separate output
bounds before the star norm proves
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-zero-component-correction}.  On the causal
tangent slice and corresponding range slice, the derivative is a Neumann
perturbation of the restricted \(\mathbb L_\sigma^{\rm aff}\), so it remains
an isomorphism.  The same contraction proves uniqueness in the displayed
affine causal ball.

At the zero, the exact history and distributed rows assemble the same
unmodified forward RFDE segment as in the last part of the proof of
\cref{thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual}.  The phase row and
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-row-identity} give
\[
 q_0(\Theta_a(r))+Q_\sigma^{\rm nl,\flat}(x,a)(r)
 =q_0(r)\Theta_a'(r).
\]
The affine bound and
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-zero-component-correction} put \(a\) in the
uniform same-sign phase-chart ball.  Hence \(\Theta_a'>c_*>0\), proving
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-zero-speed-sign}.  The repelling finite row gives
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-terminal-speed-correction-zero}, and the last
triangular row gives \cref{eq:fixed-envelope-slack-mismatch}.  Since the
physical terminal coordinate in the fixed envelope is
\(\Phi_{q_0}^{-\tau}(e_{\sigma,1})\) and is not shown to equal
\(e_{\sigma,1}\), none of the original-endpoint consequences can be
inferred.  In particular, the zero uses the canonical
zero-core raw-compatible old-history assembler, does not supply a past
orbit, and gives no identity \(\tau=\delta\Delta S\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:fixed-envelope-terminal-slow-jet}]
Write \(r=r_\sigma^{\rm fe}\), \(Z=Z_\sigma^{\rm fe}\), and
\(p=p_\sigma^{\rm fe}\), and suppress \(N,\sigma\) where no ambiguity can
result.  The hard source interface used to define the three-summand range
in \cref{thm:fixed-envelope-speed-row-zero} is not an interface in the
physical equation: the two indicator pieces add to the original smooth
source.  The order-\(h\) compatible entry history and the method of steps
therefore make the reconstructed segment \(C^2\) on every terminal window
in \cref{eq:terminal-slow-jet-windows}.  We now prove the uniform estimates;
ordinary eventual smoothing alone would not give their scale.

Put
\begin{align*}
 \Delta&=\Delta\mathcal D_N^{\rm ft}[r,Z],
 &D&=p-\ell_N(r)Z-h_{c,N}(r,Z),\\
 d&=(\mathcal D_{0,c,N}-q_0)',
 &a_p&=w_{0,N}'(r)+\varepsilon d(r).
\end{align*}
Using the speed reconstruction \(Q=\varepsilon^{-1}D+\Delta_c\), the
second equation in
\cref{eq:fold-time-tracker-Z,eq:fold-time-tracker-p} has the exact
cancellation-safe form
\begin{equation}
 \delta p_s=a_p D+\varepsilon a_p\Delta_c
                 +\varepsilon^2q_0(r)d(r).       \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-p-eliminated}
\end{equation}
This identity is the reason that no estimate of \(Q_s/\varepsilon\) will
be used.

Set
\[
 u=r_s,\qquad U=Z_s,\qquad V=p_s,\qquad
 r_k=r(s-\theta_k),\qquad u_k=u(s-\theta_k).
\]
Separate the derivative of the fixed-delay defect as
\begin{align}
 \partial_s\Delta
 &=\mathscr L_{\Delta,s}U+g_\Delta,\notag\\
 \mathscr L_{\Delta,s}U
 &=K\sum_{k=0}^mL_{k,N}(\eta)
       \{U(s)-U(s-\theta_k)\},\notag\\
 g_\Delta
 &=K\sum_{k=0}^mL_{k,N}(\eta)
   \bigl[(u-u_k)\mathbf1+z_{0,N}'(r)u
                    -z_{0,N}'(r_k)u_k\bigr]
       -\mathcal D_{0,N}'(r)u.                  \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-delay-split}
\end{align}
Differentiating the two exact tracker equations once and retaining every
term containing \(U,V\), or a delayed \(U\), in the operator gives
\begin{align}
 \delta U_s={}&\mathcal C_NU-z_{0,N}'V
 +(z_{0,N}'D_Zh_{c,N}-D_Zh_{\perp,N})U\notag\\
 &+\varepsilon\{(\mathscr L_{\Delta,s}U)_\perp
        -z_{0,N}'(\mathscr L_{\Delta,s}U)_c\}+F_1,
                                                        \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-Z-system}\\
 \delta V_s={}&a_pV-a_p(\ell_N+D_Zh_{c,N})U
       +\varepsilon a_p(\mathscr L_{\Delta,s}U)_c+H_1,
                                                        \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-p-system}
\end{align}
where all coefficients and nonlinear derivatives are evaluated at
\((r(s),Z(s))\), and
\begin{align}
 F_1={}&\mathcal C_N'(r)uZ-z_{0,N}''(r)up-R_{z,N}'(r)u
       +z_{0,N}''(r)u h_{c,N}\notag\\
 &+z_{0,N}'(r)\partial_rh_{c,N}u
       -\partial_rh_{\perp,N}u\notag\\
 &+\varepsilon\{(g_\Delta)_\perp-z_{0,N}'(r)(g_\Delta)_c
             -z_{0,N}''(r)u\Delta_c\},          \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-F1}\\
 H_1={}&a_p'(r)uD
       -a_p\{\ell_N'(r)uZ+\partial_rh_{c,N}u\}
       +\varepsilon a_p'(r)u\Delta_c\notag\\
 &+\varepsilon a_p(g_\Delta)_c
       +\varepsilon^2(q_0d)'(r)u.                \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-H1}
\end{align}
Thus \cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-Z-system,eq:proof-terminal-jet-p-system}
have exactly the current-loop plus strict-positive-delay architecture of
\cref{eq:current-absorbed-delay-equation}.  In particular, the potentially
singular \(Q_s\) has not been declared to be an external source.

We record the direct source ledger.  The terminal kinetic bounds in the
proof of \cref{thm:fixed-envelope-speed-row-zero}, the phase orientation
margin, and the fixed-delay difference formula give, on the one-delay
enlargement of the terminal buffer,
\begin{align}
 |r|&\asymp r_{\sigma,T},& |u|&\le C\delta,\notag\\
 \|Z\|_N&\le C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta,&
 |p|+|D|&\le C\varepsilon m_{\sigma,T}\lambda_\delta,\notag\\
 \|\Delta\|_N+\|g_\Delta\|_N&\le C\delta.       \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-base-ledger}
\end{align}
The coefficient jets retained in the theorem imply
\begin{align}
 |a_p|+\|z_{0,N}'\|_N+\|\ell_N\|
 &\le Cr_{\sigma,T},\notag\\
 |a_p'|+\|z_{0,N}''\|_N+\|\ell_N'\|
       +\|\mathcal C_N'\|&\le C,\notag\\
 |(q_0d)'|&\le C/r_{\sigma,T},&
 \|R_{z,N}'\|_N&\le C\varepsilon
       (1+\delta/r_{\sigma,T}),\notag\\
 |h_{c,N}|+\|h_{\perp,N}\|_N
       +|\partial_rh_{c,N}|+\|\partial_rh_{\perp,N}\|_N
 &\le C\|Z\|_N^2
       \le C\varepsilon^2\lambda_\delta^2,\notag\\
 \|D_Zh_{c,N}\|+\|D_Zh_{\perp,N}\|
 &\le C\varepsilon\lambda_\delta.              \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-coefficient-ledger}
\end{align}
Substitution in
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-F1,eq:proof-terminal-jet-H1} yields
\begin{align}
 \|F_1\|_N&\le C\varepsilon\delta,\notag\\
 |H_1|&\le C\left\{
  \varepsilon\delta m_{\sigma,T}\lambda_\delta
 +\varepsilon\delta r_{\sigma,T}\lambda_\delta
 +\varepsilon\delta^2+\varepsilon\delta r_{\sigma,T}
 +\frac{\varepsilon^2\delta}{r_{\sigma,T}}\right\}\notag\\
 &\le Cr_{\sigma,T}m_{\sigma,T}
             \frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta}.     \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-source-ledger}
\end{align}
For completeness, on the attracting terminal annulus the ratios needed in
the last inequality reduce to
\[
 \lambda_\delta,\quad
 \frac{\rho_\delta\lambda_\delta}{m_{a,T}},\quad
 \frac\delta{m_{a,T}},\quad
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{m_{a,T}},\quad
 \frac\varepsilon{\rho_\delta m_{a,T}},
\]
and on the repelling annulus to
\[
 \frac{\lambda_\delta\rho_\delta}{R_\delta},\quad
 \frac{\delta\rho_\delta}{R_\delta^2},\quad
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{R_\delta},\quad
 \frac{\varepsilon\rho_\delta}{R_\delta^3}.
\]
They are uniformly bounded, and in fact tend to zero in the order fixed by
the admissible wedge.  This verifies the direct component norm; it is not
an appeal to a generic gain of one slow derivative.

The operator terms in
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-Z-system,eq:proof-terminal-jet-p-system}
satisfy the same component ledger as
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-component-ledger}:
\begin{align}
 \|\mathbb A_Z-A_N\|&\le Cr_{\sigma,T},&
 \|C_{Zp}\|&\le Cr_{\sigma,T},&
 \|C_{pZ}\|&\le Cr_{\sigma,T}^2,\notag\\
 \sum_k\|B_k^Z\|&\le C\varepsilon,&
 \sum_k|B_k^p|&\le C\varepsilon r_{\sigma,T}.   \label{eq:proof-terminal-tangent-operator-ledger}
\end{align}
Indeed, the new \(D_Zh\)-terms are
\(O(\varepsilon\lambda_\delta)\), and all \(U,V\)-dependent pieces of
\(\partial_s\Delta\) were retained in the operator.  The current-loop inverse
and strict-delay generation proof of
\cref{prop:diagonal-terminal-component-green} therefore applies verbatim
after a uniform Neumann perturbation.

It remains to control the roots of the differentiated component words.
On the delay window immediately preceding the terminal recut, the bulk
kinetic estimate has the same bounds as
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-base-ledger}.  Moreover,
\[
 D=\varepsilon(Q-\Delta_c),\qquad
 p=D+\ell_NZ+h_{c,N},\qquad
 \delta\le C m_{\sigma,T}\lambda_\delta.
\]
Substitution in the two exact tracker equations gives, on that whole
window,
\[
 \|Z_s\|_N+\frac{|p_s|}{m_{\sigma,T}}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\lambda_\delta}{\delta}.
\]
This uses the physical \(p\), not the shifted bookkeeping coordinate
\(p^\sharp\).  It supplies both the derivative of the left \(Z\)-history
and, on the attracting branch, the left scalar value.  Together with the
terminal \(\mathsf T^1\) estimate it gives the incoming tangent bound
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal I_{\sigma,T}^{(1)}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\lambda_\delta}{\delta},             \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-incoming-data}
\end{equation}
where \(\mathcal I_{a,T}^{(1)}\) contains both the left \(U\)-history and
the left scalar value \(V/m_{a,T}\), while
\(\mathcal I_{r,T}^{(1)}\) contains the left \(U\)-history.  On the
repelling branch the scalar datum is instead selected from the future.
The exact identity
\begin{equation}
 \delta p_s=\varepsilon w_{0,N}'(r)Q
                  +\varepsilon^2q\,d(r)          \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-exact-speed-row}
\end{equation}
follows directly from
\cref{eq:fold-time-tracker-p,eq:fold-time-speed-reconstruction}.
At \(s=S_r\),
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-terminal-speed-correction-zero} gives \(Q=0\),
and hence \(q=q_0(r)\).  Since
\(R_{q,N}=\varepsilon q_0d\) and \(\varepsilon=\delta^2\),
\cref{eq:terminal-tangent-future-row} follows and
\begin{equation}
 \frac{|V(S_r)|}{m_{r,T}}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{R_\delta}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta}.    \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-future-size}
\end{equation}

To invoke the earlier Green estimates without changing their hypotheses,
split the tangent solution linearly as
\[
 (U,V)=X_{\rm src}+X_{\rm ter}+X_{\rm in}.
\]
Here \(X_{\rm src}\) has data \((F_1,H_1)\) and zero finite roots;
\(X_{\rm ter}\) is absent on the attracting branch and, on the repelling
branch, has only the future scalar datum \(V(S_r)\); and
\(X_{\rm in}\) has the incoming tangent roots and zero distributed source
and zero repelling future row.  The small perturbations in
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-tangent-operator-ledger} preserve the forward
transverse rate, the attracting forward scalar rate, and the repelling
future scalar rate used in
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-fast-kernel,%
eq:proof-terminal-attracting-scalar,%
eq:proof-terminal-repelling-scalar}.  The direct source estimate and
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-source-ledger} give
\[
 \sup\left\{\|(X_{\rm src})_Z\|_N
       +\frac{|(X_{\rm src})_p|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}
 \le C\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta},
\]
and the future scalar lift together with
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-future-size} gives the same bound for
\(X_{\rm ter}\).  Only \(X_{\rm in}\) is subjected to the rough-root
flushing factor.

Repeat the \(M\)-generation expansion in the proof of
\cref{eq:terminal-component-rough-flush} for \(X_{\rm in}\).  The
extra one-delay enlargement in
\cref{eq:terminal-slow-jet-windows} leaves the current travel length
\(L_0+1-L\ge L_0\) after all direct-history and strict-delay edges have
been removed.  Boundary-face words have the same root and edge count.
Together with
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-source-ledger,%
eq:proof-terminal-jet-incoming-data,eq:proof-terminal-jet-future-size},
this gives
\begin{align}
 \sup_{\widetilde J_{\sigma,L}}
 \left\{\|U\|_N+\frac{|V|}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right\}
 \le C\left\{
   \frac{\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta}
   +\mathcal E_{\sigma,T}^{\rm jet}(\delta)
       \frac{\varepsilon\lambda_\delta}{\delta}\right\},
                                                        \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-flushed-tangent}\\
 \mathcal E_{\sigma,T}^{\rm jet}(\delta)
 =\exp\{-cr_{\sigma,T}L_0/\delta\}
   +\left(\frac{C\varepsilon}{m_{\sigma,T}}\right)^M.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-flush-error}
\end{align}
The only scale still to check is
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal E_{\sigma,T}^{\rm jet}(\delta)
       \frac{\lambda_\delta\rho_\delta}{\delta^2}
 \longrightarrow0.                              \label{eq:proof-terminal-jet-final-scale}
\end{equation}
Now
\[
 \frac{\lambda_\delta\rho_\delta}{\delta^2}
 \le C\left\{\frac1{\delta S_\delta}
       +\varkappa_0\delta^{-1/2}S_\delta^{5/2}\right\}.
\]
On the attracting branch the exponential in
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-flush-error} is a sufficiently high power of
\(\delta\) after \(A\ge A_{\rm jet}\) is fixed, and
\(\varepsilon/m_{a,T}=O(\delta^2S_\delta^2)\).  On the repelling branch
the wedge gives \(R_\delta S_\delta/\delta\to\infty\) faster than every
logarithmic power and
\(\varepsilon/m_{r,T}=O(\delta^{3/2}S_\delta^{-3/2}/\varkappa)\);
the lower bound on \(\varkappa\) implicit in
\cref{eq:joint-diagonal-admissible-wedge} makes its \(M\)-th power
uniformly sufficient for \(M\ge2\).  This proves
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-final-scale}.

The first term of \cref{eq:terminal-tangent-component-bound} now follows
from \cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-flushed-tangent}.  On the smaller window
\(J_{\sigma,L}\), the delayed values needed by
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-Z-system,eq:proof-terminal-jet-p-system}
remain in \(\widetilde J_{\sigma,L}\).  Those equations,
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-jet-source-ledger}, and
\cref{eq:proof-terminal-tangent-operator-ledger} therefore give the
second-derivative part directly, proving
\cref{eq:terminal-tangent-component-bound,eq:fixed-envelope-terminal-slow-jet}.
Finally, the definition in
\cref{eq:fixed-envelope-formal-history-column} and the norm in
\cref{eq:terminal-voltage-interface-norm} give
\[
 \frac{\rho_\delta}{\varepsilon}
 \left\{\frac1\delta
       \frac{C\varepsilon\delta}{\rho_\delta}
       +\frac{C\varepsilon}{\rho_\delta}\right\}\le C,
\]
which is \cref{eq:fixed-envelope-history-shear-bound}.  Under the additional
selector hypotheses stated in the theorem, this formal column is exactly
the \(K_{Z,L}\) of
\cref{eq:endpoint-selector-event-shear}; no such right envelope is inferred
here.  The raw full state
retains the collective time tangent and is therefore outside the normal
history space used in this calculation.  The stated scope restrictions
follow.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:raw-gauge-fixed-section-zero}]
We use one norm symbol for the branch graph space and write
\(G=\|g_\sigma\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}\).
The normalization in \cref{eq:raw-gauge-norms} gives
\begin{align}
 \|(\beta_a,b_a,\widehat\gamma_a)\|_
      {\mathrm{col},r_{\rm out}}
 &\le C\varepsilon G,\notag\\
 \|\beta_r\|_N+
   \|\widehat\gamma_r\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
 &\le\varepsilon G.                              \label{eq:proof-raw-gauge-data-scales}
\end{align}
For a trial \(u=(x,a)\) in a fixed small graph ball,
\cref{eq:relative-graph-action-norm} and
\(\mathscr P_{c,r,N}a(0)=0\) give
\begin{equation}
 \frac{|p_x(0)|}{\rho_\delta}
 \le C\varepsilon\|u\|,
 \qquad
 \frac{|c_\sigma(a)|}{|e_{\sigma,0}|}
 \le\|a\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}
 \le\|u\|.                                      \label{eq:proof-raw-gauge-trial-scales}
\end{equation}
Consequently all arguments of the two history assemblers remain in the
single fixed-reference balls of
\cref{lem:second-jet-raw-history-assembler} after the common graph and
gauge radii are decreased.

The only change from the residual in
\cref{thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual} occurs in its old-history
and finite scalar boundary data.  The first differential of that change at
\((u,g)=(0,0)\) is \(-\mathscr J_\sigma g\): the minus sign comes from
\(\mathscr N_\sigma x-\mathscr S_{\sigma,g}^{\rm nl}\).  On the repelling
branch the \(p_x(0)\)-differential of the history assembler is exactly
\(\mathfrak C_e\) at the base and is canceled by the second term of
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-history-rows}.  Thus no new linear feedback is added to
\(L_r\).  The two maps in
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-linear-injections} are bounded from the normalized gauge
spaces to the normalized residual space, uniformly in \(N\) and \(\delta\).

More explicitly, define the joint remainder
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal R_{\sigma}^{g}(u)
 =\mathcal F_\sigma^g(u)-\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)
       -L_\sigma u+\mathscr J_\sigma g.           \label{eq:proof-raw-gauge-joint-remainder}
\end{equation}
The two-point estimates in the proof of
\cref{thm:fixed-section-nonlinear-graph-residual}, together with
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-C2-bounds,%
eq:nonlinear-raw-history-graph-remainder,%
eq:proof-raw-gauge-data-scales,%
eq:proof-raw-gauge-trial-scales}, give
\begin{align}
 &\|\mathcal R_\sigma^g(u)-
          \mathcal R_\sigma^{\widetilde g}(v)\|\notag\\
 &\quad\le C_G\bigl(\|u\|+\|v\|+
             \|g\|+\|\widetilde g\|\bigr)
       \bigl(\|u-v\|+\|g-\widetilde g\|\bigr) . \label{eq:proof-raw-gauge-joint-two-point}
\end{align}
Indeed, the pure \(u\)-terms are exactly the old two-point ledger.  The
new pure-gauge and mixed terms are the \(D_U^2\) and
\(\partial_cD_U\) terms of the history assembler.  Their physical input
size is \(O(\varepsilon G)\), and division by the normal-data factor
\(\varepsilon\) leaves the displayed dimensionless bound.  The repelling
dependence on \(p_x(0)\) is covered by the same mixed estimate.  No other
bulk or phase equation depends explicitly on \(g\).

Put \(b_\sigma^0=\mathcal F_\sigma(0,0)\).  For a constant \(C_0\) larger
than the base and gauge-injection bounds, set
\begin{equation}
 d_g=\lambda_\delta+G,
 \qquad r_g=2MC_0d_g.                             \label{eq:proof-raw-gauge-radius}
\end{equation}
On the closed \(r_g\)-ball consider
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal T_{\sigma,g}(u)
 =-L_\sigma^{-1}\left\{
    b_\sigma^0-\mathscr J_\sigma g
          +\mathcal R_\sigma^g(u)\right\}.       \label{eq:proof-raw-gauge-contraction}
\end{equation}
Choose
\(R_{\rm G}\le R_{\rm u}\) so that
\(4MC_GR_{\rm G}<1\), and then choose
\(g_*\) and \(\lambda_g\) so that
\(r_g<R_{\rm G}\) and
\(MC_G(r_g+G)<1/4\).
Equations
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-base-bound,%
eq:proof-raw-gauge-joint-two-point}
show that \(\mathcal T_{\sigma,g}\) maps the \(r_g\)-ball strictly into
itself and has Lipschitz constant at most \(1/2\).  Its fixed point is the
zero in \cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero} and proves
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-size}.  If two zeros lie in the open
\(R_{\rm G}\)-ball, the same two-point estimate with fixed \(g\) and the
choice of \(R_{\rm G},g_*\) forces their difference to vanish.  At the
fixed point, \cref{eq:proof-raw-gauge-joint-remainder} gives
\begin{equation*}
 u_{\sigma,\delta}^g+L_\sigma^{-1}b_\sigma^0
       -L_\sigma^{-1}\mathscr J_\sigma g
 =-L_\sigma^{-1}\mathcal R_\sigma^g
       (u_{\sigma,\delta}^g),
\end{equation*}
and \cref{eq:proof-raw-gauge-joint-two-point} with
\((v,\widetilde g)=(0,0)\) proves
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-first-jet}.

Differentiating the joint two-point estimate gives
\begin{equation}
 \|D_u\mathcal F_\sigma^g(u)-L_\sigma\|
 \le C_G(\|u\|+\|g\|).                           \label{eq:proof-raw-gauge-derivative-perturbation}
\end{equation}
After the same decrease of the radii, the Neumann series and
\(\|L_\sigma^{-1}\|\le M\) prove
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-inverse}.  The joint residual is \(C^1\) because
the history assembler is \(C^2\) and all other components have the
regularity already proved for \(\mathcal F_\sigma\).  The Banach implicit
function theorem therefore gives \cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-derivative}; the
uniform bound follows from \cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-inverse} and the
bounded gauge differential.  Integration along the line segment between
two gauges proves \cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-lipschitz}.  At zero gauge the
new residual equals the old one, so local uniqueness identifies its zero
with \cref{cor:fixed-section-canonical-zero}.

It remains to verify that the zero is a raw RFDE segment rather than only a
zero of an augmented equation.  On the attracting branch the first two
normal boundary rows give
\(\gamma_x=\mathscr H_{a,g_a}^{\rm raw}\) and
\(p_x(0)=b_a\); the seam condition and the current trace of the history
assembler give \(Z_x(0)=\beta_a\).  On the repelling branch, cancellation
of the identical \(\mathfrak C_ep_x(0)\) terms gives
\(\gamma_x=\mathscr H_{r,g_r}^{\rm raw}\), while the future row gives
\(p_x(S_r)=b_r\) and the seam gives \(Z_x(0)=\beta_r\).  These are
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-exact-boundary-identities}.  The exact delay splitting
and old collective/transverse decomposition in
\cref{eq:fixed-section-exact-delay-splitting,%
eq:fixed-section-physical-old-decomposition}
are algebraic and remain valid with these nonzero raw data.  The order-
\(h\) compatibility recursion then joins the old and current pieces, so
\cref{prop:fold-time-tracker-normal-form} reconstructs the unmodified raw
RFDE on the forward interval.

Finally, \cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-size} replaces the canonical size
\(O(\lambda_\delta)\) in the speed argument by
\(O(\lambda_\delta+G)\).  Decreasing the common gauge radius makes the
speed-class product in
\cref{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart} uniformly small.  The same
exact phase row therefore proves
\cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-speed-sign} for every gauge zero.  This also
shows directly that the raw-gauge ball is nonempty: it contains the zero
gauge and its canonical solution.  No step evolved an arbitrary history
backward or compared two gauges at a physical section.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{cor:generated-interior-canonical-tracker}]
The order-\(h\) raw compatibility at \(s=0\), with \(h\ge3\), and the
smooth finite-dimensional current field give a classical forward solution.
Independently of the extra seam compatibility, eventual smoothing by the
method of steps gives the \(C^2\) regularity required below at every positive
time beyond one maximal delay.  Because \(s_0-\theta_m>\theta_m\), this
includes the whole closed interval
\([s_0-\theta_m,s_1]\).  By
\cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-speed-sign},
\begin{equation}
 r_\sigma'(s)
 =\delta q_0(\bar r_\sigma(s))
       \Theta_{a_{\sigma,\delta}}'(\bar r_\sigma(s))<0.
                                                               \label{eq:proof-generated-interior-speed}
\end{equation}
The uniform bounds for \(q_0\) and
\cref{eq:anchored-phase-orientation} give
\cref{eq:generated-interior-q-bounds} and the asserted inverse
\(s_\sigma\).  The branch traverses a reduced-coordinate distance bounded
below independently of \(\delta\), while
\(|r_\sigma'|\le C\delta\).  Hence
\(S_\sigma\ge c/\delta>2\theta_m\) after one further decrease of
\(\delta_0\).

For \(r=r_\sigma(s)\) and
\(-\theta_m\le\theta\le0\), uniqueness for the scalar equation
\(r_s=\delta q_\sigma(r)\) gives
\[
 r_\sigma(s+\theta)
 =\Phi_{q_\sigma}^{\delta\theta}(r).
\]
This proves
\cref{eq:generated-interior-backtracks,eq:generated-interior-history-window}.
Every point of \(I_\sigma^{\rm gen}\), together with its full delayed
window, lies in the generated part of the same exact RFDE solution.
Autonomy therefore lets any such point serve as the initial point of the
restricted orbit.  Condition~\textup{(i)} of
\cref{prop:exact-outer-history-equation} holds on every retained subsegment;
its equivalence gives the two exact uncut invariance equations.

The resource equation, which holds at every current time in the exact
forward segment, gives on all of \(J_\sigma^{\rm gen}\)
\[
 W_\sigma'(r)=\frac{r-\varepsilon\nu}{q_\sigma(r)}.
\]
The relative phase-chart bound keeps \(|r|\) uniformly comparable with
\(|\bar r_\sigma|\ge\rho_\delta\).  Since
\(\varepsilon/\rho_\delta=O(\delta/S_\delta)\), decreasing \(\delta_0\)
once more makes \(|r-\varepsilon\nu|\) uniformly comparable with \(|r|\).
Together with \cref{eq:generated-interior-q-bounds} this proves
\cref{eq:generated-interior-wprime-bounds}.

The exact normal \(Z\)-row
\cref{eq:fold-time-tracker-Z}, the graph--action scales in
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-section-rga-scales}, the relative phase-delay bounds
\cref{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile,%
eq:nonlinear-phase-delay-quadratic-bound}, the coefficient and quadratic
bounds, the exact delay splitting, and the affine-residual bounds give
\[
 \|\delta\partial_sZ_x\|_N\le C\varepsilon
 \qquad\hbox{on the whole current branch}.
\]
Here the constant is uniform because the zero lies in the fixed
\(R_{\rm u}\)-ball, and \(\varepsilon=\delta^2\).
Since \(|r_\sigma'|\ge c\delta\), differentiating
\[
 V_\sigma(r_\sigma(s))
 =\mathbf1+r_\sigma(s)\mathbf1
       +z_{0,N}(r_\sigma(s))+Z_x(s)
\]
proves \cref{eq:generated-interior-Vprime-bound}.  The same relative
phase estimate gives \(|r|\ge c_\rho\rho_\delta\) throughout
\(J_\sigma^{\rm gen}\).

Thus the solution is compatible and \(C^2\) on the whole delay collar,
\(r_\sigma'\ne0\), \(q_\sigma\) and its reciprocal are uniformly bounded,
\cref{eq:generated-interior-Vprime-bound} holds, and
\(|W_\sigma'|\asymp|r|\) there.  Hence
\cref{prop:physical-history-phase-quotient,prop:exact-resource-gauge-quotient}
apply.  Finally,
\(\delta/\rho_\delta=2\alpha/S_\delta\), so
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-volterra-bound,eq:resource-gauge-volterra-inverse}
give \cref{eq:generated-interior-volterra-bound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:raw-gauge-physical-hit}]
Because \(\rho_\delta<r_{\rm out}/4\) and
\(0<\kappa_h<1/8\), all four values on the right of
\cref{eq:physical-hit-reference-bracket} lie strictly inside the
corresponding reduced branch.  The reduced paths are strictly decreasing,
so the two bracket times exist and have the asserted order.  The temporal
distance of either time from every branch endpoint is at least
\(c r_{\rm out}/\delta\).  Hence,
after decreasing \(\delta_0\),
\cref{eq:physical-hit-common-buffer} holds.  In particular, the choice
\(s_0=T_{\sigma,-}\), \(s_1=T_{\sigma,+}\) is admissible in
\cref{cor:generated-interior-canonical-tracker} for every gauge zero once
the common gauge ball below has been fixed.

Write \(u_g=(x_g,a_g)\) for that zero.  The uniform derivative estimate in
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-derivative} and the relative phase part of the graph
norm give
\begin{equation}
 \|D_ga_g k\|_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}
 \le C\|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.       \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-phase-derivative}
\end{equation}
Since
\(r_{\sigma,g}(s)=\Theta_{a_g}(\bar r_\sigma(s))\),
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-chart-a-derivative} gives the exact identity
\begin{equation}
 D_gr_{\sigma,g}(s)k
 =q_0(r_{\sigma,g}(s))
      (D_ga_gk)(\bar r_\sigma(s)).                 \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-r-derivative}
\end{equation}
On the common buffer, \(|\bar r_\sigma|\asymp r_h\).  Thus
\cref{eq:proof-physical-hit-phase-derivative} and the coefficient bounds
imply
\begin{equation}
 \sup_s|D_gr_{\sigma,g}(s)k|
 \le Cr_h\|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.   \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-r-bound}
\end{equation}

The current-path part of the graph norm in
\cref{eq:relative-graph-action-norm}, together with
\(p_g=p_{x_g,a_g}^\sharp
       -\varepsilon\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a_g\) and
\cref{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile}, gives on the same buffer
\begin{equation}
 \sup_s\left\{
   \|D_gZ_g(s)k\|_N+|D_gp_g(s)k|\right\}
 \le C\|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.     \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-normal-bound}
\end{equation}
Here we use only the ordinary unscaled range norm; the graph estimate is
stronger, with factors \(\varepsilon\) and
\(\varepsilon|\bar r_\sigma|\) in the two normal components.  The full
current solution is
\begin{align*}
 v_{\sigma,g}(s)
 &=\mathbf1+r_{\sigma,g}(s)\mathbf1
       +z_{0,N}(r_{\sigma,g}(s))+Z_g(s),\\
 w_{\sigma,g}(s)
 &=w_{\rm seed,N}(r_{\sigma,g}(s))-p_g(s).
\end{align*}
The dimension-uniform critical-curve and seed derivative bounds, followed
by restriction to
\([s-\theta_m,s]\), now prove the first term of
\cref{eq:physical-hit-uniform-history-jets}.

We also record why the preceding fixed-time estimate is enough for a moving
complete history.  Put
\(B_\sigma=[T_{\sigma,-}-\theta_m,T_{\sigma,+}]\).  On this interval every
delayed argument is generated.  The graph residual equations, divided by
the fixed positive \(\delta\), give the local embedding
\begin{equation}
 \|Z_y\|_{W^{1,\infty}(B_\sigma;E_N)}
 +\|p_y\|_{W^{1,\infty}(B_\sigma)}
 \le C_{\delta,h}\|y\|_{\mathcal D(\mathscr N_\sigma)},
                                                        \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-local-graph-embedding}
\end{equation}
for every graph-domain direction \(y\); the constant is independent of
\(N\), but may lose powers of \(\delta^{-1}\).  Indeed the right-hand sides
of \cref{eq:fixed-phase-normal-block} are bounded in \(C^0(B_\sigma)\) by
the current, delayed-current, algebraic, and residual components of the
graph norm.  Apply this estimate to
\(y=x-T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\).  The defining fixed-phase equations for
\(T_\sigma^{\rm rc}a\), together with
\cref{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile}, give the same local
\(W^{1,\infty}\) bound for the full normal direction \(x\).  The
\(\mathsf A_{\phi,\mathrm{rel}}^1\)-component of the graph norm and
\cref{eq:nonlinear-phase-chart-a-derivative} give it for the reconstructed
phase direction.  Differentiating the smooth reconstruction on the common
small graph ball and using
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-derivative} therefore yields
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}\le1}
 \|\partial_sD_g(v_{\sigma,g},w_{\sigma,g})k\|_
       {L^\infty(B_\sigma)}
 \le C_{\delta,h}.                              \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-gauge-W1}
\end{equation}
Consequently restriction and translation satisfy the operator-norm bound
\begin{equation}
 \|D_g\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)
       -D_g\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(t)\|_
       {\mathcal L(\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h,\mathscr Y_N)}
 \le C_{\delta,h}|s-t|.                         \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-translation}
\end{equation}

The order-\(h\) compatibility at the original seam removes the derivative
jumps.  Substitution in the exact fold-time RFDE expresses
\(\partial_s(v_{\sigma,g},w_{\sigma,g})\) as a smooth function of the
current and finitely many delayed values.  It is therefore continuous
jointly in \((s,g)\).  The fixed-time gauge derivative is continuous in
\(g\) by \cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-derivative}, and its operator-norm
continuity in \(s\) is \cref{eq:proof-physical-hit-translation}.  The
continuous-partial-derivative criterion now proves that
\((s,g)\mapsto\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)\) is jointly \(C^1\) into
\(\mathscr Y_N\).  The nonuniform constant in
\cref{eq:proof-physical-hit-gauge-W1} is used only for this topological
\(C^1\) assertion; the dimension- and \(\delta\)-uniform weak-history jet
bound remains \cref{eq:physical-hit-uniform-history-jets}.  No same-space
differentiability of translation on an arbitrary continuous history is
being used.

Choose the generated tracker in
\cref{cor:generated-interior-canonical-tracker} on this common buffer.
By \cref{eq:exact-outer-history-tangent},
\begin{equation*}
 \tau_{\sigma,g}(s)
 =\partial_r\mathcal H_{V_{\sigma,g},W_{\sigma,g},q_{\sigma,g}}
       (r_{\sigma,g}(s)).
\end{equation*}
The exact backtrack formula and
\cref{eq:generated-interior-q-bounds,%
eq:generated-interior-wprime-bounds,%
eq:generated-interior-Vprime-bound}
therefore give the second term of
\cref{eq:physical-hit-uniform-history-jets}.  This step is what prevents a
spurious \(\delta^{-1}\) loss in the normalized moving-history tangent.

It remains to construct the hit.  The phase-chart smallness and
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-size} imply, uniformly on the bracket,
\begin{equation}
 |r_{\sigma,g}(s)-\bar r_\sigma(s)|
 \le C\{\lambda_\delta+\|g\|\}
          |\bar r_\sigma(s)|.                    \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-phase-closeness}
\end{equation}
Choose \(\lambda_h\) and \(g_h\) so that the right-hand side is at most
\(\kappa_hr_h/2\).  The bounds in
\cref{eq:physical-hit-reference-bracket,%
eq:proof-physical-hit-phase-closeness}
then give
\begin{align}
 r_{\sigma,g}(T_{\sigma,-})-e_{\sigma,h}
 &\ge\frac12\kappa_hr_h,\notag\\
 r_{\sigma,g}(T_{\sigma,+})-e_{\sigma,h}
 &\le-\frac12\kappa_hr_h.                       \label{eq:proof-physical-hit-signs}
\end{align}
The strict speed estimate in
\cref{eq:fixed-section-zero-speed-sign} makes the left-hand side strictly
decreasing in \(s\).  The intermediate-value theorem proves existence and
uniqueness in the bracket, and strict monotonicity proves uniqueness on the
whole forward branch segment.

The scalar map
\((s,g)\mapsto r_{\sigma,g}(s)-e_{\sigma,h}\) is \(C^1\), with
\(\partial_sr_{\sigma,g}=\delta q_{\sigma,g}\) separated from zero.
The Banach implicit-function theorem therefore gives the \(C^1\) hit time.
Differentiation gives the first two lines of
\cref{eq:physical-hit-time-derivative}; here
\begin{equation*}
 \varrho_ND_g\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)k
 =D_gr_{\sigma,g}(s)k,\qquad
 \varrho_N\partial_s\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)
 =r_{\sigma,g}'(s).
\end{equation*}
The chain rule for
\(\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)=
  \mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(S_{\sigma,h}(g))\)
then gives
\cref{eq:physical-hit-history-derivative}.  The speed lower bound,
\cref{eq:physical-hit-uniform-history-jets}, and
\(\|\varrho_N\|\le1\) prove
\cref{eq:physical-hit-map-uniform-bound}.  Finally,
\cref{eq:physical-hit-normalized-tangent} identifies
\(\Id-\tau_{\sigma,g}\varrho_N\) with
\cref{eq:physical-quotient-process}.  The construction never compares two
gauges after an outer endpoint recut and supplies no second gauge
derivative.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:raw-gauge-strong-hit-buffer}]
We first record the uniform zero-order scales on the fixed annulus.  The
relative phase estimate, the graph--action norm, and
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-derivative} give, after decreasing the common
gauge ball,
\begin{align}
 \sup_{s\in B_{\sigma,h}}
 \left\{
  \frac{|r_{\sigma,g}(s)|}{r_h}
 +\frac{\|Z_{\sigma,g}(s)\|_N}{\varepsilon}
 +\frac{|p_{\sigma,g}(s)|}{\varepsilon r_h}
 \right\}&\le C,\notag\\
 \sup_{s\in B_{\sigma,h}}
 \left\{
  \frac{|D_gr_{\sigma,g}(s)k|}{r_h}
 +\frac{\|D_gZ_{\sigma,g}(s)k\|_N}{\varepsilon}
 +\frac{|D_gp_{\sigma,g}(s)k|}{\varepsilon r_h}
 \right\}&\le C\|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.
                                                        \label{eq:proof-strong-hit-zero-scales}
\end{align}
For the last component use
\(p_g=p_{x_g,a_g}^\sharp
 -\varepsilon\mathscr P_{c,\sigma,N}a_g\), not a nonlinear phase-delay
functional.  The first term has the relative graph scale, while
\cref{eq:relative-phase-delay-profile} controls the second; on the fixed
annulus its \(O(\varepsilon\delta)\) contribution is absorbed by
\(O(\varepsilon r_h)\).  The same argument applies after one gauge
derivative.

We next upgrade these zero-order estimates without invoking a new inverse.
By \cref{eq:raw-gauge-exact-boundary-identities} and
\cref{prop:fold-time-tracker-normal-form}, every member of the family is a
classical solution of the same unmodified fixed-delay RFDE.  Here the
complete initial voltage history is not merely the normal assembler
\(\mathscr H_{\sigma,g}^{\rm raw}\).  The exact boundary identities,
followed by the collar interface
\cref{eq:fold-time-collar-interface}, identify the full initial
history--resource pair with the corresponding value of \(\Gamma_e^h\).
The raw history assembler is \(C^2\) into \(C^h\) by
\cref{eq:nonlinear-raw-history-C2-bounds}; the collar chart is \(C^3\) on
the retained data ball.  Composing both with the \(C^1\) zero map in
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-derivative} therefore makes this full compatible
initial pair \(C^1\) in \(g\), into \(C^h\).

For completeness, perform the required method-of-steps estimate directly.
On the annulus
\(|r_{\sigma,g}|\asymp r_h\), introduce the normalized current variables
\begin{equation*}
 \mathbf y_{\sigma,g}
 =\left(r_{\sigma,g}/r_h,
        Z_{\sigma,g}/\varepsilon,
        p_{\sigma,g}/(\varepsilon r_h)\right).
\end{equation*}
After substitution in
\cref{eq:fold-time-speed-reconstruction,eq:fold-time-tracker-r,eq:fold-time-tracker-Z,eq:fold-time-tracker-p},
the right-hand sides and their state derivatives through order three are
bounded on the common graph ball.  The bounds are uniform in \(N\): every
delayed row is estimated by the common layer-sum bound, and all critical
curve, seed, and nonlinear coefficient derivatives are uniform on this
fixed annulus.  Every differentiation of a current equation costs at most
one factor \(\delta^{-1}\).  The finitely enlarged delay collar needed for
three such substitutions remains generated interior and in the same-sign
fixed annulus after decreasing \(\delta_0\); this follows from the
\(O(\delta^{-1})\) distance of the bracket from the branch endpoints.
Using the already available whole-branch \(C^0\) graph bound, differentiate
the RFDE and its first variational equation pointwise in time, rather than
propagating a new estimate across \(O(\delta^{-1})\) delay cells.  The
three-step derivative recursion gives, for \(0\le j\le3\),
\begin{align}
 \sup_{B_{\sigma,h}}\delta^j
       \|\partial_s^j\mathbf y_{\sigma,g}\|&\le C,\notag\\
 \sup_{B_{\sigma,h}}\delta^j
       \|\partial_s^jD_g\mathbf y_{\sigma,g}k\|
 &\le C\|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.  \label{eq:proof-strong-hit-step-induction}
\end{align}
To verify Fr\'{e}chet, rather than merely bounded, gauge dependence, fix
\(g,k\) and insert the difference quotient
\[
 \Delta_t\mathbf y
 =t^{-1}(\mathbf y_{\sigma,g+tk}-\mathbf y_{\sigma,g})
\]
in the normalized RFDE.  The \(C^1\) graph reconstruction gives
\(\Delta_t\mathbf y\to D_g\mathbf y_{\sigma,g}k\) first in the whole-branch
\(C^0\) topology.  Subtracting the two equations and passing successively
through the same three pointwise time-derivative identities upgrades this
convergence to the scaled \(C^3\) topology, uniformly for \(k\) in the unit
ball.  The coefficients in those identities depend continuously on
\(g\), so the resulting operator-valued derivative is continuous.  Since
\(T_{\sigma,-}-\theta_m>2\theta_m\), every point of
\(B_{\sigma,h}\) lies beyond the two method-of-steps seams needed for
third time regularity.  Equivalently, the order-\(h\) compatibility already
removes those seam jumps.  Thus the one-sided cell derivatives in
\cref{eq:proof-strong-hit-step-induction} join to genuine \(C^3\)
functions.  Estimates
\eqref{eq:proof-strong-hit-zero-scales} and
\eqref{eq:proof-strong-hit-step-induction}
prove \cref{eq:strong-hit-C1-map,eq:strong-hit-C1-bound}.

At the left bracket endpoint,
\cref{eq:proof-physical-hit-signs} and
the generated speed bound
\(|r_{\sigma,g}'|\le\delta q_+\) from
\cref{eq:generated-interior-q-bounds} give
\begin{equation*}
 S_{\sigma,h}(g)-T_{\sigma,-}
 \ge\frac{\kappa_hr_h}{2\delta q_+}.
\end{equation*}
The same calculation between the hit and the right bracket endpoint gives
the corresponding right-hand bound.  Decreasing the constant once to pay
for the extra left delay collar proves
\cref{eq:strong-hit-time-margin}.

Condition \cref{eq:strong-hit-window-budget} and the time margin show that
\cref{eq:strong-hit-reference-buffer} is defined.  Under the additional
shift budget \cref{eq:strong-hit-chart-shift-budget}, every translation by
\(\widehat\tau_{\sigma,h}(g)/\delta
 =S_{\sigma,h}(g)-S_{\sigma,h}^0\)
remains inside \(B_{\sigma,h}\).  Restriction and fixed translation are
bounded on the scaled \(C^3\) space, so
\cref{eq:strong-hit-C1-map} gives the asserted \(C^1\) fixed-reference
buffer.  Its reference event has
\begin{equation*}
 r_{\sigma,0}(S_{\sigma,h}^0)=e_{\sigma,h},\qquad
 r_{\sigma,0}'(S_{\sigma,h}^0)
 =\delta q_{\sigma,0}(e_{\sigma,h}),\qquad
 |q_{\sigma,0}(e_{\sigma,h})|\ge q_-.
\end{equation*}
Hence all hypotheses of
\cref{lem:terminal-relative-trace-scale-chart} hold with \(d=1\).
Composition with the \(C^1\) gauge map proves the recut assertion.  Finally,
\cref{eq:physical-hit-map-uniform-bound} controls
\(D_g\widehat\tau\), and the first translation estimate in
\cref{eq:trace-scale-translation-losses}, together with
\cref{eq:strong-hit-C1-bound}, gives the safe ordinary-product bound
\cref{eq:strong-hit-recut-loss}.  To see that the shift budget is available
on a fixed gauge ball, integrate
\cref{eq:physical-hit-map-uniform-bound}: after decreasing the gauge radius,
\[
 |S_{\sigma,h}(g)-S_{\sigma,h}^0|
 \le C\delta^{-1}\|g\|
 <\frac{c_{\rm buf}}{8\delta}.
\]
Taking \(\ell_\delta=c_{\rm buf}/(4\delta)\) gives
\cref{eq:strong-hit-chart-shift-budget}; since
\(\delta S_\delta\to0\), any \(L_\delta=O(S_\delta)\) also satisfies
\cref{eq:strong-hit-window-budget} after a harmless decrease of
\(c_{\rm buf}\).  No second gauge derivative or normalized terminal Schur
estimate has been used.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:repelling-component-flushing}]
The repelling travel time satisfies \(S_r\ge c/\delta\), whereas
\(L_b=O(S_\delta)\).  Thus
\(2L_b+\theta_m<S_r\) for small \(\delta\).  On the inner buffer,
\begin{equation}
 \rho_\delta\le|\bar r_r(s)|
 \le C_{A_d}\rho_\delta,\qquad 0\le s\le L_b,     \label{eq:repelling-inner-buffer-geometry}
\end{equation}
because \(|\partial_s\bar r_r|\asymp\delta\) and
\(\delta L_b=O(\delta S_\delta)=O(\rho_\delta)\).  On
\([S_r-L_b-\theta_m,S_r]\), one has instead
\begin{equation}
 \tfrac12r_{\rm out}\le|\bar r_r(s)|\le r_{\rm out}.          \label{eq:repelling-terminal-buffer-geometry}
\end{equation}
Linearity gives the two asserted components.

We now expose the orientation hidden by the full mixed Green operator.
Expand every same-generation inverse by
\cref{eq:repelling-current-loop-series}.  A resulting decorated component
word contains forward transverse edges of lengths \(F_j\ge0\), future
scalar edges of lengths \(P_j\ge0\), and exactly \(n\) recursive strict
delay edges.  For endpoint and terminal words set
\(t_{\rm in}=0,S_r\), respectively.  For an old-history word use its current
insertion label \(t_{\rm in}=v_0\in[0,\theta_m]\), thereby absorbing the
direct collar edge \(\sigma_0\to v_0\) into the root.  The time labels then
give the exact telescoping identity
\begin{equation}
 t_{\rm out}-t_{\rm in}
 =\sum_{j=1}^n\theta_{k_j}
       +\sum_jF_j-\sum_jP_j.                       \label{eq:repelling-oriented-word-identity}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, if an old-history word is rooted at its true history label
\(\sigma_0\), the right-hand side has the additional direct delay
\(\theta_{k_0}\), and
\[
 t_{\rm out}-\sigma_0
 =\theta_{k_0}+\sum_{j=1}^n\theta_{k_j}
       +\sum_jF_j-\sum_jP_j.
\]
Current loops add one edge of each orientation but no strict delay.  Their
arbitrary multiplicities sum geometrically by
\cref{eq:repelling-current-loop-bound}.  This is the component-word action
lemma; no causal support is attributed to the full mixed Green operator.

Consider first the inner component on
\([L_b-\theta_m,L_b]\).  Its insertions occur at the initial trace or in
the first delay window.  If \(n\le M-1\), then
\begin{equation}
 t_{\rm out}-t_{\rm in}
       -\sum_{j=1}^n\theta_{k_j}\ge L_0.           \label{eq:repelling-inner-word-distance}
\end{equation}
Indeed, the endpoint-rooted lower bound is
\[
 L_b-\theta_m-(M-1)\theta_m
   =L_b-M\theta_m=L_0+\theta_m+1,
\]
whereas the old-history-rooted lower bound, using
\(v_0\le\theta_m\), is
\[
 L_b-\theta_m-\theta_m-(M-1)\theta_m
   =L_b-(M+1)\theta_m=L_0+1.
\]
This is why the direct collar edge is not a generation but is still paid
for in the buffer length.
Hence the total forward transverse length is at least \(L_0\) plus the
total future scalar length.  The component kernel product is therefore
bounded by \(e^{-cL_0/\delta}\).  Keeping the coarser alternative in which
a far-future scalar subword is estimated directly gives the harmless
envelope
\begin{equation}
 e^{-cL_0/\delta}+e^{-cS_\delta L_0},              \label{eq:repelling-inner-low-word-tail}
\end{equation}
because \(a_p/\delta\ge cS_\delta\) throughout the inner buffer.  Boundary
faces created by differentiation are decorated words with fewer
convolutions and the same oriented distance.  The strong \(\delta\)-weights
cancel all kernel derivatives.

For the terms with at least \(M\) strict edges, sum the convergent remainder
using \cref{eq:repelling-generation-number}.  The genuine generation number
is even \(C\delta/S_\delta\); using the stated, larger uniform budget gives
\begin{equation}
 \left\|\sum_{n\ge M}x_{\rm in}^{[n]}\right\|_{\mathfrak S_{r,d}}
 \le C_d\left(\frac{\delta^{1-2\vartheta}}{S_\delta}\right)^M
       \|\gamma\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}.  \label{eq:repelling-inner-generation-tail}
\end{equation}

Turn next to the terminal component.  Put
\[
 s_*=S_r-L_b,\qquad s_o=S_r-L_b-\theta_m,\qquad
 W_T=[s_o,s_*],\qquad O_T=[s_o,S_r].
\]
Thus \(W_T\) is the complete-history handoff window and \(O_T\) is contained
in the outer buffer of
\cref{eq:repelling-terminal-buffer-geometry}.  A decorated terminal word is
rooted at \(t_{\rm in}=S_r\).  If it has \(n<M\) recursive strict edges,
traverse it from the root toward its output in \(W_T\).  If it stays in
\(O_T\), the oriented identity gives
\[
 \sum_jP_j-\sum_jF_j
 =S_r-t_{\rm out}+\sum_{j=1}^n\theta_{k_j}\ge L_b,
\]
so its accumulated outer scalar action is at least \(cL_b/\delta\).  If it
leaves \(O_T\), the first-exit argument below gives the still stronger
bound.  Hence all \(n<M\) words contribute
\[
 C_de^{-cL_0/\delta}
       \frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}}.
\]
Here and below, each complete generation uses the already resummed current
inverse.  Since \(M\) is fixed, all loop factors in a finite root-prefix are
absorbed into \(C_d\); the global generation number likewise already
contains its loop sum.

Now let \(n\ge M\).  In root-to-output order select the first \(M\)
recursive strict edges nearest \(S_r\), together with their complete
generation blocks.  Let \(\mathcal V_M\) contain every time label in this
prefix: both endpoints of every strict edge, all component integration
vertices, and the full intervening kernel intervals of every same-generation
current loop.  This last clause is essential; checking only the strict-edge
endpoints would not localize the loop.  If
\(\mathcal V_M\subset O_T\), each of these \(M\) complete blocks is measured
in the outer normalized state and has the one-edge bound
\begin{equation}
 \beta_{r,\rm out}\le C_d\varepsilon
       \le C_d\delta^{2-2\vartheta}.               \label{eq:repelling-terminal-generation-number}
\end{equation}
Indeed, on \(O_T\),
\(B_k^Z=O(\varepsilon)\) and
\(B_k^p=-\varepsilon a_pc_{k,N}\), while
\[
 \delta^{-1}\int_s^{S_r}u_p(s,u)a_p(u)\,\dd u\le1.
\]
The scalar normalization costs only the fixed factor
\(r_{\rm out}^{-1}\), the layer sum is dimension-uniform, and the restricted
current loops contribute the fixed factor
\((1-q_{\rm loop})^{-1}\).  The weighted coefficient jets give the same
bound through order \(d\).
All remaining recursion blocks are estimated by the global generation
number \(\beta_{r,\delta}\).  Summing delay labels by the layer-sum bound and
loop multiplicities by the current-loop series gives the explicit stay
estimate
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{\mathscr W:\,{\rm stay}}\|\mathscr W\|
 \le C_d\beta_{r,\rm out}^M
       \left(\sum_{j\ge0}\beta_{r,\delta}^j\right)
       \frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}}.                  \label{eq:repelling-terminal-stay-sum}
\end{equation}

Suppose instead that \(\mathcal V_M\not\subset O_T\).  Follow the selected
prefix from \(S_r\) and stop at its first hit or crossing of the left
boundary \(s_o\).  Forward \(Z\)-edges and strict delay edges move time
labels to the right; only a future scalar edge can make this first leftward
crossing.  Split that scalar kernel at \(s_o\), using its semigroup identity.
All component labels before the split lie in \(O_T\).  Applied to this
root-prefix, \cref{eq:repelling-oriented-word-identity} gives the
crossing-number identity
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{\rm pre}P_j-\sum_{\rm pre}F_j
 =S_r-s_o+\sum_{\rm pre}\theta_{k_j}
 \ge L_b+\theta_m.                                 \label{eq:repelling-terminal-first-exit-action}
\end{equation}
Since every pre-exit scalar segment lies in \(O_T\),
\begin{equation*}
 \prod_{\rm pre}|u_p|
 \le C_d\exp\left\{-\frac{c_{\rm out}}{\delta}
                   \sum_{\rm pre}P_j\right\}
 \le C_de^{-c(L_b+\theta_m)/\delta}
 \le C_de^{-cL_0/\delta}.
\end{equation*}
The suffix after the split may leave or re-enter
\(O_T\); it cannot remove an action factor already extracted.  Estimate all
remaining recursion blocks globally.  There are only \(M\) possible
first-exit generation-block classes.  If the marked crossing lies inside a
current loop, its possible location is summed by
\(\sum_{\ell\ge0}(\ell+1)q_{\rm loop}^{\ell}<\infty\) and is absorbed into
\(C_d\).  Therefore
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{\mathscr W:\,{\rm exit}}\|\mathscr W\|
 \le C_de^{-cL_0/\delta}
       \left(\sum_{j\ge0}\beta_{r,\delta}^j\right)
       \frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}}.                  \label{eq:repelling-terminal-exit-sum}
\end{equation}
The remaining geometric series is dimension-uniform and bounded after
decreasing \(\delta_0\).  Combining the short, stay, and first-exit word
classes gives
\begin{equation}
 \left\|x_{\rm u}\right\|_{\mathfrak S_{r,d};
       [S_r-L_b-\theta_m,S_r-L_b]}
 \le C_d\left\{e^{-cL_0/\delta}
            +\delta^{M(2-2\vartheta)}\right\}
       \frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}}.                 \label{eq:repelling-terminal-strong-tail}
\end{equation}
Cellwise differentiation of an iterated integral produces interior kernel
derivatives and boundary faces.  Each face is again a decorated word with
the same root and output labels; if a convolution collapses, its recursive
edge count can only decrease.  Reclassifying the face as short, stay, or
first-exit and using
\cref{eq:repelling-weighted-kernel-bounds} proves
\cref{eq:repelling-terminal-stay-sum,eq:repelling-terminal-exit-sum,%
eq:repelling-terminal-strong-tail} through order \(d\), including every
one-sided seam trace.

The method-of-steps seam argument used on the attracting branch applies to
the sum solving the original retarded equation.  Since \(M>d\) and both
output windows lie after more than \(d\theta_m\) of forward evolution, their
restrictions are genuine \(C^d\) histories, not merely piecewise ones.

It remains to convert the strong estimates to slow norms.  On the inner
window, \cref{eq:repelling-inner-buffer-geometry} and
\(\partial_s\bar r_r=\delta q_0(\bar r_r)\) give
\begin{equation}
 \max_{0\le j\le d}\sup |r|^j|\partial_r^jf|
 \le C_{d,A_d}\left(\frac{S_\delta}{\delta}\right)^d
       \max_{0\le i\le d}\delta^i\sup|\partial_s^if|.        \label{eq:repelling-inner-strong-to-slow}
\end{equation}
On the terminal window the corresponding factor is
\(C_d\delta^{-2d}\).  The factor \(|r|^{-1}\) required for the
\(\mathsf P_\varepsilon^d\)-norm is already present in
\cref{eq:repelling-component-strong-space}.  Applying these conversions to
\cref{eq:repelling-inner-low-word-tail,%
eq:repelling-inner-generation-tail,%
eq:repelling-terminal-strong-tail} gives the \(Z\)- and \(p\)-parts of
\cref{eq:repelling-inner-flush,eq:repelling-terminal-flush}.

Finally, because \(H=0\), the exact recovery identity remains
\begin{equation}
 Q(r)=\frac{q_0(r)p'(r)}
              {w_{0,N}'(r)+\varepsilon\mathfrak d_N(r)}.      \label{eq:repelling-Q-from-p}
\end{equation}
By \cref{eq:repelling-inverse-coefficient-jets}, for \(0\le i\le d-1\),
\[
 |r|^{i+1}|\partial_r^iQ(r)|
 \le C_d\max_{1\le j\le i+1}
          |r|^{j-1}|\partial_r^jp(r)|.
\]
Thus the \(\mathsf P_\varepsilon^d\)-bound gives the
\(\mathsf Q_\varepsilon^{d-1}\)-bound with no additional power of
\(\delta\).
Inserting the common \(\varepsilon^{-1}\) normalization proves the two
displayed estimates.

The first and third terms in \(\mathcal E_{r,\rm in}\) tend to zero by
superalgebraic decay and
\(M(1-2\vartheta)>d\).  Since
\[
 \left(\frac{S_\delta}{\delta}\right)^d
 e^{-cS_\delta L_0}
 =S_\delta^d\delta^{-d}
       e^{-cA_dS_\delta^2},
\]
one fixed sufficiently large \(A_d\) also makes the middle term a positive
power of \(\delta\).  The terminal exponents are positive by the same
inequality used in the attracting proof.  This completes the proof and the
scope statements follow from the construction.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:compatible-repelling-collar-closure}]
The strong cap bound in
\cref{eq:strong-compatible-feedback-bound} implies the fading \(C^0\)
bound in \cref{eq:fixed-phase-collar-feedback}.  Therefore
\cref{prop:fixed-phase-green-collar-buffer} gives a unique closed-loop
solution of \cref{eq:strong-compatible-feedback-problem}.  Since the
problem and the feedback are linear, its inverse splits into the two
columns in \cref{eq:compatible-repelling-column-split}.  The two actual
histories constructed below belong to \(C^h\).  Hence the corresponding
open high-order component solutions supplied by
\cref{lem:repelling-component-generations} coincide with these mild
closed-loop columns by the \(C^0\) uniqueness just invoked.

Apply the \(C^0\) inverse separately to those columns.  Current evaluation
in \(\mathsf X_{r,0}\), followed by
\cref{eq:strong-compatible-feedback-bound}, gives
\begin{align*}
 \frac{|p_{\rm c}(0)|}{\rho_\delta}
 &\le C\|\gamma_0\|_{N,-\rho_\delta,\delta}^{\sharp},&
 \frac{|p_{\rm u}(0)|}{\rho_\delta}
 &\le C\frac{|b_u|}{r_{\rm out}}.
\end{align*}
The zeroth term of the strong cap norm dominates the fading norm.  Hence
\begin{align*}
 \|\gamma_{\rm c}\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
 &\le\|\gamma_0\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
       +C_\Gamma\frac{|p_{\rm c}(0)|}{\rho_\delta},\\
 \|\gamma_{\rm u}\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}
 &\le C_\Gamma\frac{|p_{\rm u}(0)|}{\rho_\delta},
\end{align*}
which proves \cref{eq:compatible-repelling-history-columns}.  Notice that
\((\mathfrak C_ec)(0)=0\) leaves the transverse boundary row unchanged.

The first closed-loop column is the fixed-history solution with total old
history \(\gamma_{\rm c}\) and zero terminal datum.  Substitution of the
first bound in \cref{eq:compatible-repelling-history-columns} into
\cref{eq:repelling-inner-flush} proves
\cref{eq:compatible-repelling-inner-column}.

For the second column, split once more into the fixed-history response to
\(\gamma_{\rm u}\), with zero terminal datum, and the response to
\((0,b_u)\).  The latter is bounded on \(J_{r,\rm u}\) by
\cref{eq:repelling-terminal-flush}.  We record the cross estimate for the
former.  Use the decorated-word expansion in the proof of
\cref{prop:repelling-component-flushing}.  For an endpoint- or
old-history-rooted word with fewer than \(M\) recursive strict edges and
output in \([S_r-L_b-\theta_m,S_r-L_b]\),
\cref{eq:repelling-oriented-word-identity} gives
\begin{equation*}
 t_{\rm out}-t_{\rm in}
      -\sum_{j=1}^n\theta_{k_j}\ge L_0
\end{equation*}
after decreasing \(\delta_0\): indeed \(t_{\rm in}\le\theta_m\), whereas
\(S_r\ge c/\delta\), \(L_b=O(S_\delta)\), and, more explicitly,
\begin{equation*}
 S_r-L_b-(M+1)\theta_m
   >L_b-M\theta_m=L_0+\theta_m+1
\end{equation*}
for small \(\delta\).  Thus the total forward
transverse length exceeds the total future scalar length by at least
\(L_0\), and the weighted component kernels give
\(C_de^{-cL_0/\delta}\).

All words with at least \(M\) strict edges are bounded globally by
\cref{eq:repelling-generation-number}; after summing the geometric
remainder their strong norm is at most
\begin{equation*}
 C_d\left(\frac{\delta^{1-2\vartheta}}{S_\delta}\right)^M
       \|\gamma_{\rm u}\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}.
\end{equation*}
Kernel differentiation and boundary faces preserve the same root, output,
and oriented-distance alternatives, exactly as in
\cref{eq:repelling-inner-generation-tail}.  The output window is in the
outer annulus, so the strong-to-slow conversion costs
\(C_d\delta^{-2d}\).  Recovering \(Q\) from
\cref{eq:repelling-Q-from-p} costs no further power.  Consequently the
fixed-history cross response is bounded by
\begin{equation*}
 C_{d,A_d}\mathcal E_{r,\rm cross}(\delta)
       \varepsilon^{-1}
       \|\gamma_{\rm u}\|_{\mathrm{cap},h,-\rho_\delta}.
\end{equation*}
Together with \cref{eq:compatible-repelling-history-columns} and the pure
terminal estimate this proves
\cref{eq:compatible-repelling-terminal-column}.  Condition
\cref{eq:compatible-repelling-generation-count} makes the algebraic term in
\(\mathcal E_{r,\rm cross}\) tend to zero; the exponential term is
superalgebraically small.

Finally, hold \(Z_e,\widehat\gamma,\nu,\eta\) fixed in
\cref{prop:compatible-endpoint-jet-collar}.  Its current voltage value is
independent of \(p_e\), so the resulting voltage-history derivative
vanishes at \(\theta=0\).  The tame chart estimate
\cref{eq:collar-chart-tame-bound} gives exactly
\cref{eq:strong-compatible-feedback-bound}.  This identifies the abstract
feedback with the raw-compatible collar column and completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{prop:physical-history-phase-quotient}]
Because the rescaled fold-time RFDE is autonomous, differentiation of the
base solution in fold time gives a solution of the variational equation.  At the
history level this says
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal U_*(s,u)
 \left(\theta\mapsto v_*'(u+\theta),w_*'(u)\right)
 =\left(\theta\mapsto v_*'(s+\theta),w_*'(s)\right).
                                                               \label{eq:phase-quotient-time-tangent}
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\[
 \varrho_N
 \left(\theta\mapsto v_*'(s+\theta),w_*'(s)\right)
 =\pi_N^Tv_*'(s)=r_*'(s).
\]
Division by the nonzero endpoint speed proves both the normalization in
\cref{eq:physical-normalized-tangent} and
\cref{eq:physical-tangent-transport}.  It also shows uniqueness among scalar
multiples of the time tangent.

Since \(\varrho_N\tau_*(s)=1\), the operator \(Q_*(s)\) is a projection with
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Ran}Q_*(s)=\ker\varrho_N,
 \qquad
 \ker Q_*(s)=\operatorname{span}\{\tau_*(s)\}.      \label{eq:phase-quotient-range-kernel}
\end{equation}
For \(x\in\ker\varrho_N\), use the variational cocycle identity and
\cref{eq:physical-tangent-transport} to compute
\begin{align*}
 &Q_*(s)\mathcal U_*(s,t)Q_*(t)\mathcal U_*(t,u)x\\
 &\quad=Q_*(s)\mathcal U_*(s,u)x
 -Q_*(s)\mathcal U_*(s,t)\tau_*(t)
       \varrho_N\mathcal U_*(t,u)x\\
 &\quad=Q_*(s)\mathcal U_*(s,u)x,
\end{align*}
because the second term is a scalar multiple of \(Q_*(s)\tau_*(s)=0\).
This is \cref{eq:physical-quotient-cocycle}.  The range--kernel identities
identify the process with the quotient process, and the triangle inequality
gives \cref{eq:physical-quotient-bound}.  Finally, normalizing any nonzero
multiple of the time tangent by \(\varrho_N\tau=1\) recovers exactly
\cref{eq:physical-normalized-tangent}; hence the construction contains no
choice of tangent scale or frozen eigendirection.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{prop:exact-resource-gauge-quotient}]
The history derivative in
\cref{eq:exact-outer-history-tangent} gives
\(E_s^c=\partial_r\mathcal H_{V,w,q}(r_s)\).  Since the autonomous
time tangent is \(\delta q(r_s)E_s^c\),
\cref{eq:phase-quotient-time-tangent} yields
\[
 \mathcal U_*(s,\sigma)\{\delta q(r_\sigma)E_\sigma^c\}
 =\delta q(r_s)E_s^c,
\]
which proves \cref{eq:resource-gauge-center-cocycle}.

For reference, the full fold-time variational equation along the tracker is
\begin{align}
 \xi'(s)={}&\delta^{-1}\mathcal J_*(s)\xi(s)
 -\delta^{-1}\omega(s)\mathbf1\notag\\
 &+\delta K\sum_{k=0}^mL_{k,N}(\eta)
       \{\xi(s)-\xi(s-\theta_k)\},\notag\\
 \omega'(s)={}&\delta\pi_N^T\xi(s).                 \label{eq:resource-gauge-full-variation}
\end{align}
At the current endpoint,
\[
 \xi(s)=u(s)+V'(r_s)a(s),\qquad
 \omega(s)=w'(r_s)a(s).
\]
Differentiate \(a=\omega/w'(r_s)\), use \(r_s'=\delta q(r_s)\), and
differentiate the exact resource identity
\[
 q(r)w'(r)=r-\delta^2\nu
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 q'(r)w'(r)+q(r)w''(r)=1=\pi_N^TV'(r).
\]
The full variation equation then gives
\begin{align*}
 a'
 &=\frac{\delta\pi_N^T\{u+V'a\}}{w'}
   -a\frac{\delta q w''}{w'}\\
 &=\frac{\delta}{w'}\pi_N^Tu+\delta q'a,
\end{align*}
which is \cref{eq:resource-gauge-a}.

For \(-\theta_m\le\theta<0\), histories translate, so
\[
 (\partial_s-\partial_\theta)\xi(s+\theta)=0.
\]
The definition of \(e_s\), or direct differentiation of
\(\delta q(r_s)e_s(\theta)=v_*'(s+\theta)\), gives
\begin{equation}
 (\partial_s-\partial_\theta)e_s(\theta)
 =-\delta q'(r_s)e_s(\theta).                       \label{eq:resource-gauge-e-transport}
\end{equation}
Apply \(\partial_s-\partial_\theta\) to
\(\psi_s=\xi_s-e_sa\) and use
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-a,eq:resource-gauge-e-transport}; the two
\(\delta q'a\) terms cancel and give
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-transport}.

The normalized center history is not itself a solution but obeys the
transport law \cref{eq:resource-gauge-center-cocycle}.  Equivalently, the
full variational operator applied to \(E_s^c\) equals
\(\partial_sE_s^c+\delta q'(r_s)E_s^c\).  Substitute
\[
 (\xi_s,\omega)
 =(\psi_s,0)+aE_s^c
\]
in \cref{eq:resource-gauge-full-variation}, subtract the derivative of
\(aE_s^c\), and use \cref{eq:resource-gauge-a}.  All terms proportional to
\(a\), including the delayed tangent terms, cancel.  The voltage endpoint
of the remaining rank-one term is
\[
 -V'(r_s)\frac{\delta}{w'(r_s)}\pi_N^Tu(s)
 =-\delta H_s\pi_N^Tu(s).
\]
This proves \cref{eq:resource-gauge-boundary}.  It also proves directly that
\(\psi_s\) is unchanged by adding a multiple of \(E_s^c\).

We next solve the nonstandard transport equation along characteristics.
For fixed \(s,\theta\), set
\[
 F(\sigma)=\psi_{s+\sigma}(\theta-\sigma),
 \qquad \theta\le\sigma\le0.
\]
Then \(F(\theta)=u(s+\theta)\), \(F(0)=\psi_s(\theta)\), and
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-transport} gives
\[
 F'(\sigma)
 =-\delta h_{s+\sigma}(\theta-\sigma)
       \pi_N^Tu(s+\sigma).
\]
Integration proves
\[
 \psi_s(\theta)=u_s(\theta)
 -\delta\int_\theta^0h_{s+\sigma}(\theta-\sigma)
        \pi_N^Tu_s(\sigma)\,\dd\sigma,
\]
which is \cref{eq:resource-gauge-history-conjugacy}.  In particular,
\[
 \psi_s(-\theta_k)
 =u(s-\theta_k)-(\mathcal K_su_s)(-\theta_k).
\]
Substitution in \cref{eq:resource-gauge-boundary} proves
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-standard-rfde}; the Volterra term therefore has the
displayed positive sign inside the delay bracket.  This derivation is along
characteristics on the delay-enlarged time interval, so no derivative of a
formally time-dependent conjugacy has been discarded.

It remains to prove the uniform bounds.  Under
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-collar-bounds}, the ratios of \(q\), the vector
\(V'\), and hence \(e_s\) are bounded by common constants, while
\[
 \|h_s(\theta)\|_N\le\frac{C}{\rho_\delta}.
\]
If \(\theta\le\sigma\le0\), then
\(e^{2\kappa_*(\theta-\sigma)}\le1\).  Therefore
\begin{align*}
 e^{2\kappa_*\theta}\|(\mathcal K_s\phi)(\theta)\|_N
 &\le\frac{C\delta}{\rho_\delta}
   \int_\theta^0
      e^{2\kappa_*(\theta-\sigma)}
      e^{2\kappa_*\sigma}\|\phi(\sigma)\|_N\,\dd\sigma\\
 &\le C\frac{\delta\theta_m}{\rho_\delta}
       \|\phi\|^\sharp_{\kappa_*}.
\end{align*}
The definition of \(\rho_\delta\) proves
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-volterra-bound}.  For small \(\delta\), the bound is
at most \(1/2\), and the Neumann series proves
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-volterra-inverse}.

Conversely, let \(u\) solve
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-standard-rfde} and define
\(\psi_s=(\Id-\mathcal K_s)u_s\).  Reversing the characteristic calculation
shows that \(\psi\) satisfies
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-transport}; reversing the endpoint substitution
shows that it satisfies \cref{eq:resource-gauge-boundary}.  Solve the scalar
linear equation \cref{eq:resource-gauge-a} from any prescribed value of
\(a\), and reconstruct
\[
 (\xi_s,\omega(s))=(\psi_s,0)+a(s)E_s^c.
\]
The cancellations already proved above, now read in reverse, show that this
pair solves the full variational equation.  Thus
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-history-conjugacy,eq:resource-gauge-history-inverse}
give the asserted two-sided quotient coordinate equivalence.

Finally, \(|\omega|/|w'(r_s)|=|a|\) and
\(\xi_s=\psi_s+e_sa\), with \(\|e_s\|^\sharp_{\kappa_*}\le C\).
The two triangle inequalities give the claimed uniform equivalence between
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-anisotropic-norm} and
\(\|\psi_s\|^\sharp_{\kappa_*}+|a|\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:physical-hit-quotient-flushing}]
We give the finite-window trajectory argument because ordinary
bounded-generator roughness on \(\mathscr X_N\) would not be legitimate:
changing an RFDE boundary functional changes the generator domain.  The
argument below works with current values and their translated pasts, and
never treats point injection at \(\theta=0\) as a bounded map into
\(C([-\theta_m,0];\mathbb R^N)\).

Fix a gauge, suppress \(\sigma,g\), and write \(s_h=S_{\sigma,h}(g)\).
On the one-delay enlargement of
\([s_h-L_\delta,s_h+L_\delta]\), the generated speed bounds give
\begin{equation}
 |r_s-e_{\sigma,h}|
 \le C\delta(L_\delta+\theta_m)
 \le C_A\delta S_\delta.                          \label{eq:proof-hit-local-r-geometry}
\end{equation}
The exact voltage reconstruction on this buffer is
\[
 v(s)=\mathbf1+r_s\mathbf1+z_{0,N}(r_s)+Z_{\sigma,g}(s).
\]
By \cref{eq:strong-hit-C1-bound},
\(\|Z_{\sigma,g}(s)\|_N\le C\varepsilon\).  Smoothness of the
critical curve and of the diagonal nonlinearity therefore gives
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal J_*(s)
       -\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(e_{\sigma,h})\|
 \le C_A(\delta S_\delta+\varepsilon).            \label{eq:proof-hit-current-coefficient-difference}
\end{equation}
Moreover, on this fixed annulus,
\begin{equation}
 |W'(r_s)|\asymp r_h,\qquad
 \|E_s^c\|_{\mathscr Y_N}\le C,\qquad
 \|H_s\|_N\le\frac C{r_h},\qquad
 \|\mathcal K_s\|_{\mathcal L(
       \mathscr X_N,\|\cdot\|_{\sigma,h}^{\sharp})}
 \le C\frac{\delta}{r_h}.                         \label{eq:proof-hit-resource-coordinate-bounds}
\end{equation}
The last estimate is the proof of
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-volterra-bound} with the local lower bound
\(|r|\asymp r_h\), instead of the weaker global lower bound
\(\rho_\delta\).  The same Volterra estimate with \(\kappa_*=0\) gives
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal K_s\|_{\mathcal L(
       (\mathscr X_N,\|\cdot\|_{\infty,N}),
       (\mathscr X_N,\|\cdot\|_{\infty,N}))}
 \le C\frac{\delta}{r_h}.                         \label{eq:proof-hit-volterra-C0-bound}
\end{equation}

Put
\[
 J_h=\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(e_{\sigma,h}).
\]
At \(e_{a,h}=r_h\), the whole current matrix \(J_h\) is uniformly
stable.  Indeed, the transverse semigroup estimate in
\cref{eq:outer-stable-layer-semigroup} combines with the negative simple
eigenvalue in \cref{eq:outer-eigen-identity}.  At
\(e_{r,h}=-r_h\), it has a uniformly stable subspace and one simple
unstable line.  Denote the corresponding fixed current projections by
\(P_h^s,P_h^u\), taking \(P_h^s=\Id\), \(P_h^u=0\) on the attracting
branch.  Their norms are common, and for some \(c_0,C_0>0\),
\begin{align}
 \|e^{J_h(s-t)/\delta}P_h^s\|
 &\le C_0e^{-c_0(s-t)/\delta},&&s\ge t,\notag\\
 \|e^{J_h(s-t)/\delta}P_h^u\|
 &\le C_0e^{-c_0(t-s)/\delta},&&s\le t.           \label{eq:proof-hit-current-green-bounds}
\end{align}
The second line is only the inverse on the finite-dimensional unstable
line.

Write the exact standard quotient equation as
\begin{equation}
 u'(s)=\delta^{-1}J_hu(s)+\mathscr R_su_s,         \label{eq:proof-hit-quotient-perturbation}
\end{equation}
where
\begin{align}
 \mathscr R_s\phi
={}&\delta^{-1}\{\mathcal J_*(s)-J_h\}\phi(0)\notag\\
 &+\delta K\sum_{k=0}^mL_{k,N}(\eta)
   \bigl[\phi(0)-\phi(-\theta_k)
          +(\mathcal K_s\phi)(-\theta_k)\bigr]
   -\delta H_s\pi_N^T\phi(0).                    \label{eq:proof-hit-quotient-remainder}
\end{align}
This is an identity, not a frozen approximation.  From the definition of
\(\kappa_{\sigma,h}\),
\begin{equation}
 e^{2\kappa_{\sigma,h}\theta_m}
 \le\delta^{-2\vartheta},\qquad
 \kappa_{\sigma,h}\le\frac{C}{\delta},\qquad
 \kappa_{\sigma,h}\ge c\log(1/\delta).            \label{eq:proof-hit-rate-ledger}
\end{equation}
Consequently the delayed Volterra term, as a current functional on the
fixed fading history space, satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \left\|\delta K\sum_kL_{k,N}
       (\mathcal K_s\,\cdot)(-\theta_k)\right\|
 \le C\frac{\delta^{2-2\vartheta}}{r_h},          \label{eq:proof-hit-volterra-evaluation}
\end{equation}
where there is only one delayed-evaluation loss.  The ordinary delayed
row is bounded by \(C\delta^{1-2\vartheta}\), and the last rank-one row
in \cref{eq:proof-hit-quotient-remainder} is bounded by \(C\delta/r_h\).

For completeness, we now close the finite-window
Lyapunov--Perron estimate.  On the attracting branch put
\[
 s_-=s_h-L_\delta,\qquad d_a(s)=s-s_-
 \quad(s_-\le s\le s_h).
\]
On the repelling branch also put \(s_+=s_h+L_\delta\) and
\[
 d_r(s)=\min\{s-s_-,s_+-s\}
 \quad(s_-\le s\le s_+).
\]
Extend either distance by zero on the prescribed old collar
\([s_--\theta_m,s_-]\), and choose
\(\widehat\kappa=c_1\kappa_{\sigma,h}\) with a fixed sufficiently small
\(c_1>0\).  Both distances are one-Lipschitz.  Hence translating a current
value by at most \(\theta_m\) costs at most
\(e^{\widehat\kappa\theta_m}\), while convolution of either kernel in
\cref{eq:proof-hit-current-green-bounds} against the weight
\(e^{\widehat\kappa d_\sigma}\) costs at most \(C\delta\).

On the attracting branch, variation of constants is causal from \(s_-\).
On the repelling branch, split the current value by
\(P_h^s+P_h^u\), evolve the stable component from \(s_-\), and evolve
only the one-dimensional unstable component from \(s_+\).  Explicitly,
the mixed current Green kernel is
\begin{equation}
 G_h(s,t)=
 \begin{cases}
  e^{J_h(s-t)/\delta}P_h^s,&t\le s,\\
  -e^{J_h(s-t)/\delta}P_h^u,&t>s.
 \end{cases}                                      \label{eq:proof-hit-mixed-current-green}
\end{equation}
For an already existing solution, the resulting integral identity follows
by applying the two fixed projections to
\cref{eq:proof-hit-quotient-perturbation}.  It imposes no new
boundary-value condition.

Use these identities on the weighted current trajectory space
\[
 \|y\|_{\mathcal W_\sigma}
 =\sup e^{\widehat\kappa d_\sigma(s)}\|y(s)\|_N,
\]
including the fixed old collar in the supremum.  Split every delayed value
lying before \(s_-\) into the prescribed old-history term; all remaining
delayed values belong to the trajectory unknown.  The bounds in
\cref{eq:proof-hit-current-coefficient-difference,%
eq:proof-hit-resource-coordinate-bounds,%
eq:proof-hit-current-green-bounds,%
eq:proof-hit-volterra-evaluation}
show that the linear part acting on the trajectory unknown has norm at
most
\begin{equation}
 q_{\rm loc}
 \le C_A\left\{
    \delta S_\delta+\delta^2
       +\delta^2e^{2\kappa_{\sigma,h}\theta_m}
       +\delta^3e^{2\kappa_{\sigma,h}\theta_m}
          \right\}
 \le C_A\{\delta S_\delta+\delta^{2-2\vartheta}\}
 =o(1).                                           \label{eq:proof-hit-local-contraction}
\end{equation}
This is the RFDE-specific roughness step.  The factor \(\delta\) in front
of the braces comes from integrating the finite-dimensional current Green
kernel; the strict delay is then paid by its explicit outer factor
\(\delta\).  No unbounded point-source operator on the history space has
been used.

After decreasing \(\delta_0\), \(q_{\rm loc}\le1/2\), so the Neumann
series closes.  The affine boundary and old-history terms may incur a
fixed power of
\(e^{\kappa_{\sigma,h}\theta_m}\), but their weighted trajectory norm is
bounded by
\[
 C\delta^{-C\vartheta}
 \begin{cases}
  \|u_{s_-}\|_{a,h}^{\sharp},&\sigma=a,\\
  \|u_{s_-}\|_{r,h}^{\sharp}
      +\|u_{s_+}\|_{r,h}^{\sharp},&\sigma=r.
 \end{cases}
\]
Every point in the output history
\([s_h-\theta_m,s_h]\) has
\(d_\sigma(s)\ge L_\delta-\theta_m\).  Because
\(L_\delta=AS_\delta\to\infty\), the fixed polynomial loss and the
\(\theta_m\)-shift are absorbed by decreasing the exponential constant:
\[
 \delta^{-C\vartheta}
 e^{-\widehat\kappa(L_\delta-\theta_m)}
 \le C e^{-c\kappa_{\sigma,h}L_\delta}.
\]
This proves
\cref{eq:hit-attracting-interior-flush,%
eq:hit-repelling-interior-flush}.  Finally,
\cref{eq:proof-hit-rate-ledger} proves
\cref{eq:hit-fading-rate-bounds}, and
\[
 \kappa_{\sigma,h}L_\delta
 \ge cA S_\delta\log(1/\delta)
\]
dominates every fixed multiple of \(\log(1/\delta)\).  Hence the
flushing factor is superalgebraic.  The construction used the inverse only
on \(P_h^u\mathbb R^N\), proving the asserted scope.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:physical-hit-first-order-flatness}]
Fix a gauge direction \(k\), and let
\[
 X_k(s)=(\xi_{k,s},\omega_k(s))
 =D_g\mathcal Z_{\sigma,g}(s)k
\]
be the fixed-time variation of the actual physical branch.  Differentiating
the exact RFDE family at fixed time shows that \(X_k\) solves the homogeneous
full variational equation
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-full-variation}; there is no coefficient forcing
at first order.  On the generated hit window define
\begin{equation}
 a_k(s)=\frac{\omega_k(s)}{W'(r_s)},\qquad
 \psi_{k,s}=\xi_{k,s}-e_sa_k(s),\qquad
 \psi_{k,s}=(\Id-\mathcal K_s)u_{k,s}.             \label{eq:proof-hit-gauge-quotient-coordinates}
\end{equation}
Thus \(u_k\) solves the exact quotient RFDE used in
\cref{prop:physical-hit-quotient-flushing}.

The endpoints \(s_\pm=s_h\pm L_\delta\), together with their complete
histories, lie in the strong generated buffer.  The weak complete-history
bound \cref{eq:physical-hit-uniform-history-jets}, the fixed-annulus
bounds \(|W'|\asymp r_h\), \(\|e_s\|\le C\), and the inverse in
\cref{eq:resource-gauge-volterra-inverse} give
\begin{equation}
 \|u_{k,s_\pm}\|_{\sigma,h}^{\sharp}
 \le C_h\|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.     \label{eq:proof-hit-gauge-endpoint-bound}
\end{equation}
The attracting estimate uses only the minus sign; retaining both signs is
harmless.  Apply
\cref{eq:hit-attracting-interior-flush,%
eq:hit-repelling-interior-flush} and then the fading-to-ordinary comparison
to obtain
\begin{equation}
 \|u_{k,s_h}\|_{\infty,N}
 \le C\delta^{-2\vartheta}
       e^{-c\kappa_{\sigma,h}L_\delta}
       \|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.      \label{eq:proof-hit-gauge-center-bound}
\end{equation}

It remains to identify the complete event variation without losing the
resource component.  At the hit,
\(E_{s_h}^c=\tau_{\sigma,g}(s_h)\) exactly by
\cref{eq:exact-outer-history-tangent}.  Put
\[
 b_k=\pi_N^T\psi_{k,s_h}(0)=\pi_N^Tu_k(s_h);
\]
the second equality uses
\((\mathcal K_{s_h}u_{k,s_h})(0)=0\).
Since
\[
 X_k(s_h)=(\psi_{k,s_h},0)+a_k(s_h)E_{s_h}^c,
 \qquad
 \varrho_N E_{s_h}^c=1,
\]
the event derivative
\cref{eq:physical-hit-history-derivative} has the exact form
\begin{equation}
 D_g\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)k
 =\left(
   \psi_{k,s_h}-e_{s_h}b_k,\;
   -W'(e_{\sigma,h})b_k
   \right).                                      \label{eq:proof-hit-exact-event-bridge}
\end{equation}
In particular, using
\(\psi_{k,s_h}=(\Id-\mathcal K_{s_h})u_{k,s_h}\),
\cref{eq:proof-hit-resource-coordinate-bounds,%
eq:proof-hit-volterra-C0-bound}, and
\(|W'(e_{\sigma,h})|\asymp r_h\),
\begin{equation}
 \|D_g\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)k\|_{\mathrm{ev},h}
 \le C\varepsilon^{-1}\|u_{k,s_h}\|_{\infty,N}.   \label{eq:proof-hit-event-bridge-bound}
\end{equation}
The resource term in
\cref{eq:proof-hit-exact-event-bridge} is essential for this complete
history statement.

Because \(\varepsilon=\delta^2\),
\cref{eq:proof-hit-gauge-center-bound,%
eq:proof-hit-event-bridge-bound}
give
\begin{equation}
 \|D_g\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(g)\|
 \le C\delta^{-2-2\vartheta}
       \exp\{-cA S_\delta\log(1/\delta)\}.         \label{eq:proof-hit-flat-envelope}
\end{equation}
For every prescribed \(P>0\), the right-hand side is at most
\(C_P\delta^P\) after decreasing \(\delta_P\), because
\(S_\delta\to\infty\).  This proves
\cref{eq:physical-hit-gauge-flat-derivative}.  The common gauge ball is
convex, so integration of the derivative along the line segment from
\(g_1\) to \(g_2\) proves
\cref{eq:physical-hit-gauge-flat-pair}.  The definition and the stated
limitations of the flat class follow immediately.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{lem:fixed-reference-parameter-residual}]
Fix $\lambda^\circ$ and suppress it from the notation.  The domain current
paths now all live on the single interval $[0,S_\sigma^\circ]$ and its
fixed-delay enlargement.  We emphasize that this is a new chart of the raw
equations, not the map that translates a path from a parameter-dependent
interval.  In particular, if $T_kZ(s)=Z(s-\theta_k)$, then each $T_k$ is a
fixed bounded linear map on the finite-seam graph domain.

Use $\bar r_\sigma^\circ$ and $q_0^\circ$ in the nonlinear phase chart and
write $r_u=\Theta_a^\circ(\bar r_\sigma^\circ)$.  Substitute
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-physical-fields} and the actual
$(\nu,\eta,g)$-compatible old collar directly into
\cref{eq:raw-fold-time-fields}.  More explicitly, first form
$R_\gamma$ and $R_{\rm bulk}^{H}$ in
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-collar-mismatch} and then apply the triangular
row isomorphism \cref{eq:fixed-reference-row-isomorphism}.  Its displayed
inverse proves that vanishing is equivalent to
$R_\gamma=R_{\rm bulk}^{H}=0$.  At $\lambda=\lambda^\circ$, inserting
$\gamma_x=H+R_\gamma$ into the delayed voltage slots gives exactly
\begin{equation}
 R_{\rm bulk}^{\gamma}
 =R_{\rm bulk}^{H}
   +\mathcal J_{\lambda^\circ}^{\rm old}R_\gamma, \label{eq:proof-fixed-reference-row-identity}
\end{equation}
because the old history occurs linearly and only at the fixed delayed
arguments.  Thus the transformed rows are literally the old graph rows at
the reference parameter, not merely tangent to them.

Move the fixed-reference principal normal
block and phase row to the left exactly as in
\cref{eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-Z-source,%
eq:fixed-section-nonlinear-p-source,%
eq:raw-gauge-residual}; every coefficient difference, including the
change of the shared speed with $\nu$, remains on the right.  This defines
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-parameter-residual}.  Reversing these algebraic
row operations shows that its vanishing is equivalent to the two raw
fold-time equations on the fixed forward interval, the compatible initial
history, the seam, the selected finite boundary row, and the inner phase
row.  It does not extend that initial history backward.  Identity
\cref{eq:proof-fixed-reference-row-identity} shows that at
$\lambda=\lambda^\circ$ the construction is literally
\cref{eq:raw-gauge-residual}.
Moreover,
\[
 \partial_sr_u(s)
 =\delta q_0^\circ(\bar r_\sigma^\circ(s))
       (\Theta_a^\circ)'(\bar r_\sigma^\circ(s)).
\]
At a zero the left side is also $\delta$ times the reconstructed physical
collective speed in the raw equation.  Division by $\delta$ proves
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-speed-identity}.

We next verify the claimed Banach regularity.  No trial current path is
composed with a moving time map.  The only history operators acting on it
are the fixed $T_k$.  The layer law is affine in $\eta$, the current field
is polynomial, and the remaining coefficient maps are smooth Nemytskii
maps on a common same-sign compact collar.  Although
\cref{lem:inner-anchored-nonlinear-phase-chart} recorded only the first
derivative needed there, its fixed-reference formula
\[
