 \Theta_a^\circ(r)=\Phi_{q_0^\circ}^{a(r)}(r),
 \qquad
 \mathscr B_{\phi,\sigma}^{\rm nl,\circ}(a)
 =q_0^\circ(r)q_0^\circ(\Theta_a^\circ(r))a'(r)
\]
is four times differentiable from the retained $\mathsf A_\phi^1$ ball
to the same weak phase range: every derivative is a finite product with at
most one differentiated direction $h'$ and otherwise undifferentiated
directions.  The common coefficient bounds control all such products.

Likewise, differentiating the finite triangular compatibility recursion
in \cref{eq:collar-compatibility} at most four times gives a finite sum of
products of lower rows.  The core history enters linearly, all delayed
core evaluations are fixed, and multiplication of the level-$j$ row by
$\delta^j$ leaves the same uniform triangular induction as in
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-collar-jet-recursion,eq:mixed-data-majorants},
continued for the fixed four derivatives.  The normalized powers of
$\delta$ cancel at each triangular level, so the resulting four-derivative
constants are independent of $\delta$ as well as $N$ and the branch.
Thus the actual-parameter collar map is four times differentiable in the
normalized data, gauge, and parameter norms.  Combining these observations
with the fixed bounded row operations proves all derivatives in
\cref{eq:physical-gauge-staircase}, uniformly in $N$.

At the reference parameter the $u$-derivative is the invertible operator
in \cref{eq:raw-gauge-zero-inverse}.  Continuity in operator norm and one
Neumann argument, after decreasing the product ball, give
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-parameter-inverse}.  This argument occurs on the
fixed rough graph and hence invokes neither same-space differentiability of
translation nor a high-regularity inverse.  It completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{prop:parameter-coherent-physical-hit-family}]
Apply the parameter-dependent implicit-function theorem to the single
fixed-reference residual
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-parameter-residual}.  The inverse in
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-parameter-inverse} and the four-times
differentiable rough residual give one zero map on a smaller product box.
There is no moving graph-domain overlap to patch.  For completeness,
differentiate
\[
 \widehat{\mathcal F}_{\sigma,\lambda^\circ}
       (u(\nu,\eta,g);\nu,\eta,g)=0.
\]
The top derivative always has the form
\begin{equation}
 D_u\widehat{\mathcal F}_{\sigma,\lambda^\circ}(u)\,
     D_g^\ell\partial_\nu^iD_\eta^ju
 =-\mathfrak R_{\ell,i,j},                       \label{eq:proof-parameter-zero-induction}
\end{equation}
where \(\mathfrak R_{\ell,i,j}\) is a finite sum of lower zero jets and
derivatives of the fixed-reference residual.  Induction in
\(\ell+i+j\), followed by its common inverse bound, proves the asserted
dimension-uniform estimates.  The exact speed identity in
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-speed-identity} and the orientation margin of the
fixed-reference phase chart are uniform on the smaller product ball.  The
generated bounds for $V'$ and $W'$ then prove
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-tracker-signs}.

We next prove the triangular time ledger without propagating a new
Gr\"onwall estimate across $O(\delta^{-1})$ delay cells.  The rough IFT
already bounds every retained zero jet in the whole-branch $C^0$ graph
norm.  Normalize the current variables by
\[
 \mathbf y=(r/r_h,Z/\varepsilon,p/(\varepsilon r_h)).
\]
Differentiate the exact fixed-delay RFDE first in the parameter and gauge
directions and then pointwise in $s$.  At joint order $(a,d)$, with
$a=\ell+i+j$, the highest time derivative occurs linearly on the left; the
right side contains only lower time derivatives, fixed delayed values of
the same parameter jet, and lower parameter jets.  Each time derivative
costs at most one factor $\delta^{-1}$.  The affine layer bound and the
common current-field coefficient bounds close induction in $a+d$.  The
order-$h$ compatibility recursion makes the one-sided derivatives agree at
every seam.  This proves
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-time-jet-ledger}; it is the higher-order version
of the pointwise argument in
\cref{eq:proof-strong-hit-step-induction}, not long-time smooth dependence
of a generic RFDE semiflow.  To upgrade the formal differentiated
identities to Fr\'{e}chet strong jets, insert parameter--gauge difference
quotients in the normalized RFDE.  The rough IFT gives convergence first
in the whole-branch $C^0$ graph norm; subtracting the equations and using
the same pointwise induction upgrades it successively to each retained
scaled time-derivative norm, uniformly on unit direction balls.

It remains to center the family at the physical event.  The hit equation
has derivative
\[
 \partial_s r_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}(S_{\sigma,h})
 =\delta q_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}(e_{\sigma,h}),
\]
whose scaled coefficient is bounded away from zero by
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-tracker-signs}.  The scalar implicit-function
theorem therefore gives the same mixed jets for the scaled hit displacement
$\widehat\tau=\delta(S-S^0)$.  The unscaled time can lose one factor
\(\delta^{-1}\).

We reprove the margin in the fixed-duration chart.  The inner anchors in
\cref{eq:fixed-reference-inner-endpoints}, the free outer endpoint's
phase-chart closeness to $\pm r_{\rm out}$, and
$\rho_\delta<r_{\rm out}/4$ give
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-endpoint-gap} after shrinking the product
ball.  Since
$c_q\le-q_{\sigma,\nu,\eta,g}\le C_q$, integration of
$r'=\delta q$ from either endpoint to the unique intermediate hit gives
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-hit-margin}, after decreasing the constant to
pay for the fixed left delay collar.  Because
$L_\delta=AS_\delta=o(\delta^{-1})$, the event-relative window
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-event-window} lies in the common generated
buffer.  Apply the finite-order chain rule to
$Y(S(\nu,\eta,g)+\varsigma)$.  A term with $a$ explicit parameter/gauge
derivatives on $Y$ and a partition into $m$ hit-time blocks satisfies
$m\le b-a$, while the sum of the block sizes is $b-a$.  In the scaled
$C^3$ target it uses time derivatives only through order $3+m$, and
\[
 3+m\le3+b-a\le h-a
\]
because $b\le4$ and $h\ge8$.  Each hit-time block contributes at most
$\delta^{-1}$ through $D^B S=\delta^{-1}D^B\widehat\tau$, and the scaled
time derivative contributes another $\delta^{-1}$ per block.  The total
loss is therefore $\delta^{-2m}\le\delta^{-2b}$.  This proves
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-event-buffer-jets}.  Restriction to the last
delay interval and the reconstruction
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-physical-reconstruction} give
\cref{eq:parameter-coherent-hit-map}.  This completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of
\cref{thm:physical-hit-rectangular-flatness}]
We differentiate the finite-window quotient construction rather than infer
jet smallness from \cref{eq:physical-hit-gauge-flat-pair}.  Fix one
distinguished gauge direction $k$.  For a base point
\(\zeta=(\nu,\eta,g)\), let $u_k(\zeta)$ be the ordinary-history quotient
in \cref{eq:proof-hit-gauge-quotient-coordinates}.  It is the boundary-to-hit
solution of the exact standard quotient RFDE.

Recenter before differentiating:
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde u_k^\zeta(\varsigma)
 =u_k^\zeta(s_h(\zeta)+\varsigma).               \label{eq:proof-centered-gauge-quotient}
\end{equation}
For the attracting branch use $-L_\delta-\theta_m\le\varsigma\le0$
and the fixed weight
$d_a(\varsigma)=\varsigma+L_\delta$ on $[-L_\delta,0]$, extended by zero
on the old collar.  For the repelling branch use
$-L_\delta-\theta_m\le\varsigma\le L_\delta$ and
$d_r(\varsigma)=\min\{\varsigma+L_\delta,
L_\delta-\varsigma\}$, again extended by zero on the old collar.  These
define fixed weighted current spaces by
\begin{equation}
 \|y\|_{\mathcal W_\sigma}
 =\sup_{\varsigma}
    e^{\widehat\kappa_\sigma d_\sigma(\varsigma)}
       \|y(\varsigma)\|_N,
 \qquad
 \widehat\kappa_\sigma=c_1\kappa_{\sigma,h},     \label{eq:proof-centered-W-norm}
\end{equation}
where the fixed $c_1>0$ is the sufficiently small constant used in the
proof of \cref{prop:physical-hit-quotient-flushing}.  Neither the domains
nor the weights depend on $\zeta$.

The matrices $J_h=\mathcal J_N^{\rm fv}(e_{\sigma,h})$ and
$P_h^{s,u}$ in \cref{eq:proof-hit-current-green-bounds} are fixed-section
objects.  They do not depend on $\nu,\eta$, or $g$: the reduced layers
enter only the delayed remainder.  Hence their current Green kernel
$G_h$ is fixed and no spectral projection is differentiated.  Splitting
the stable left boundary, the one-dimensional unstable right boundary,
and the prescribed old collar exactly as in
\cref{eq:proof-hit-mixed-current-green}, the centered equation is
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde u_k^\zeta
 =\mathcal A_\zeta k
       +\mathcal T_\zeta\widetilde u_k^\zeta.    \label{eq:proof-differentiated-hit-LP}
\end{equation}
We now define both operators in that identity.  Split the exact remainder
in \cref{eq:proof-hit-quotient-remainder} after recentering as
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr R_\zeta(\varsigma)y_\varsigma
 =\mathscr R_\zeta^{\rm cur}(\varsigma)y
       +f_{\zeta,k}^{\rm old}(\varsigma).         \label{eq:proof-centered-remainder-split}
\end{equation}
In the first term every argument before $-L_\delta$ is set to zero.  In the
second, the current part is set to zero and the prescribed quotient collar
\begin{equation}
 \gamma_{\zeta,k}(\varsigma)
 =\widetilde u_k^\zeta(\varsigma),
 \qquad -L_\delta-\theta_m\le\varsigma\le-L_\delta,
                                                               \label{eq:proof-centered-old-collar}
\end{equation}
is inserted.  This split includes the portions of every delayed and
Volterra evaluation that cross the left boundary.  In particular, its
current-interval forcing is supported in
$[-L_\delta,-L_\delta+\theta_m]$.

Put
\begin{align*}
 b_{-,\zeta,k}&=\widetilde u_k^\zeta(-L_\delta),
 &&\sigma=a,\\
 b_{s,\zeta,k}&=P_h^s\widetilde u_k^\zeta(-L_\delta),
 &b_{u,\zeta,k}&=P_h^u\widetilde u_k^\zeta(L_\delta),
 &&\sigma=r.
\end{align*}
On the current intervals the operators in
\cref{eq:proof-differentiated-hit-LP} are exactly
\begin{align}
 (\mathcal T_{a,\zeta}y)(\varsigma)
 &=\int_{-L_\delta}^{\varsigma}
   e^{J_h(\varsigma-t)/\delta}
   \mathscr R_\zeta^{\rm cur}(t)y\,\dd t,\notag\\
 (\mathcal A_{a,\zeta}k)(\varsigma)
 &=e^{J_h(\varsigma+L_\delta)/\delta}b_{-,\zeta,k}
   +\int_{-L_\delta}^{\varsigma}
      e^{J_h(\varsigma-t)/\delta}
          f_{\zeta,k}^{\rm old}(t)\,\dd t,       \label{eq:proof-centered-attracting-operators}\\
 (\mathcal T_{r,\zeta}y)(\varsigma)
 &=\int_{-L_\delta}^{L_\delta}
      G_h(\varsigma,t)
      \mathscr R_\zeta^{\rm cur}(t)y\,\dd t,\notag\\
 (\mathcal A_{r,\zeta}k)(\varsigma)
 &=e^{J_h(\varsigma+L_\delta)/\delta}b_{s,\zeta,k}
   +e^{J_h(\varsigma-L_\delta)/\delta}b_{u,\zeta,k}\notag\\
 &\quad+\int_{-L_\delta}^{L_\delta}
      G_h(\varsigma,t)f_{\zeta,k}^{\rm old}(t)\,\dd t.
                                                               \label{eq:proof-centered-repelling-operators}
\end{align}
On the old collar set $\mathcal T_{\sigma,\zeta}y=0$ and
$\mathcal A_{\sigma,\zeta}k=\gamma_{\zeta,k}$.  These formulas display
the left current trace, the repelling right one-dimensional trace, and all
old-collar forcing separately; hence
\cref{eq:proof-differentiated-hit-LP} is the exact Green identity.

We record the operator ledger explicitly.  The proof of
\cref{eq:proof-hit-local-contraction}, now in the fixed centered variable,
gives
\begin{equation}
 \|\mathcal T_\zeta\|_{\mathcal L(\mathcal W_\sigma)}\le\frac12.
                                                               \label{eq:proof-centered-LP-margin}
\end{equation}
Let
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal K_{\rm base}
 =\{(\ell-1,i,j):(\ell,i,j)\in\mathcal K_{\rm phys},
                         \ \ell\ge1\}.            \label{eq:proof-centered-base-staircase}
\end{equation}
For every base derivative indexed by
$\alpha\in\mathcal K_{\rm base}$, with $b=|\alpha|\le3$,
\begin{align}
 \|D_\zeta^\alpha\mathcal T_\zeta\|_{
       \mathcal L(\mathcal W_\sigma)}
 &\le C_b\delta^{-M_b}e^{c_bS_\delta},\notag\\
 \|D_\zeta^\alpha(\mathcal A_\zeta k)\|_{\mathcal W_\sigma}
 &\le C_b\delta^{-M_b}e^{c_bS_\delta}
       \|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.  \label{eq:proof-centered-LP-jets}
\end{align}
Indeed, \cref{eq:parameter-coherent-time-jet-ledger} supplies the centered
tracker, old-collar, and two endpoint jets.  The current coefficient
$\mathcal J_*-J_h$, the ratios in $H_s$, and the Volterra kernel
$\mathcal K_s$ are finite products and fixed translations of those jets;
$|W'|\asymp r_h$ controls every reciprocal.  The layer row is affine in
$\eta$.  A fixed delay costs at most
$e^{\widehat\kappa_\sigma\theta_m}$ in
\cref{eq:proof-centered-W-norm}, whereas convolution with either fixed
Green kernel costs $C\delta$.  Applying these two facts to
\cref{eq:proof-centered-attracting-operators,%
eq:proof-centered-repelling-operators} gives exactly the two estimates in
\cref{eq:proof-centered-LP-jets}.  Thus every retained derivative is a
bounded insertion on the same fading space.  It need not be small; only the
zeroth-order operator in
\cref{eq:proof-centered-LP-margin} is inverted.

Differentiate \cref{eq:proof-differentiated-hit-LP}.  For
$\alpha\in\mathcal K_{\rm base}$, multivariate induction gives
\begin{equation}
 D_\zeta^\alpha\widetilde u_k
 =(\Id-\mathcal T_\zeta)^{-1}
 \left\{D_\zeta^\alpha(\mathcal A_\zeta k)
 +\sum_{0<\beta\le\alpha}c_{\beta,\alpha}
       (D_\zeta^\beta\mathcal T_\zeta)
       D_\zeta^{\alpha-\beta}\widetilde u_k\right\}. \label{eq:proof-centered-LP-induction}
\end{equation}
Equations
\cref{eq:proof-centered-LP-margin,eq:proof-centered-LP-jets} therefore
bound its $\mathcal W_\sigma$ norm by the same tame envelope, with
$b=|\alpha|$.  Every point
of the center history $[-\theta_m,0]$ has
$d_\sigma\ge L_\delta-\theta_m$.  Converting the fixed fading norm to the
ordinary history norm and absorbing the fixed delay shift gives, for
$b\le3$,
\begin{equation}
 \|D_\zeta^b\widetilde u_{k,0}\|_{\infty,N}
 \le C_b\delta^{-M_b}e^{c_bS_\delta}
       e^{-c\kappa_{\sigma,h}L_\delta}
       \|k\|_{\mathfrak B_{\sigma,\delta}^h}.    \label{eq:proof-differentiated-hit-center}
\end{equation}
This calculation never differentiates an RFDE generator.  On the
repelling branch the only future inverse is still the inverse on the fixed
one-dimensional current line.

The exact bridge \cref{eq:proof-hit-exact-event-bridge} is a bounded
operator $\mathcal B_\zeta^{\rm ev}$ applied to the center history:
\begin{equation}
 D_g\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(\zeta)k
 =\mathcal B_\zeta^{\rm ev}\widetilde u_{k,0},
 \quad
 \mathcal B_\zeta^{\rm ev}u
 =\left((\Id-\mathcal K_\zeta)u
          -e_\zeta\pi_N^Tu(0),
        -W_\zeta'(e_{\sigma,h})\pi_N^Tu(0)\right).
                                                               \label{eq:proof-centered-event-bridge}
\end{equation}
The same coefficient ledger gives tame operator jets of
$\mathcal B_\zeta^{\rm ev}$ through order three.  Leibniz' rule and
\cref{eq:proof-differentiated-hit-center} therefore yield
\begin{equation}
 \|D_\zeta^bD_g\mathcal E_{\sigma,h}(\zeta)\|_{\rm ev,h}
 \le C_b\delta^{-M_b}e^{c_bS_\delta}
       e^{-c\kappa_{\sigma,h}L_\delta},
 \qquad b\le3.                                  \label{eq:proof-differentiated-hit-event}
\end{equation}
Choose among the $b$ base directions exactly $\ell-1$ gauge directions
and $i+j$ parameter directions.  The index set
\cref{eq:physical-gauge-staircase} makes $b\le3$, so this proves every
slot in \cref{eq:physical-hit-flat-mixed-index}.

Finally,
\[
 \kappa_{\sigma,h}L_\delta
 \ge cA S_\delta\log(1/\delta).
\]
This dominates
$M_b\log(1/\delta)+c_bS_\delta$.
Hence \cref{eq:proof-differentiated-hit-event} is $O(\delta^P)$ for
every prescribed $P$, proving
\cref{eq:physical-hit-mixed-flatness}.

For the pairwise statement, apply the multivariate Fa\`a di Bruno formula
separately to
$\mathcal E(\lambda,g_a(\lambda))$, $a=1,2$, through each slot of
$\mathcal I_{\rm phys}$.  Every non-pure term contains
\begin{equation}
 D_g^m\partial_\lambda^\beta\mathcal E,
 \qquad 1\le m\le3,\qquad m+|\beta|\le3,        \label{eq:proof-composite-gauge-slots}
\end{equation}
and is superalgebraically small by
\cref{eq:physical-hit-mixed-flatness}; the remaining factors are tame jets
of $g_a$.  The only terms without a gauge derivative are the two pure
explicit-parameter derivatives.  At each fixed $\lambda$ compare those by
the convex-line identity
\begin{equation}
 (\partial_\lambda^\alpha\mathcal E)(\lambda,g_1(\lambda))
 -(\partial_\lambda^\alpha\mathcal E)(\lambda,g_2(\lambda))
 =\int_0^1D_g\partial_\lambda^\alpha
       \mathcal E(\lambda,g_t(\lambda))
       [g_1(\lambda)-g_2(\lambda)]\,\dd t,        \label{eq:proof-rectangular-gauge-integration}
\end{equation}
where $g_t=g_2+t(g_1-g_2)$ and
$\alpha\in\mathcal I_{\rm phys}$.  These are precisely the one-gauge
slots retained in \cref{eq:physical-gauge-staircase}.  The constants,
uniform in $N$, $\delta$, and the branch, in
\cref{eq:physical-hit-tame-gauge-fields}, including any
$e^{cS_\delta}$ loss, are absorbed by the same superalgebraic envelope.
This direct endpoint expansion uses $D_g^3\mathcal E$ but never
$D_g^4\mathcal E$.  It proves
\cref{eq:physical-hit-rectangular-pair-flatness}.  The scope statements
follow because the argument never relates the attracting and repelling
sections.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Why unnormalized backward convergence does not select a history}

The next elementary RFDE example makes precise why an outer-history theorem
needs a normalized boundary coordinate.  It rules out a logical inference;
it remains compatible with a separately imposed global anchor for the
particular network field of the main article.

\begin{proposition}[Unnormalized backward convergence and loss of jet control]
\label[proposition]{prop:backward-asymptotic-nonselection}
Fix \(0<\delta_*<1\), let
\(\tau:(0,\delta_*)\to(0,\infty)\) be arbitrary, and write
\(\tau_\delta=\tau(\delta)\).  On \(\R^2\) use
\(|(y,z)|_1=|y|+|z|\), and put
\begin{equation*}
 X_\delta=C([-\tau_\delta,0];\R^2),\qquad
 X^1_\delta=C^1([-\tau_\delta,0];\R^2),
\end{equation*}
with
\begin{equation*}
 \|\phi\|_{X_\delta}=\sup_\vartheta|\phi(\vartheta)|_1,
 \qquad
 \|\phi\|_{X^1_\delta}=\|\phi\|_{X_\delta}
       +\sup_\vartheta|\phi'(\vartheta)|_1.
\end{equation*}
Consider the RFDE
\begin{equation}
 \dot x(t)=\mathcal F_\delta(x_t),
 \qquad \mathcal F_\delta(\phi)=\delta^{-2}\phi(0),           \label{eq:nonselection-rfde}
\end{equation}
whose compatible \(C^1\) histories satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal M^1_\delta
 =\{\phi\in X^1_\delta:\phi'(0)=\delta^{-2}\phi(0)\}.        \label{eq:nonselection-manifold}
\end{equation}

Even after fixing the common phase row
\begin{equation}
 \phi_y(0)=1,                                        \label{eq:nonselection-phase-row}
\end{equation}
bounded complete backward extension and convergence to zero in the
unnormalized history norm do not select a history.  Indeed, for every
\(A\in\R\),
\begin{equation}
 x_{\delta,A}(t)=e^{t/\delta^2}(1,A),\qquad t\le0,   \label{eq:nonselection-family}
\end{equation}
solves \cref{eq:nonselection-rfde} and satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{t\le0}\|x_{\delta,A,t}\|_{X_\delta}<\infty,
 \qquad
 \|x_{\delta,A,t}\|_{X_\delta}\longrightarrow0
       \quad(t\to-\infty).                          \label{eq:nonselection-asymptotics}
\end{equation}

Even superalgebraic closeness in \(X^1_\delta\), together with smooth
parameter dependence for each fixed \(\delta\), does not imply a tame
\(C^1_\nu C^2_\eta\) history jet.  Fix \(0<a<b\), put
\(\omega_\delta=e^{b/\delta^2}\), and, on
\(K=[-1/2,1/2]^2\), define
\begin{align}
 A_\delta(\nu,\eta)
 &=e^{-a/\delta^2}(1+\nu)
   \{\sin(\omega_\delta\eta)+1-\cos(\omega_\delta\eta)\},\notag\\
 \Phi_\delta(\nu,\eta)(\vartheta)
 &=e^{\vartheta/\delta^2}
       (1,A_\delta(\nu,\eta)),
 \qquad -\tau_\delta\le\vartheta\le0.             \label{eq:nonselection-jet-family}
\end{align}
The formula extends to a \(C^\infty\) map from \(\R^2\) to the closed
linear space \(\mathcal M^1_\delta\); restrict it to \(K\).  Every member
obeys \cref{eq:nonselection-phase-row}, extends to a solution satisfying
\cref{eq:nonselection-asymptotics}, and, relative to
\(\Phi_\delta^{\rm ref}(\vartheta)=e^{\vartheta/\delta^2}(1,0)\),
\begin{equation}
 \sup_K\|\Phi_\delta-\Phi_\delta^{\rm ref}\|_{X^1_\delta}
 \le\frac92(1+\delta^{-2})e^{-a/\delta^2}
 =O(\delta^q)\quad\text{for every }q>0.              \label{eq:nonselection-flat-size}
\end{equation}
At \((\nu,\eta)=(0,0)\), however,
\begin{align}
 \|\partial_\eta\Phi_\delta\|_{X_\delta}
 &=e^{(b-a)/\delta^2},&
 \|\partial_{\eta\eta}\Phi_\delta\|_{X_\delta}
 &=e^{(2b-a)/\delta^2},\notag\\
 \|\partial_\nu\partial_\eta\Phi_\delta\|_{X_\delta}
 &=e^{(b-a)/\delta^2},&
 \|\partial_\nu\partial_{\eta\eta}\Phi_\delta\|_{X_\delta}
 &=e^{(2b-a)/\delta^2}.                              \label{eq:nonselection-jet-blowup}
\end{align}
In particular, for arbitrary fixed \(C>0\), \(M,m,c\ge0\), and \(p>0\),
the ratio of each of the four displayed derivative norms to the envelope
\begin{equation}
 C\delta^{-M}\langle S_\delta\rangle^m e^{cS_\delta},
 \qquad S_\delta=\sqrt{2p\log(1/\delta)},            \label{eq:nonselection-tame-envelope}
\end{equation}
tends to \(+\infty\) as \(\delta\downarrow0\).  Since
\(\|\cdot\|_{X_\delta}\le\|\cdot\|_{X^1_\delta}\), the same conclusion
holds a fortiori for the strong-history derivative norms.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The functional \(\mathcal F_\delta\) is bounded linear on \(X_\delta\),
so \cref{eq:nonselection-rfde} is an RFDE, in fact in the nondelayed
subclass.  Direct differentiation proves \cref{eq:nonselection-family}.
Its history segment is
\begin{equation*}
 x_{\delta,A,t}(\vartheta)=e^{(t+\vartheta)/\delta^2}(1,A),
\end{equation*}
and hence
\begin{equation*}
 \|x_{\delta,A,t}\|_{X_\delta}=(1+|A|)e^{t/\delta^2},
 \qquad
 \|x_{\delta,A,t}\|_{X^1_\delta}
 =(1+\delta^{-2})(1+|A|)e^{t/\delta^2}.
\end{equation*}
This proves \cref{eq:nonselection-asymptotics}; the distinguished histories
\(x_{\delta,A,0}\) all satisfy the same phase row, while present evaluation
of the second component recovers the free coefficient \(A\).  Moreover,
\(\partial_\vartheta\phi(0)=\delta^{-2}\phi(0)\), so these histories and
the family below lie in \(\mathcal M^1_\delta\), which is the kernel of a
bounded linear map on \(X^1_\delta\) and hence is closed.

For the second component of \cref{eq:nonselection-jet-family}, relative to
the reference history, use
\(|\sin u|\le1\), \(|1-\cos u|\le2\), and
\(1+\nu\le3/2\).  This gives \cref{eq:nonselection-flat-size}, since
\(e^{-a/\delta^2}\) dominates every power of \(\delta\).  At \(\eta=0\),
direct differentiation of \(A_\delta\) gives coefficients
\begin{equation*}
 e^{(b-a)/\delta^2},\quad e^{(2b-a)/\delta^2},\quad
 e^{(b-a)/\delta^2},\quad e^{(2b-a)/\delta^2}
\end{equation*}
in the four derivative slots of
\cref{eq:nonselection-jet-blowup}.  The exponential profile has
\(X_\delta\)-norm one, proving the equalities.  Finally, the logarithm of
the ratio of the first quantity to
\cref{eq:nonselection-tame-envelope} is
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{b-a}{\delta^2}-M\log(1/\delta)
 -m\log\langle S_\delta\rangle-cS_\delta-\log C,
\end{equation*}
which tends to \(+\infty\).  The two derivative norms with exponent
\((2b-a)/\delta^2\) diverge still faster, proving all four assertions.
\end{proof}

Thus the words ``complete backward'', ``bounded'', and ``convergent in an
unnormalized history norm'', even with the displayed phase row, do not by
themselves select a complete history.  Nor may tame parameter jets be
inferred from fixed-\(\delta\) smoothness and superalgebraic \(C^0\) or
\(C^1\) closeness.  A valid construction must add a model-specific selection
or normalization and prove its uniform jets.
